Getting Started Getting Started Save Page to .PDF EXAMPLE Author ® defines all features of an insurance product: rates, rules, algorithms, documentation, screen flow, and policy transactions. In EXAMPLE Author ® , you create, edit, and test ManuScripts, XML files used to describe products, and define how those products process the rules and values of data. The EXAMPLE Author ® User Interface The EXAMPLE Author ® application is divided into several main views: Fields – Defines fields, groups, and their relationships. Tables – Defines data tables. Page Designer – Creates and edits input screens. Data Tester – Tests the execution of the ManuScript and creates and edits data documents associated with the active ManuScript. Forms Mapper – Creates and edits forms. Logging in to EXAMPLE Author ® You use the Login dialog to identify yourself as the user and to select an EXAMPLE Server ® connection. To login to EXAMPLE Author ® : On the Windows Start menu, in the All Programs list, point to Duck Creek Technologies , point to Author , and then click Author . The Login dialog box appears. If you are already in EXAMPLE Author ® and wish to change your server or user name, on the File menu, click Login User/Server . Enter your user name. In the Server list, click the appropriate EXAMPLE Server ® , or click (work locally) to work in standalone mode. Enter your password. Click OK . The Open/New ManuScript dialog box appears. Working in Enterprise Mode You use Enterprise Mode when you work on a ManuScript stored on an EXAMPLE Server ® . You can also work in Enterprise Mode locally by setting up your local installation as an EXAMPLE Server ® . If you log in to an EXAMPLE Server ® , you are automatically placed in Enterprise Mode. When you work in Enterprise Mode: You are prompted to confirm any change you make. If revision tracking has been enabled, you must enter a comment about your changes. Setting Directories Options Click the Directories tab in the Options dialog box to define where the Catalog and schema files reside for local usage of EXAMPLE Author ® . The directories, except the EnterpriseFormsRootPath, are pre-set during installation. They can be modified for custom use. You can modify the directory file locations for the following files: Catalog — Location of ManuScript and data files Catalogs and directories. Debug — Location of the .tsv debug folder. Forms — Location of forms for the preview (Enterprise mode). This is the initial search point for forms. The path should be where the form files are created by default. Preview — .Xsl files that define the preview. Schema — Location of data schemas. You can click Restore Default Catalogs to return the settings to the defaults. Login Dialog Box You use the Login dialog to identify yourself as the user and to select an EXAMPLE Server ® connection. You can also work locally with single user access to a local instance of the EXAMPLE Server ® components on the local machine. Item Action Enter user name Enter your user name for the appropriate server. Server Click the appropriate server in the list. You maintain the list of available servers on the Servers tab of the Options dialog box within EXAMPLE Author ® . Enter password Enter the password for your entered user name. EXAMPLE Author ® Toolbar The EXAMPLE Author ® toolbar contains the following buttons. Button Name Action New ManuScript (CTRL+N) Opens the Open/New ManuScript dialog box to create a new ManuScript. Open ManuScript (CTRL+O) Opens the ManuScript Catalog dialog box to open an existing ManuScript. Save ManuScript (CTRL+S) Saves an existing ManuScript to its current location. Print (CTRL+P) Opens the Report/Print Options dialog box to print an existing or new report. Validate Opens the Validator pane. Fields View Opens the Fields view. Tables View Opens the Tables view. Data Tester Opens the Data Tester. Page Designer Opens the Page Designer. Forms Mapper Opens the Forms Mapper. Automatically Update Changes Saves all changes as they are made, rather than asking to confirm each. (Local mode only.) Additional toolbar buttons are available within the different views. The EXAMPLE Author ® toolbar also contains the Versions list. This list displays all versions of the active ManuScript and all ManuScripts it inherits. Clicking a ManuScript in the list opens that ManuScript in EXAMPLE Author ® for editing. Keyboard Shortcuts Use the following keyboard shortcuts to control EXAMPLE Author ® . Key Combination Action F1 Opens the Help Contents dialog box. F3 Finds the next occurrence of the previously entered text. F5 Refreshes the view. F7 Opens the Search Viewer. Double-click the name of a field, table, or other item to go to the item. F8 Opens the Constant Viewer dialog box. Double-click the name of a field, table, or other item to go to the item. CTRL+C Copies the selected text to the clipboard. CTRL+F Opens the Search dialog box to enter text to find. CTRL+N Opens the Open/New ManuScript dialog box to create a new ManuScript. CTRL+O Opens the ManuScript Catalog dialog box to open an existing ManuScript. CTRL+P Opens the Report/Print Options dialog box to print an existing or new report. CTRL+S Saves an existing ManuScript to its current location. CTRL+U Opens the Usage dialog box to list the items that use the selected field. To go to a field, click Go to . CTRL+V Pastes the text from the clipboard. CTRL+X Cuts the selected text to the clipboard. CTRL+Z Undo the last action performed. CTRL+TAB Switches between views. CTRL+ALT+D Moves the selected item down in the list. CTRL+ALT+L Moves the selected item left in the list. (Promote) CTRL+ALT+R Moves the selected item right in the list. (Demote) CTRL+ALT+U Moves the selected item up in the list. SHIFT+ENTER Inserts a line break. Additional keyboard shortcuts apply to specific views and the associated menus. Reference Select You use the Reference Select dialog box in many instances in EXAMPLE Author ® to define field references or constant values. The Ellipsis button generally opens the Reference Select dialog. To define a constant value: In the Reference Select dialog, click Value . In the Value text box, enter the appropriate value. Click OK . You may also use the Code Select dialog box to choose a value defined in the option list of a field. For more information, see Defining a Constant Value Using Selected Codes . To define a field reference: In the Reference Select dialog, click Field Reference . In the list of fields, click the appropriate field. Click OK . The Reference Select toolbar contains the following buttons and check boxes. Button Name Action Add Field (CTRL+A) Opens the New Field dialog box to create a new field or group. Delete Field Deletes the selected field or group. Refresh List Refreshes the fields list. Find (CTRL+F) Opens the Search dialog box to enter text to find. Find Next (F3) Finds the next occurrence of the previously entered text. Duplicate Opens the Duplicate Item dialog box to duplicate the selected field or group. Back (CTRL+B) Returns to last selected field or group. Public Select this check box to display public fields and groups. Private Select this check box to display private fields and groups. Local Only Select this check box to display only those fields and groups defined in the local ManuScript, hiding inherited items. Worksheet Select this check box to display only fields included in the rating worksheet. Searching for Items You can find items in lists within the Fields, Data Tester, and Forms Mapper views. To find an item: Click the Find button . The Search dialog box appears. You can also: • Press CTRL+F. • On the Edit menu, click Find . Enter the text to find. Click OK . If there is more than one instance of the text, click the Find Next button to find the next instance. You can also: • Press F3. • On the Edit menu, click Find Next . Inserting Line Breaks You can insert line breaks in text fields in EXAMPLE Author ® . To insert a line break: Press SHIFT+ENTER. Security in EXAMPLE Author ® Security in EXAMPLE Author ® relies on the roles and privileges assigned through EXAMPLE UserAdmin™ to restrict user access. EXAMPLE Author ® is shipped with the System Administrator role assigned, which has no restrictions. For more information about the security provided through EXAMPLE UserAdmin™, see the EXAMPLE UserAdmin™ Overview or the EXAMPLE UserAdmin™ User Guide .

Best Practices for ManuScript Development Best Practices for ManuScript Development Save Page to .PDF The following are recommended best practices for ManuScript development: Leave the default of data for the root group in a new ManuScript. Save the ManuScript often. There is no autosave feature in EXAMPLE Author ® . Add the following three private groups under every public group: GroupName Input, GroupName Output, and GroupName Private (AccountInput, AccountOutput, and AccountPrivate, for example). Always select the Prepend group name to field check box when creating a field. Before you modify a field, check where that field is used to determine how the change might affect previously coded fields. Note that the Used by function does not display the use of a field in external ManuScripts. There are many places where you can either enter a value manually or let the program look it up for you. To ensure that the value is entered correctly, do not manually enter field names. To avoid errors, always select fields using the Ellipsis button rather than manually entering field names. Many tables have a large number of entries. Avoid entering large amounts of table data if there is already an electronic list of the items. Instead, copy and paste from the electronic version of the list. Assign all fields when mapping to the EXAMPLEData Database. Do not change the versionID of a versioned ManuScript. The versionID links all the versions of a ManuScript together and should never be modified for versioned ManuScripts. For more information about best practices for ManuScript development, contact your Professional Services representative or Account Manager.

ManuScripts ManuScripts Save Section to .PDF Save Page to .PDF ManuScripts are XML files that contain fields, groups, tables, forms, and Web page definitions that fully detail all aspects of an insurance product. A ManuScript: Defines the content, rules, values, and processes needed to manage data by populating or validating a data document containing the information needed by a business process. Enables you to document each product by giving you a powerful engine to collect and process data. ManuScripts contain information to describe the data document and to create product documentation. Provides the definition information needed to generate data entry screens to collect data from an end user. The ManuScript Catalog The ManuScript Catalog Save Page to .PDF The ManuScript Catalog is an XML file that contains pointers and other attributes that specify the system location of ManuScript files. These files may be: Created directly Linked into the Catalog Imported into the Catalog To open the ManuScript Catalog: Click the Open ManuScript button . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. You can also: • On the File menu, click Open . • In the Open/New ManuScript dialog box, click Open Existing . From this point, you can perform a variety of tasks including: Opening a ManuScript Linking a ManuScript into the Catalog Importing a ManuScript into the Catalog Exporting a ManuScript Removing a ManuScript from the Catalog Catalog Buttons and Keyboard Shortcuts Use the following keyboard shortcuts and buttons to control a Manuscript Catalog: Button Name Action Link Links a file to this Catalog item. Import Imports a file into this Catalog. Export Exports a copy of selected item to file. Remove Removes selected item from Catalog. Properties View properties of the selected item. Dependencies View dependencies for selected item. (Available only in the ManuScript Catalog) New Version Makes a new version of the selected item. (Available only in the ManuScript Catalog) Duplicate Saves as (duplicates) the selected item. This item is not linked to the original item. Columns Switches between showing all columns and showing default columns. Catalog Keys The default and required Catalog keys are lob (line of business) and state . For organizational purposes, you can further subdivide ManuScripts or data files by adding additional keys. You do this in the Catalog dialog box. You can also remove keys you have added. Adding Catalog Keys Catalog keys help to organize your files. The default keys are lob (Line of Business) and state . To add a catalog key: In the Catalog dialog box, click Add Key . The New Key Name dialog box appears. Enter the new key name, and then click OK . Deleting Catalog Keys The default keys, lob (line of business) and state cannot be removed, but additional keys you have created can be deleted. To delete a Catalog key: In the Catalog dialog box, click the key to delete. Click Delete Key . The Confirm dialog box appears. Click Yes to delete the key, or No to cancel. Filtering the Catalog Keys are useful for filtering a Catalog to display only applicable items. To filter a Catalog: For each Key Name , click the appropriate Restriction in the dropdown list. The list of items now shows only those files that match the key value you chose. You can filter by any combination of key values. Opening and Closing ManuScripts To open a ManuScript: Click the Open ManuScript button . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. You can also: • On the File menu, click Open . • In the Open/New ManuScript dialog box, click Open Existing . Click a ManuScript in the list, and then click Load . The selected ManuScript opens. To close a ManuScript and the EXAMPLE Author ® program: On the File menu, click Exit . You can also: Click the Close button in the upper-right corner of the window. If you have not saved the ManuScript since making a change, EXAMPLE Author ® will prompt you to save the changes. Creating New ManuScripts To create a new ManuScript: Click the New ManuScript button . The Open/New ManuScript dialog box appears. You can also: On the File menu, click New . Click Blank ManuScript . The Add New Group dialog box appears. Enter a name for the root group of the new ManuScript, and then click OK . It is best practice to use the name data for the root group of any ManuScript. This group must be public. Saving a ManuScript for the First Time When saving a new ManuScript for the first time, you must first enter a Description and key Values. To save a ManuScript: Click the Save ManuScript button . The ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box appears. You can also: On the File menu, click Save . Enter a Description . For each Key Name , click an appropriate Value . You may add Values to Key Names by clicking Add on the appropriate line. Click OK . If you have previously saved a ManuScript, saving again will not open the ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box. Linking a ManuScript A link to a ManuScript or data file is similar to a shortcut button on your desktop. Selecting that link opens the ManuScript or data file from its original location. To have a copy of the ManuScript or Data File reside in the ManuScript or Data File Catalog directory, you import the item. To link a ManuScript or data file into the Catalog: In the ManuScript Catalog dialog box, click the Link button . The Link ManuScript dialog box appears. Browse to the appropriate directory, and then click the ManuScript. Click Open . (Optional) Change the Description as appropriate. (Optional) Change the key Values as appropriate. Click OK . The lob and state keys are used to create a directory structure in the Catalog location. The ManuScript or data file is stored in the state folder which is stored within the lob folder in the path set up for the Catalog. The keys used in the ManuScript or data file remain the default when you link the item into the Catalog. Removing ManuScripts Removing a ManuScript from the Catalog has one of two results. If the ManuScript is: Linked into the Catalog, you remove only the link to the ManuScript. An imported ManuScript, or one that was created in the Catalog structure, you delete the ManuScript from the Catalog and delete the file from the folder on the computer. To determine if the ManuScript is linked or imported, check the Source by clicking Advanced in the ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box. If the ManuScript is not stored in the Catalog path, it is a linked ManuScript. To remove a ManuScript: In the ManuScript Catalog dialog box, click the ManuScript you wish to remove. Click the Remove button . The Confirm dialog box appears. Click Yes to remove the ManuScript or No to cancel. Duplicating ManuScripts You can copy a ManuScript without linking the copy to the original ManuScript. To duplicate a ManuScript: In the ManuScript Catalog dialog box, click the appropriate ManuScript. Click the Duplicate button . The ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box appears. (Optional) Change the Description for the duplicated ManuScript. (Optional) Change the key Values as appropriate. Click OK . Changing ManuScript Details The ManuScript details are stored in the ManuScript XML. The Description appears on the title bar of the EXAMPLE Author ® application, and the key Values determine where the ManuScript is saved. To change the ManuScript Description: In the ManuScript Catalog dialog box, click the ManuScript. Click the Properties button . The ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box appears. Enter an appropriate Description. The description may contain spaces and any alphanumeric characters. Click OK . To change ManuScript key Values: In the ManuScript Catalog dialog box, click the ManuScript. Click the Properties button . The ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box appears. For each Key Name , click an appropriate Value . You can add key Values by clicking Add on the appropriate line. Creating a New ManuScript Version You can create a new version of a ManuScript with the same content. The new ManuScript has the same ManuScript ID as the item on which it was based, but has a new Version ID and file name. The internal version number of the ManuScript is incremented. The Catalog opens a Date Selector dialog box to allow a new date to be set for the version. By default, the new entry retains the same description and keys. When there are multiple versions of a ManuScript, EXAMPLE Author ® facilitates easy transition from version to version while editing. When a ManuScript is loaded into EXAMPLE Author ® , all the entries in the Catalog that have the same ManuScript ID as the loaded ManuScript are listed in the Versions list at the top of the main screen. Selecting any one of these entries from the Versions list automatically loads the selected version of the ManuScript. To create a new version from the ManuScript Catalog: In the ManuScript Catalog dialog box, click the appropriate ManuScript. Click the New Version button . The Date Selector dialog box appears. Click the appropriate date. This is the date the new version of the ManuScript becomes active. Click OK . You can view the new version number and date in the ManuScript Properties or ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog boxes. Exporting ManuScripts You export a ManuScript to copy the file from the Catalog directory to another directory of your choice. To export a ManuScript: In the ManuScript Catalog dialog box, click the ManuScript. Click the Export button . The Export ManuScript dialog box appears. Click the appropriate directory. In the File name text box, enter a name for the file. Click OK . Viewing ManuScript Sources In the ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box, you can see where a ManuScript is stored. To view the file source: In the ManuScript Catalog dialog box, click the file. Click the Properties button . The ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box appears. It includes the description of the file and the keys assigned to it. Click Advanced . The dialog box expands to show additional information. The directory of the selected ManuScript is listed in the Source text box. Viewing Dependencies When a ManuScript is derived from another ManuScript, it is dependent upon the base ManuScript. To see how various ManuScripts are related in terms of inheritance, you can view the dependencies a ManuScript has to others. To view dependencies: In the ManuScript Catalog dialog box, click the appropriate ManuScript, and then click the Dependencies button . The ManuScript Dependencies dialog box appears. This dialog box shows the ManuScripts that the selected ManuScript inherits in the Used With list and the ManuScripts that inherit the selected ManuScript in the Used By list.

Inheriting ManuScripts Inheriting ManuScripts Save Page to .PDF To reduce costly ManuScript re-work for similar requirements, Duck Creek Technologies has developed the Inheritance Model. With the Inheritance Model, you can reuse the content of any ManuScript and extend it in subsequent ManuScripts. When a ManuScript is inherited from a base, the resulting ManuScript is called derived . Benefits of Inheritance There are many benefits to using inheritance in the EXAMPLE Platform ® , but the primary benefit is reusability. You create a base ManuScript and then reuse it in subsequent ManuScripts, making only the necessary changes. This also means that when you change the base ManuScript, the changes are automatically updated in ManuScripts that have inherited the base, as long as the updated fields have not been modified in the derived ManuScript. Issues with Inheritance Although inheritance brings great benefits to the EXAMPLE Platform ® , there are still design considerations and maintenance issues to keep in mind. Any inherited items that have been overridden in the derived ManuScript are not changed when you update the base ManuScript. You must change these items manually. First identify the items that must be changed. Then demote any overridden items in the derived ManuScript to bring in the new base definitions. Finally, modify these items again in the local ManuScript to reflect the changes that were made to the derived ManuScript. When you override an item that is used in calculations in the base ManuScript, the change you make may cause the calculations in the derived ManuScript to function incorrectly or not at all. When you edit the base ManuScript and move or rename a field, fields in a derived ManuScript still search the original paths. If you override the renamed or moved item in the derived ManuScript, the override link is broken. The ManuScript validation process identifies these unlinked override items, but carefully consider renaming or moving items in a base ManuScript. XML for an Inherited ManuScript No inherited elements actually exist in the ManuScript XML of an inherited ManuScript. There is instead a pointer in the XML to the inherited ManuScript. For more information on inheritance, see Inheritance . Creating Inherited ManuScripts To create an inherited ManuScript: Click the New ManuScript button . You can also: On the File menu, click New . Click Inheritance Wizard . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. Click the base ManuScript, and then click Load . The new ManuScript, derived from the base ManuScript, appears. Inheriting into an Existing ManuScript To inherit a ManuScript, the base ManuScript must be in the Catalog. The base ManuScript can be a linked ManuScript. To inherit into an existing ManuScript: Open the ManuScript to be derived from the base ManuScript. On the File menu, click ManuScript Properties . On the Inheritance tab, click the Ellipsis button to the right of Inherited ID . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. Click the ManuScript to inherit. Click Load . In the ManuScript Properties dialog box, click OK . Any given ManuScript can inherit up to three other ManuScripts, one each for fields, pages, and forms. Changing a Derived ManuScript A ManuScript derived from a base ManuScript is not a finished product. It is only a starting point. You still need to make the changes specific to the particular ManuScript you are creating. In a derived ManuScript, you can change inherited groups and fields or add new ones. Inherited, changed, and new groups and fields are indicated visually. Field and group names are displayed: In italics when they are derived. In bold if they have been changed. As normal text when they are created in the current ManuScript. Demoting or Reverting Changes to Inherited Fields You can remove a change you make to a derived field by demoting it, or making it revert back, to the original form found in the inherited ManuScript. To demote or revert a field: Click a changed field. On the Edit menu, click Demote/Revert Field . The Confirm Demote/Revert dialog box appears. You can also: Right-click the field, and then click Demote/Revert Field . Click OK . The field name is no longer bold, signifying a changed field. It reverts back to italics, indicating that it is a derived field. Viewing a ManuScript Inheritance Chain You can view a ManuScript's inheritance chain in EXAMPLE Author ® in two ways: On the Inheritance tab of the ManuScript Properties dialog box ( File > Properties ). In the Inheritance dialog box ( View > Inheritance ). ManuScript Properties Dialog Box The Inheritance tab on the ManuScript Properties dialog box ( File > Properties ) allows users to view and define a ManuScript's inheritance chain. Depending upon the type of inheritance used by a ManuScript, the Inheritance tab may display that ManuScript's inheritance chain in two ways. The Inherited ID , Inherited Page ID , and Inherited Form ID fields specify the ManuScripts directly inherited by the derived ManuScript. If a ManuScript's inheritance chain is defined using these fields, that ManuScript only contains information about ManuScripts that are directly inherited. If those inherited ManuScripts are, in turn, derived from other ManuScripts, users must open the inherited ManuScripts to view their inherited ManuScripts. Users must continue opening each ManuScript specified on the Inheritance tab of derived ManuScripts until they reach the bottom of the inheritance chain. The Inheritance Maps section specifies the entire inheritance chain for the active ManuScript. If a ManuScript's inheritance chain is defined using this section, that ManuScript contains information about each ManuScript in its inheritance chain. As a result, users can view the entire chain for each type of inheritance (data, pages, and forms) in this section without opening any additional ManuScripts. Inheritance Dialog Box The Inheritance dialog box ( View > Inheritance ) allows users to view an entire ManuScript inheritance chain for each type of inheritance (data, pages, and forms) and, if desired, open any ManuScript in that chain.

Importing ManuScripts Importing ManuScripts Save Page to .PDF You can import all or part of an existing ManuScript. When you import ManuScript elements into another ManuScript, copies of those elements become part of the new ManuScript with no connection to the original ManuScript. For more information, see Importing Fields and Groups . Importing an entire ManuScript makes a copy of the ManuScript and places it in the ManuScript Catalog directory. To include the ManuScript in the Catalog but not copy it to the ManuScript Catalog directory, link the ManuScript into the Catalog, rather than importing it. Importing a ManuScript into the Catalog Importing a ManuScript into the Catalog creates a copy of the ManuScript and stores that copy in the system location dictated by the Catalog path and the key values specified in the ManuScript. To import a ManuScript into the Catalog: Click the Open ManuScript button . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. Click the Import button . The Import ManuScript dialog box appears. Browse to the appropriate location, and then click the file to import. Click Open . The ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box appears. (Optional) Change the Description for the new ManuScript. (Optional) Change the key Values as appropriate. Click OK . The lob and state keys are used to create a directory structure in the Catalog location. The ManuScript or data file is stored in the state folder which is stored within the lob folder in the path set up for the Catalog. The keys used previously in the ManuScript are the default when you import a ManuScript into the Catalog. Importing Elements into an Existing ManuScript To import elements into an existing ManuScript: On the File menu, click Import . The Import Wizard dialog box appears. Click Catalog . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. Click the ManuScript from which to import, and then click Load . Click the appropriate import option. The options are: Import Fields/Groups Import Tables Import Pages Import Forms You can select only one element type during an import function. If you need to import additional types, you must do a second import. Click Next . The elements in the selected ManuScript are displayed in the Select the items to import list. Click each element to import. You can multi-select by using CTRL+click and SHIFT+click. If you select: A group, you automatically select all fields and groups contained within the selected group. A page, you automatically select all elements within the page. Click Next . The elements of the ManuScript are listed. Any items that already exist in the current ManuScript are automatically deselected. You can clear or force the importation of any element by selecting or clearing the appropriate check box. Click Finish . All selected items are imported into the ManuScript. If you start with a new ManuScript with just the root element and import all elements from another ManuScript, you are, in essence, importing the ManuScript into the Catalog. A better method is to import the ManuScript directly. Import ManuScript dialog box In the Import ManuScript dialog, browse to and then click an appropriate file, and then click Open .

Managing ManuScript Properties Managing ManuScript Properties Save Page to .PDF The properties defined in the ManuScript Properties dialog box are stored in the <properties> tag of the ManuScript XML. To open the ManuScript Properties dialog: On the File menu, click Properties . ManuScript Properties Dialog Box The ManuScript Properties dialog box specifies the properties for a ManuScript. It includes two tabs, Standard Properties and Inheritance. Standard Properties Tab Item Action/Description ManuScript ID Unique identifier for the ManuScript. EditID Click to open the Edit ManuScriptID dialog box where you can edit the ManuScriptID. Version ID Unique ID for all versions of a ManuScript. Caption Edit the name of the file as it appears in the ManuScript Catalog. Image File Change the image file used for rendering presentations. Click the Ellipsis button to browse to the file location. Version Edit the version number of the ManuScript. This number is automatically updated when you click New version in the ManuScript Catalog dialog box. Version Date Click Date to display a calendar from which you can click an appropriate Version Date. Culture Culture for this ManuScript. If a numeric format mask begins with the dollar sign ($), the mask will output with the culture-aware currency symbol, thousands separator, and decimal separator. For example, a mask such as $???,??0.00 will replace the dollar sign ($), the comma (,) and the period (.) with the currency symbol, thousands separator, and decimal separator determined by the ManuScript culture. Currency Currency symbol appropriate to the selected culture. This symbol will be included in any C n format masks, or custom format masks beginning with the dollar sign($), used in the ManuScript. TRUE=1 When selected, the value for TRUE throughout the ManuScript is 1. If cleared, the value for TRUE is -1. Field Cache When this check box is selected, fields are only executed once, unless overridden by the Always Execute Property. Should only be cleared for legacy ManuScript purposes. Short Circuit Conditionals Set this property to True to cease processing compound conditionals upon resolution, leaving unneeded conditions unprocessed. True and False override the default setting, defined as the value of the ShortCircuitCond setting in the ManuScript section of the Server.config file. Clear Iterator Context Set this property to True to support processing iterators nested under iterators. Enabling this option can slow ManuScript processing. True and False override the default setting, defined as the value of the ClearIterContext setting in the ManuScript section of the Server.config file. Data Schema Name of the XML file used for mapping, paths, and other data schema related functions. Click the Ellipsis button to browse to the data schema file location. Primary Value Field used as the final result of the ManuScript when the ManuScript is executed. Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select the primary value field. Contexts List of current contexts for this ManuScript. Add Click to add a new context. Delete Click to delete an existing context. Rename Click to rename a context. Release Notes The list of user-entered notes about the ManuScript. Add New Click to open the ManuScript Release Notes dialog box to add a new release note to the Release Notes text box. Inheritance Tab Item Action/Description Inherited ID Select the base ManuScript for Data inheritance. Click the Ellipsis button to select a ManuScript from the Catalog. Click Clear to remove the Inherited ID from the ManuScript. Inherited Page ID Select the base ManuScript for Pages inheritance. Click the Ellipsis button to select a ManuScript from the Catalog. Click Clear to remove the Inherited Page ID from the ManuScript. Inherited Form ID Select the base ManuScript for Forms inheritance. Click the Ellipsis button to select a ManuScript from the Catalog. Click Clear to remove the Inherited Form ID from the ManuScript. Build Inheritance Maps From Static List Click this to display the map of all inherited ManuScripts in the lists for Data, Pages, and Forms ManuScripts. Data ManuScripts List of ManuScripts inherited by the base Data ManuScript. You can adjust the list with the buttons as follows: Button Description Add a new ManuScript to the inheritance map. Delete the selected ManuScript. Change the version of the selected ManuScript. Move selected ManuScript up in the list. Move selected ManuScript down in the list. Pages ManuScripts List of ManuScripts inherited by the base Pages ManuScript. You can adjust the list with the buttons as shown above. Forms ManuScripts List of ManuScripts inherited by the base Forms ManuScript. You can adjust the list with the buttons as shown above. Changing ManuScript IDs The ManuScript ID is a number that uniquely identifies each ManuScript to the system. You should change the ManuScript ID only if you are certain you know the effect of such a change. To change the ManuScript ID: On the File menu, click Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, click Edit ID . The Edit ManuScriptID dialog box appears. In the ManuScriptID text box, enter the new ManuScript ID. You should read and pay particular attention to the information shown in the dialog box. Click OK . Changing ManuScript Captions The ManuScript Caption is the name of the file as it is listed in the ManuScript Catalog. To change the ManuScript Caption: On the File menu, click Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, in the Caption text box, enter the new ManuScript Caption. Click OK . Changing the Image File Location The Image File location is the base file name of the image file to be used for rendering presentations. To edit the Image File location: On the File menu, click Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, in the Image file text box, enter the path to the appropriate file. Click the file, and then click OK . Changing ManuScript Version Numbers The Version Number is a reference number that allows you to keep track of the versions of the ManuScript. To change the Version Number: On the File menu, click Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, in the Version text box, enter the new version number. Click OK . Changing ManuScript Version Dates The Version Date is the date of the last revision of this ManuScript. To edit the Version Date: On the File menu, click Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, click Date . The Date Selector dialog box appears. Click the appropriate date in the calendar, or enter the date in YYYY-MM-DD format. Click OK . Changing the Data Schema File To change the Data Schema file: On the File menu, click Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, in the Data Schema text box, enter the path to the appropriate file. Click the file, and then click OK . Changing the Primary Value The Primary Value should be the field that holds the final result of the ManuScript. To change the Primary Value: On the File menu, click Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Primary Value text box. The Reference Select dialog box appears. Click the appropriate field, and then click OK . Changing the ManuScript Culture The Culture property determines date and currency formatting in a ManuScript. Note that short date formats are determined by Web browser settings when viewing the ManuScript in EXAMPLE Express ® . To change the Culture: On the File menu, click Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, click the appropriate Culture . The Currency field changes to display the currency symbol for the selected culture. Defining Contexts Contexts specify the type of user accessing the ManuScript in the downstream system or user interface. For instance, an insurance ManuScript may be used by an agent, an underwriter, or a customer. Contexts ensure that users can view only the interface portions required for their parts of the process. An agent may need to see a specific surcharge applied to the customer whereas the customer only needs to see the total policy premium. By adding a context to specific fields, messages, and pages, only those ManuScript viewers logged in under that context see the context-related portions. You can use contexts directly on annotations, on message text, and in conditionals for many other EXAMPLE Author ® elements, such as action buttons on pages, using the current context of the user. Deleting Contexts To delete a context: On the File menu, click ManuScript Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, in the Contexts area, click the context to delete. Click Delete . Adding Contexts To add a context: On the File menu, click ManuScript Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, in the Contexts section, click Add . The Add Context dialog box appears. Enter the context name. Click OK . Renaming Contexts To rename a context: On the File menu, click ManuScript Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, in the Contexts area, click the context to rename. Click Rename . The Rename Context dialog box appears. Enter the new context name. Click OK . Adding Release Notes To add a Release Note: On the File menu, click ManuScript Properties . The ManuScript Properties dialog box appears. On the Standard Properties tab, in the Release Notes section, click Add New . The ManuScript Release Notes dialog box appears. Enter the release note, and then click OK . (Optional) Click the Add New button to add additional notes.

Fields View Fields View Save Section to .PDF Save Page to .PDF The development of a ManuScript involves defining how each field gets its value and the rules each field processes. You use groups in the ManuScript to organize fields and sub-groups into a logical collection. Fields contain the power of computation and rules execution. The Fields view is the listing of groups and fields in the ManuScript. It allows you to edit the details for each group or field. To open the Fields view: Click the Fields button . You can also: On the View menu, click Fields . Creating and Modifying Fields and Groups Creating and Modifying Fields and Groups Save Page to .PDF In the Fields view, you can add, delete, move, edit, and duplicate fields and groups. Adding a Group Fields View A group is an organizational tool that helps hold and organize all fields within a ManuScript. Every ManuScript must start with a root group to hold all information in that ManuScript. Every group is a child of another group unless it is the root group of the ManuScript. Group names can contain only alphanumeric characters, and the first character cannot be a number. To add a group: On the Edit menu, click Add Group . The Add New Group dialog box appears with a tree diagram of the groups and fields in the ManuScript. You can also: • Press CTRL+G. • Right-click the Fields list, and then click Add Group . In New Group Name , enter the name of the new group. Click Public or Private to specify the visibility of the group. For more information, see Visibility . The root group of a ManuScript must be public. Other groups may be either public or private. Private groups are an organizational tool to help group fields together within the ManuScript. In the Create Group as child of list, click the group that is to be the parent for the new group. If you select a group before adding a new group, the selected group is highlighted in the Group as child of list. (Optional) Click Path to open the Path Selector dialog box to choose a path from the ManuScript schema. Click OK . If you added the group from the New Field dialog box, click OK . Adding a Field Fields View Fields contain the rules, calculations, and values that define a product. To add a field: Click the Add Field button . The Add New Field dialog box appears. You can also: • On the Edit menu, click Add Field . • Press CTRL+A. • Right-click the Fields list, and then click Add Field . Enter a Name for the field. If you need more than one word to name the field, use a capital letter to begin each word with no spaces between words. Move the cursor to another field. (Optional) Select the Prepend group name to field check box. When you select this option, the group name is added to the field name so you can use the same name for fields in different groups. (Optional) For the group name to be included as part of the caption for the field, select the Use group name in Caption default check box. Click Public or Private to define field visibility. For more information, see Visibility . (Optional) In Caption , enter a caption for the field. In the Value list, click the value type. For more information, see Defining Property Value Types . In the Data Type list, click the data type. The available data types include boolean , date , datetime , float , int , string , and time . For more information, see Setting Field Data Types . In the Create in list, click the group in which the field is to be created. If you select a group before adding a field, Create in defaults to the selected group. (Optional) Click Path to open the Path Selector dialog box to choose a path from the ManuScript schema. Click OK . Deleting a Field or Group Fields View To delete a field or group: Click the field or group to be deleted, and then click the Delete button . You can also: • On the Edit menu, click Delete Field/Group . • Right-click the field, and then click Delete Field/Group . In the Confirm Delete dialog box, click OK . Moving Fields and Groups Fields View To move a field or group to a different group: Click the field or group. On the Edit menu, click Move . The Move Field to Group dialog box appears with a list of the groups and fields in the ManuScript. You can also: Right-click the field or group, and then click Move . Click the group to which the field or group should move. Click OK . Duplicating a Group Fields View To duplicate a group: Click the group. Click the Duplicate button . You can also: • On the Edit menu, click Duplicate . • Right-click the group, and then click Duplicate . In the Duplicate Item dialog box, enter the Name of the duplicate group. (Optional) If appropriate, select the Change Path Elements check box. If this option is selected, the data path for the new group is changed to reflect the new name. If cleared, the data path for the new group is duplicated from the original group. Click OK . When you duplicate a group, all the fields and groups within that group are duplicated. (Optional) In any Duplicate Sub-Group dialog box that appears, change the sub-group name, and then click OK . Duplicating a Field Fields View To duplicate a field: Click the field. Click the Duplicate button . You can also: • On the Edit menu, click Duplicate . • Right-click the field and then click Duplicate . In the Duplicate Item dialog box, enter the Name of the duplicate field. (Optional) If appropriate, select the Prepend group name to field check box. If this option is selected, the group name, followed by a period, appears before the field name. (Optional) If appropriate, select the Change Path Elements check box. If this option is selected, the data path for the new field is changed to reflect the new name. If cleared, the data path for the new field is duplicated from the original field. Click OK . Renaming Fields and Groups Fields View To rename a field or group: Click the field or group. On the Edit menu, click Rename . You can also: Right-click the field or group and then click Rename . The Rename Item dialog box appears. In the Name text box, enter the new name for the field or group. (Optional) Select the Prepend group name to field check box to all the appropriate group name, followed by a period, to the new field name. (Optional) Select the Change path elements check box to change the field or group path to reflect the new name. Click OK . Importing Fields and Groups Fields View To import fields or groups into a ManuScript: On the File menu, click Import . The Import Wizard dialog box appears. Click Catalog . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. Click the ManuScript from which to import, and then click Load . Click Import Fields/Groups , and then click Next . The groups and fields in the selected ManuScript are displayed in the Select the item(s) to import list. Click the groups and fields to import. You can multi-select using CTRL+click and SHIFT+click. If you select a group, you automatically select all fields and groups contained within it. Click Next . The elements to be imported are listed. Any items you selected in the previous display that already exist in the ManuScript are not selected for importing. The list includes any tables that the fields reference. You can clear any element you do not want to import by clearing the check box. You can force the importation of an element you already have in the current ManuScript by selecting the appropriate check box or by clicking the Mark all items button . Click Finish . All selected items are imported into the ManuScript. Data Mart Attributes You can set data mart attributes to shred data in both fields and groups. Setting Group Shredding Attributes Fields View To set shredding attributes for a group: Click the group. Only public groups may be shredded. On the Edit menu, click Data Mart Attributes . The Data Mart Attributes dialog box appears. Define the appropriate Attributes. Data Mart Attributes for a group include some or all of the following properties. Property Description Exclude from Schema Select to exclude this group from the schema. pkeyColumn Name of the primary key within the foreign table referenced by the ID field. parentTable Name of the table referenced by the ID field. parentForeignKey New name for the foreign key of the parent table. tableName Name for the database table. By default, the name is taken from the group name path. relationship Indication of whether the group is involved in a many-to-many relationship or a single relationship. Setting Field Shredding Attributes Fields View To set shredding attributes for a field: Click the field. Only public fields may be shredded. On the Edit menu, click Data Mart Attributes . The Data Mart Attributes dialog box appears. Define the appropriate Attributes. Data Mart Attributes for a field include some or all of the following properties. Property Description Exclude from Schema Select to exclude this field from the schema. fkeyColumn Name of the foreign key column that references the related table. fIDTable Name of the table referenced by the ID field. fkeyTable Name of the foreign table that contains the database lookup value. pkeyColumn Name of the primary key within the foreign table referenced by the ID field.

Identifying Where a Field or Group Is Used Identifying Where a Field or Group Is Used Save Page to .PDF Fields View You can see all the places a field, group, or table is used with the Used by function. This function identifies the other fields in the ManuScript that use the selected item. To identify other fields that use the selected item: On the Edit menu, click Used by . You can also: • Press CTRL+U • Right-click the field, and then click Used by . Before you modify a field, check where that field is used to determine how the change might affect previously coded fields. Note that the Used by function does not display the use of a field in external ManuScripts.

The Inheritance (Data) dialog box displays the entire ManuScript inheritance chain for the current ManuScript's data (fields and tables). From this dialog box, you can open any ManuScript in the inheritance chain by selecting the desired ManuScript and clicking Go to . To access the Inheritance (Data) dialog box from Fields view: On the View menu, point to Inheritance , and then click Data . Navigating to Parent and Ancestor Groups Navigating to Parent and Ancestor Groups Save Page to .PDF Fields View In complex ManuScripts, you can quickly navigate to parent or ancestor groups. To select the parent group of the active field or group: Click the appropriate field or group. On the Edit menu, click Go to Parent . You can also: • Press F10. • Right-click the field or group and then click Go to Parent . To select the first public ancestor group of the active field or group: Click the appropriate field or group. On the Edit menu, click Go to Public Parent . You can also: • Press F11. • Right-click the field or group and then click Go to Public Parent .

Visibility Visibility Save Page to .PDF Fields View Visibility in ManuScripts is a means to hide those groups and fields that are used only for organization or processing within a ManuScript. Fields and groups set to Private will not appear in the XML output of a ManuScript. Public fields can be included in private groups. Private groups are useful for organization within a ManuScript. Private fields contain calculations, conditions, or other processing functions that are used only within a given ManuScript or its derived ManuScripts. Groups must be public if they are: Fields must be public if they are: Iterated A table in a shredded database Required by a data schema Included in output XML Fed into any downstream system Included on an EXAMPLE Express ® or other HTML page A column in a shredded database Required by a data schema Included in output XML Fed into any downstream system Included in a report generated from a shredded database

Group Properties Group Properties Save Page to .PDF You can modify the way groups work by modifying group properties. Setting Group Visibility Fields View The visibility of a group identifies whether the group is viewable by the end user (public) or used only within the ManuScript (private). To set the visibility of a group: Click the group. Click Public or Private . In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to change the item visibility, or No to cancel. For more information on Public and Private settings, see Visibility . Setting the Caption Fields View The Caption property specifies the name of the field or group as the user sees it. To set the value of the Caption property: Click the field or group. In the Caption text box, enter the appropriate caption. No quotes are required around the text. Setting Group Classes Fields View Classes are a method used for sorting tables, fields, and groups. To set the value of the Class property: Click the group. In the Class text box, enter a name for the class. No quotes are required around the text. Setting Group Maximums Fields View The Maximum property specifies the maximum number of iterations allowed for a public group. To set the value of the group Maximum property: Click the group. In the Maximum text box, enter the maximum number of iterations for the group. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. If you enter a constant value, do not enclose it in quotes unless you enter an asterisk, which indicates unlimited iterations. Setting the Group Required Property Fields View The Required property indicates to EXAMPLE Express ® that a value for the group or field is mandatory in the interview process. To set the value of the Required property: Click the group. In the Required text box, enter 1 (for true) if the field or group is required, or 0 (for false) if the field or group is not required. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. You can specify a boolean field to make the group conditionally required. Setting Related Groups Fields View The Related Group property specifies which groups are related and how. To set the value of the Related Group property: Click the group. Click Related Groups . The Group Relationships dialog box appears. Specify the related groups. For each related group: In the Related Group list, click the related group. Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Foreign Key Linking to the Related Group column to use the Reference Select dialog box to select the appropriate field In the Link Type list, click reference or mirror . Click OK . Setting the Data Path Fields View The Data Path property is the element or attribute of the XML data document where the group is mapped or that contains the value of the field. To change or add a path to a field or group, the field or group must be public, and a schema must be defined for the ManuScript. To set the Data Path property: Click the field or group. Click Path . The Path Selector dialog box appears. Click the item from the pre-determined schema to develop a path. Click OK .

Field Properties Field Properties Save Section to .PDF Save Page to .PDF You can modify the way fields work by modifying field properties. Section Contents Execution Order of Field Properties Defining Property Value Types Value Tab Definition Tab Affects Tab

Execution Order of Field Properties Execution Order of Field Properties Save Page to .PDF Fields View The order of execution of the value types of a public field is listed below. For each step, if the property is set to (n/a), that step is ignored. Otherwise, the value of the field is modified by the rule. Any access to the field during the execution of these rules returns the in-progress value of the field. The ManuScript executes field Properties in the following order: Check the Applicable status of the field. If the Applicable status is false, check only the Default rule. Execute the fields listed with a Pre-Effect type on the Affects tab. Access the value from the data document according to the Path of the field. If the path node does not yet exist in the data document or the value of the node is empty, check the Default rule. If a value is returned in the Default rule, set the value of the field to that default value and issue a message indicating that the default was applied to the field. Apply the Value rule. Check the Minimum rule. If there is a specified minimum and the current value of the field is less than the specified minimum, set the value of the field to the specified minimum, and issue the message associated with the minimum rule. Check the Maximum rule. If there is a specified maximum and the current value of the field is greater than the specified maximum, set the value of the field to the specified maximum, and issue the message associated with the maximum rule. Apply any Miscellaneous Rules to the value. If the value was modified, assign the node in the data document, as specified by the Path , the resulting new value. If the node does not yet exist in the data document, add it. Execute the fields listed with a Post-Effect type on the Affects tab. For private fields, the order of execution is to access the Value rule, apply Pre-Effect fields, and then apply any Miscellaneous Rules .

Defining Property Value Types Defining Property Value Types Save Section to .PDF Save Page to .PDF You can set the value of many field Properties by selecting a value type and then defining it. Property value types can be selected from drop-down lists associated with these Properties. When you select a value type for a property, the Definition pane includes the information needed to further define the property. The types of property values include the following. Type Value Determination (none) User-entered value. Constant/Field Constant value or field reference. Calculation Result of a calculation. Conditional True or false result based on a condition. Date Result of a calculation using dates. External Value from a field in another ManuScript. Function Value used to determine various field characteristics such as padding, length, and whether to use upper-case or lower-case text. Iterator Result of multiple iterations in terms of a sum, maximum, minimum, or count. Lookup Value extracted from a table. Request Value from an attribute in a request. Select Value returned if the field value is equal to one of a set of specified cases. Session Value of specified data for the current session. Substring Specified portion of a string. Sum/Accumulator Result of an evaluation of the value from all iterations of a specified field in an iterative group. WrittenPrem Written premium value based on the policy data defined in the field. Integration Point Values returned from a third party reporting service. Available only on the Value property.

Calculation Calculation Save Page to .PDF With a Calculation value type, you specify an equation to determine the value. Each step of the equation is listed in the calculation grid. Steps are performed in the order listed, modified by groupings. Calculation fields do not follow the standard mathematic order of operations. Be sure to group the lines in your calculation appropriately. When the value of a field is determined by a calculation, the calculation definition identifies the operators, values, groupings, and rounding used to calculate the value. Calculation Buttons Fields View The Calculations Definitions pane toolbar contains the following buttons. Button Name Action Insert Phrase Adds a step to the calculation. Remove Phrase Removes the selected step from the calculation. Group Phrases Groups the selected steps. Ungroup Phrases Ungroups the selected steps. Ungroup All Clears all groupings. Cut Cuts the selected text to the clipboard. Copy Copies the selected text to the clipboard. Paste Pastes the text from the clipboard. Move Up Moves the selected item up in the list. Move Down Moves the selected item down in the list. View Equation Displays a mathematical summary of the calculation. Calculation Operators Fields View The following operators are available in the Op list for calculation fields. Operator Description = Begins a calculation. + Adds a value. - Subtracts a value. * Multiplies a value. / Divides a value. min Returns the highest of the following options: The current value The parameter value max Returns the lowest of the following options: The current value The parameter value power Calculates the current value raised to the specified power. and Performs a logical AND between the current value and the parameter value. (Usually returns a boolean value.) or Performs a logical OR between the current value and the parameter value. (Usually returns a boolean value.) mod Calculates the integer remainder of the division of a value by the parameter value. abs Calculates the absolute value of a value. cos Calculates the cosine of a value (in degrees). cosh Calculates the hyperbolic cosine of a value (in degrees). exp Calculates e raised to the specified power. log Calculates the logarithm of a value. log10 Calculates the base 10 logarithm of a value. sin Calculates the sine of a value (in degrees). sinh Calculates the hyperbolic sine of a value (in degrees). sqrt Calculates the square root of a value. tan Calculates the tangent of a value. tanh Calculates the hyperbolic tangent of a value. rand Sets the current value to a random integer less than the specified value and greater than or equal to zero (0). Defining a Calculation Fields View To define a Calculation value: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Calculation . Specify each step in the calculation: In the Op list, click the operator. For a description of operators, see Calculation Operators . The first operator in any equation must be = . Enter the Value . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. (Optional) In the Round list at the end of each line, click the appropriate rounding for the calculation. (Optional) Highlight steps to be grouped in the calculation, and then click the Group button . Ignoring a Calculation Fields View A ManuScript can include a decision as to whether or not a calculation is performed. The calculation may not be pertinent in some situations, and you may wish to use a value other than the calculation result. When you select the Ignore calculation when check box, the ManuScript checks a condition before processing the calculation and then determines whether to use the value of the calculation or another value. For example, you only need to compute a premium if a particular coverage is requested. To limit when a calculation is performed: Select the Ignore calculation when check box to specify conditions for the calculation. Click CB to use the Condition Builder dialog box to specify the conditions under which the calculation should be ignored. In set the value to , enter the value to be used when the calculation is ignored. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Calculation Rounding Options Fields View You can use the following rounding options. As a rule, the digits 1, 2, 3, and 4 round down and 5, 6, 7, 8, and 9 round up. Option Description Dollar (1) Rounds to the nearest whole number. FiftyCents (.50) Rounds to the nearest .0, .5 or 1.00. Quarter (.25) Rounds to the nearest .0, .25, .5, .75, or 1.00. TwentyCents (.20) Rounds to the nearest .0, .2, .4, .6, .8, or 1.00. Dime (.10) Rounds to the nearest tenth. Nickel (.05) Rounds to the nearest .00 or .05. Penny (.01) Rounds to the nearest hundredth. 3-digits Rounds to the nearest thousandth. 4-digits Rounds to the nearest ten-thousandth. 5-digits Rounds to the nearest hundred-thousandth. 10s Rounds to the nearest ten. 100s Rounds to the nearest hundred. 1000s Rounds to the nearest thousand. Truncate Removes all digits after the decimal without any rounding. Original whole number remains. Trunc(10s) Removes all digits after the tenths place without rounding. Trunc(100s) Removes all digits after the hundredths place without rounding. Trunc(1000s) Removes all digits after the thousandths place without rounding. RoundUp Rounds up to the nearest whole number. RoundDown Rounds down to the nearest whole number.

Conditional Conditional Save Page to .PDF With a Conditional value type, you define a condition that determines a true or false value. Conditions can be added to conditional tables, conditional fields, and to Miscellaneous Rules for fields and in other places where a condition is needed. When using the Condition Builder dialog box you can add, delete, group, ungroup, edit, and change the order of conditions. Condition Builder Buttons Fields View The Condition Builder toolbar contains the following buttons. Button Name Action Add Phrase Adds a phrase to the condition. Remove Phrase Removes the selected phrases. Group Phrases Groups the selected phrases. Ungroup Phrases Ungroups the selected phrases. Ungroup All Clears all groupings. Cut Cuts the selected phrase to the clipboard. Copy Copies the selected phrase to the clipboard. Paste Pastes a phrase from the clipboard. Move Up Moves the selected phrase up. Move Down Moves the selected phrase down. Defining a Condition Fields View To define a Condition value: In the list for the appropriate property, click Condition . You can enter the condition directly into the condition window, but it is best practice to use the Condition Builder. Click Condition Builder . The Condition Builder dialog box appears with the Reference Select dialog box over it. Specify each phrase of the condition: For each condition, you identify two references to be compared and the method of comparison. To use multiple conditions, connect the conditions with And or Or connectors. Enter the first Reference . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. In the Comparison list, click the appropriate item. The available comparisons are = , < , > , <= , >= , <> , Is , IsNot , and Contains . Enter the second Reference . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. (Optional) In the Connector list, click And or Or to add another phrase. (Optional) Highlight phrases to be grouped in the condition, and then click the Group button . In the Boolean Result list, click the desired result. Options are: Just return TRUE or FALSE Return the specified value for TRUE, nothing for FALSE Return the specified value for TRUE or FALSE If you specified a Boolean Result using specified values, enter the TRUE Value and FALSE Value to be used as appropriate. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Adding a Condition Phrase Fields View To add a condition phrase: Click the line above the line on which you wish the new phrase to appear. Click the Add Phrase button . Define the condition phrase. Deleting a Condition Phrase Fields View To delete a condition phrase: Click the condition phrase. Click the Delete Phrase button . Grouping and Ungrouping Condition Phrases Fields View To make sure that the certain condition phrases are carried out in the correct logical sequence, groupings can be added or removed to isolate logical conditions. To group and ungroup condition phrases: Select the appropriate condition phrases. Click the Group Phrases button to group the selected condition phrases. Click the Ungroup Phrases button to remove the grouping. Click the Ungroup All button to clear all groupings in the current condition. Moving Condition Phrases Fields View To move condition phrases up or down: Click the condition phrase to move within the Condition Builder. Click the Move Up button or the Move Down button to move the condition phrase.

Constant Field Constant Field Save Page to .PDF With a Constant/Field value type, you assign the value as a constant value or as the value of a referenced field. Click a field with a defined Options property, and then click SC . The Code Select dialog box appears. Use the arrow buttons to move codes from the Available Codes list to the Used Codes list. Click OK . The codes are placed in the Value text box. Click OK . To the right of the Constant/Field Value column, click the Ellipsis button to open the Reference Select dialog box. In the Field or Value area, click Field Reference . Click the appropriate field. Click OK .

Date Date Save Page to .PDF Date value types are defined based on a: Numeric difference between two dates New date value determined by addition or subtraction Year, month, or day portion of the date returned as a number Defining a Numeric Difference Between Dates Fields View To define a Date value for the numeric difference between dates: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Date . (Optional) Select the Round up if partial difference check box to round up if any part of the unit is left over. (Optional) Select the Ignore years in difference check box to determine the value based on the number of months and days between the dates regardless of the difference in years. Defining a Date by Addition or Subtraction Fields View To define a new Date value by addition or subtraction: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Date . In the Date Method list, click Create a new date by adding amount or Create a new date by subtracting amount . Enter a Start Date . This defines the beginning date value. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Enter an Amount to be added or subtracted. The unit option determines the unit to be changed. If the day of the month is not valid, the day is moved back to a valid date.
