In the Date Method list, click Get a part of the date . Enter a Start Date . This defines the date from which to extract the value. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. In the Unit list, click Days , Months , or Years . The unit option determines the portion of the date to get. The resulting value is the numeric value of the portion requested.

External External Save Page to .PDF With an External value type, you specify how to get a value from a field in another ManuScript. You can separate the logic into multiple ManuScripts and access the values and fields found in them. All the ManuScripts involved in external references must use the same data document. When a ManuScript executes a reference to an external field, the referenced ManuScript is executed using the active data document. Any modifications to the content of the data document remain after return from the referenced ManuScript. There are times when the accessed ManuScript may need to be determined at runtime. To compute the ManuScript ID for an External field reference, use another field in the ManuScript to determine the correct ID. It is a best practice to use a field that has a Lookup value type. If you must determine the ManuScript ID at runtime, create a table to hold the possible IDs and access the ID using a lookup. This is the simplest way to manage the ID values and allows the deployment process to publish the list of required external ManuScript IDs. When a ManuScript is deployed, it communicates with the deployment manager and lists all the dependencies that should be deployed with the ManuScript. When a ManuScript accesses another ManuScript through the External value type, the ManuScript must be able to identify the ManuScripts upon which it is dependant. Defining an External Value Fields View To define an External value: In the list for the appropriate property, click External . Enter the ManuScript to access . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Identify the external ManuScript using the unique ID used in the ManuScript Catalog. Because a ManuScript ID is globally unique, it should always correctly identify the target ManuScript. The referenced ManuScript ID must exist in the ManuScript Catalog of the machine on which the execution occurs. You can use the ManuScript Catalog Detail dialog box to view the IDs of ManuScripts. In Field in external ManuScript to access , enter the text name of the field to access in the external ManuScript. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. (Optional) If the field being accessed in the external ManuScript is within an iterated group, enter the full Data path of the field to identify the iteration to be used within the data document. Leaving the Data Path empty gets the current iteration, meaning that whatever iteration is active in the calling ManuScript is the iteration in the external ManuScript. It is usually sufficient to leave the Data Path empty. (Optional) Select the Preserve the current data check box. If this option is selected, the Session data is not modified. A copy is sent to the EXAMPLE Server ® . If this option is cleared, the actual Session data is used and may be modified. In the All data changes are discarded other than the contents of the following group list, click the group that is not to have its content discarded. When a group is selected, the data changes from the EXAMPLE Server ® for that group are preserved.

Function Function Save Page to .PDF ManuScript functions serve as shorcuts for applying commonly-used field formatting. With a Function value type, you apply one of several possible functions to a specified field. The following table lists the functions available out of the box for use with the Function value type. Function Description String.Contains Determines whether a string is contained within another string. String.CountItems Counts the number of entries in a delimited string. String.Format Formats a string according to the specified format mask and ManuScript culture. Primarily used to format numbers and some date formatting. String.HashMD5 Returns a MD5 hash of the string. String.IsNumeric Determines whether the string is a numeric value. The IsNumeric function can be used with all culture settings and comma separators. String.Lookup Retrieves a value within a specifically delimited string of key/value pairs given the key and delimiters. String.LowerCase Converts a string to its lower-case equivalent. String.Left Retrieves a portion of a string starting from the front of the string given the number of characters to retrieve. String.Length Counts the number of characters in a string. String.PadRight Appends a string to a given length with a specified character. String.PadLeft Prepends a string to a given length with a specified character. String.RegExMatch Returns true if the string contains a match for the specified regular expression. The .NET RegEx parser is used. String.Replace Replaces a string within another string with a new string. String.Right Retrieves a portion of a string starting from the end of the string given the number of characters to retrieve. String.Trim Removes leading and trailing white space from a string. String.UpperCase Converts a string to its upper-case equivalent. Adding Custom Functions You can create custom functions and add them to EXAMPLE Author ® by extending the ManuScriptFunctions_custom.xml file located in the Shared directory in the Duck Creek root directory (for example, C:\Program Files\Duck Creek Technologies \Shared ). To add a new function: In the ManuScriptFunctions_Custom.xml file, add all the desired functions, following the same syntax found in the ManuScriptFunctions.xml file. To avoid losing custom functions during an upgrade, never add them to the ManuScriptFunctions.xml file. Defining a Function Value Type in EXAMPLE Author® Fields View To define a function value: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Function . In the Function list, click the appropriate function option. The type of value returned by the function is displayed and the following possible function options appear: Source Item Separator Item Separator *Key Separator Key Length Count Character The function options available are determined by the type of function selected in the Function list. Enter the value for each function option available.

Integration Point Integration Point Save Page to .PDF With an Integration Point value type, a ManuScript can make calls directly to a third-party Web service. The Integration Point type allows a user to select from lists of pre-defined integrations. Once the user has selected the desired integration, he or she can then map the desired ManuScript fields to each of the integration's send and receive data points. Integration Point fields will return a value of 1 if the <Integration.sendRq> request executed by the field is successful. If the request is not successful, the system will return a 0 and a message describing the error. This value type is available only on the Value property for a field. Integrations can be added to the list of available integrations using the Integration Definition Manager tool in the EXAMPLE Utility Center. Defining an Integration Point Value Fields View This value type is available only on the Value property for a field. To define an Integration Point value: In the dropdown list for the Value property, click Integration Point . In the Integration File list, click the file containing the list of integrations you wish to choose from. In the Integration list, click the desired integration. On the Items to Send tab, for each data point listed: In the Field column, use the Ellipsis button to open the Reference Select dialog box and select a field to map to the data point. Helpful information about the selected data point appears below the list of data points. (If applicable) If an Ellipsis button appears to the right of the Option Mapping column, use the button to open the Edit Coded List dialog box and map the values of the specified ManuScript field to the values expected by the integration. When finished, click OK . Hover over an option in the list to see its description. Click the Items to Receive tab. Repeat Step 4 for all data points on the Items to Receive tab. On the Integration Options tab, define additional options for the integration point: Enter a value for On Failure . This is the field to execute if the integration request fails. Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. (Optional) Enter a value for Only Execute . This field determines when to execute the integration request. If left blank, the request will always execute. Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. (Optional) Enter a value for Error Message . This field specifies the field that will store an error message received from the third-party Web service if the integration request fails. If no field is specified, the error message will be viewable through DCT Trace Monitor but will not be available for use in the ManuScript. Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field.

Iterator Iterator Save Page to .PDF With an Iterator value type, the returned value is based on the iterations of a group in the ManuScript. The value can be the sum of iterative fields, the minimum or maximum value of an iterative field, the number of iterations in a group, or the current iteration of a group. The Iterator type loops through the group based on the number of iterations in the data document. Every field in the ManuScript has an answer specific to the iterations of its public parent group. When a group is iterated, each field under it has a unique value within the ManuScript. The Iterator type allows those unique values to be summed, compared, or counted. When the Process Subtotals within iterations check box is selected, the Subtotal definition grid appears. The iterator itself runs normally, but as the group is processed, the additional fields defined in the Subtotal definition grid calculate as well. Each line in the Subtotal definition grid acts similarly to a Sum/Accumulator field. During the iteration of a given group, each occurrence of the Field in Iteration is added to the value of the Subtotal Field , assuming the optional Condition is met. Defining an Iterator Value Fields View To define an Iterator value: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Iterator . In the Iterate through list, click the appropriate iteration option: all iterations all other iterations to check all the iterations except the current one the first iteration the last iteration In the of the group list, click the group to iterate. The options are all public groups in the ManuScript. In the to find the list, click the activity to perform on each iteration. The options are: sum to add all values of the specified field. minimum to return the lowest value in all iterations. maximum to return the highest value in all iterations. count to return the number of iterations. current iteration to return the value of the active iteration. Specify the fields upon which to act. Click Add . In the Reference Select dialog box, click the field. The selected field should be within the specified group. (Optional) Add any remaining fields. (Optional) Use the arrow buttons to change the order of the fields. (Optional) Select the Include Deleted check box to include those items marked for deletion. (Optional) Select the Restrict to iterations where check box and specify the conditions to restrict the interactions involved in the looping. (Optional) Select the Advanced Query check box and specify a custom query to make during the iteration. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. (Optional) Select the Order the iterations by check box, and specify the fields with the order for each. In the Field column, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. In the Order list, click Ascending or Descending . Defining Subtotals in Iterator Fields Fields View Each line in the Subtotal definition pane defines a subtotal to calculate. Any field defined in the Field in Iteration column must be a part of the iteration defined by the Iterator field. To define Subtotals within an iteration: Select the Process Subtotals within iterations check box. The Subtotal definition pane appears. To the right of Field in Iteration , click the ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to choose which field to subtotal. To the right of Subtotal Field , click the ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to choose the field which will hold the value of the calculated subtotal. (Optional) To the right of Condition , click the ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to choose a boolean field that determines whether or not an iteration is added to the subtotal.

Lookup Lookup Save Page to .PDF With a Lookup value type, you access values from a table in the ManuScript. The Lookup type specifies the table to be accessed, the name of the data item in the table to be extracted, and the values used to access the keys of the table. Defining a Lookup Value Using an Existing Table Fields View To define a Lookup value using an existing table: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Lookup . In the Table to access list, click the table to use for the lookup. Once a table is selected, the rest of the details reflect the information for the selected table: The Data name list includes the data names from the table. The Keys grid lists the keys defined for the table. (Optional) Click Determine table accessed and then enter a table name and (optional) an external ManuScript ID. This feature allows you to determine the actual table used at runtime. Select the option and a field that returns the text name of the table to be accessed. Any table to be accessed by this lookup must have the same Data Name and list of Key Names as the selected table. You can also enter a ManuScript ID if the table is contained in an external ManuScript. In the Data name list, click the data name from the table to use for the lookup. Enter a Value To Find for each Key Name . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Defining a Lookup Value Using a Custom Defined Table Fields View To define a Lookup value using a custom defined table: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Lookup . In the Table list, click (custom defined) . Select the Determine table accessed check box and then enter a table name and (optional) an external ManuScript ID. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. In the Data name list, enter a data name to use for the lookup in the table. Enter Key Names for the table. The Data name and all Key Names must be the same in each potential table to use. Enter a Value To Find for each Key Name . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Ignoring a Lookup Fields View A ManuScript can include a decision to ignore a lookup. The lookup may not be pertinent in some situations and you may wish to use a value other than the lookup value. When you select the Ignore lookup when check box, the ManuScript checks a condition before processing the lookup and then determines whether to use the value of the lookup or another value. To limit when a lookup is performed: Select the Ignore lookup when check box. Click CB to use the Condition Builder dialog box to specify the conditions under which the lookup should be ignored. In the set the value to text box, enter the value to be used when the condition is met. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference.

Request Request Save Page to .PDF With a Request value type, a ManuScript can make calls directly to the EXAMPLE Server ® and then use a returned attribute as the value for a field. The Request type is best used when calling an external function to drive an application or when extending EXAMPLE Server ® to execute custom components such as calling an object that performs a specialized VIN lookup. When you use EXAMPLE Server ® with a form of authentication, the request is created under the security role of the current session. If the current user permissions are set to a level lower than the specified request requires, an error occurs in the ManuScript. To avoid this error you must define a Default value to be used if the Response Path cannot be found. Defining a Request Value Fields View To define a Request value: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Request . In the Request Command list, click the appropriate command. In the Response Path list, enter the desired path by: Clicking the appropriate path in the list. (List options vary according to the selected Request command.) Clicking the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Entering a literal value (in quotes). Enter a Default Value . The Default value is the value the field is assigned if the request is made and the Response Path cannot be found. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. (Optional) Enter a value for Only Execute . Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field that determines whether or not the request should be made. Enter a value for Modify Session . Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field that determines whether or not session data should be updated if the request is successful. Enter a value for Uses Session . Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field that determines whether or not session data is used by this request. Enter a value for On Failure . This is the field or value to use if the initial request fails. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Add any required request parameters by clicking Request Parameters and then entering the appropriate values in the Name Value Definitions dialog box.

Select Select Save Page to .PDF With a Select value type, you determine a value from a selection list. The Select type is best used when the key selection field has a list of options, but can be used with any field that has a predictable value. To the right of the Select on text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to choose a field from which to select. The Code Select dialog box appears with a list of the codes, if any, for the selected field. Use the Code Select dialog to select the cases to be matched for the Case column. Click a code or codes in the Available codes . Use the arrow buttons to move the codes for the left column (existing) into the right column (used). You can use the arrows to move codes between boxes in both directions. You can also enter values directly in the Case column. Enter values in the Value column. These values are used if the associated cases are matched. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Enter a value in the If no match above, use this value text box. This value is used if no match is found to any of the specified cases. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. The Auto-Fill button can also be used to open the Code Select dialog box to make changes to the Case column.

Current Form Name Name of the current form. Current Form Caption Caption of the current form. Portfolio ID ID of the Portfolio.

Substring Substring Save Page to .PDF With a Substring value type, you create a new string value by extracting a portion of a source string. You can select a substring starting at a specified position or from a delimited list. Defining a Substring Starting at a Specified Position Fields View When you extract a substring starting at a specified position, the new value contains the number of characters, determined by the Length , beginning at the specified start position. To define a Substring starting at a specified position: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Substring . In the Search Method area, click Start at Position . Enter the Source String . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Enter the Start Position . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Enter the Length of the substring. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. If the length is longer than the initial string, only the remaining portion of the string is returned. To define a Substring using a delimited list: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Substring . In the Search Method area, click Delimited List . Enter the Source String . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Enter the Separator Character . You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference.

Sum Accumulator Sum Accumulator Save Page to .PDF With a Sum/Accumulator value type, the returned value is based on the iterations of a group in a ManuScript when called from an Iterator field. Sum/Accumulator fields increase the efficiency of a ManuScript for large iterations. For example, a ManuScript with two Iterator fields loops through the iterative group two complete times.  You can use a single Iterator to increase efficiency.  If you create two Sum/Accumulator fields in the place of the two Iterator fields, and one Iterator field that references the two Sum/Accumulator fields, the iterative groups only loop once for the number of instances of the iterative group.  As the Iterator processes through each group, it performs the selected function for the fields that the Sum/Accumulator fields reference.  When a complete loop is finished, each Sum/Accumulator field holds the calculated value. You do not have to specifically reference the Iterator group.  When a Sum/Accumulator field is referenced and the Iterator has not yet processed, the Iterator executes and gets values for each Sum/Accumulator field. Defining a Sum/Accumulator Value Fields View To define a Sum/Accumulator value: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click Sum/Accumulator . Click the Ellipsis button to the right of Field to access for each iteration's value to use theReference Select dialog box to select a field reference. This is the value on which the iterator operates. Click the Ellipsis button to the right of Iterator field that evaluates the Iterator item to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. This is the field that defines and controls the iteration.

WrittenPrem WrittenPrem Save Page to .PDF WrittenPrem fields simplify the calculation of written premiums. They allow EXAMPLE Server ® , rather than ManuScript logic, to process the complex equations required to calculate written premiums. This feature simplifies ManuScript development and eases EXAMPLE Server ® processing load. WrittenPrem calculation includes a shared configuration option to simplify the creation of WrittenPrem fields. When using the shared configuration, users can apply the shared configuration to a WrittenPrem field, eliminating the need to define ten values or field references that are likely to be common across all written premium calculations in a ManuScript. Two of the values used in written premium processing, Prior Term Premium and Prior Written Premium , are generally not a part of ManuScript architecture. These two values are a part of EXAMPLE TransACT ® 2.1 ManuScripts, or they can be defined in a custom ManuScript. If the ManuScript in which the written premium is being calculated cannot access appropriate values for these components of the written premium calculation, the calculation will not result in a correct written premium. Defining a WrittenPrem Value Fields View To define a WrittenPrem Value: In the dropdown list for the appropriate property, click WrittenPrem . (Optional) To use the previously defined Shared Configuration for written premium processing, select the Use Shared Configuration check box. For each of the following items, define an appropriate value, Xpath, or field reference: Item Description Premium Information Term Premium Full-term premium amount for the coverage. This field should contain the full-term premium amount. Minimum Premium (Optional) Minimum premium amount for the written premium value of the coverage. A value of zero indicates there is no minimum premium. Fully Earned Boolean value (1 or 0) that determines whether the premium for the policy is fully earned. Flat Charge Boolean value (1 or 0) that determines whether the charge for the policy is not pro-rated. Clear Premium Determines whether the value of the term premium is to be cleared. Options include: Never — Do not clear the value of the term premium. If Deleted — Clear the value of the term premium if it is marked for delete and the value specified in the Field column is true (1). The literal value or field reference specified in the Field column must be boolean. This value defaults to true (1). Custom — Clear the value of the term premium according to custom rules. The literal value or field reference specified in the Field column must be boolean. This value defaults to true (1). This option is not available in a shared configuration. Auditable Type Value (0, 1, 2) that determines whether the premium is auditable. Out of the box, the premium is non-auditable. A non-auditable premium will be returned on the final audit. The options are: 0 — Non-Auditable (This is the default setting.) 1 — Auditable Exposure 2 — Auditable Units Configuration Section Prior Term Premium Field or Xpath that contains the premium amount for the prior term. The written premium calculation reads this value. Field Reference — Must be a public field and reference the term premium from the previous transaction. Xpath — Must be a relative path from the specified Term Premium field to the appropriate value. Prior Written Premium Field or Xpath that contains the written premium amount for the prior term. The written premium calculation reads this value. Field Reference — Must be a public field and reference the term premium from the previous transaction. Xpath — Must be a relative path from the specified Term Premium field to the appropriate value. Charge Premium Field or Xpath to which the calculation writes the written premium result. Field Reference — Must be a public field. Xpath — Must be a relative path from the specified Term Premium field to the appropriate value. Date Information Policy Effective Date Effective date of the policy. Policy Expiration Date Expiration date of the policy. Transaction Effective Date Effective date of the transaction initiating the written premium calculation. ProRate Type Numeric value indicating the type of transaction. The written premium calculation uses this value to determine how to handle prorating. The options are: 0 — Any transaction other than those below 1 — Cancel with Return Premium 2 — Reinstate 4 — Final Audit Factor Information Short-Rate Factor Factor defining the short-rate penalty for early cancellation of the policy. This value should be 1.0 if the cancellation is pro-rated, otherwise it should be less than 1.0 to indicate the short-rate applied under a cancellation. Factor Digits of Precision Numeric value indicating the number of digits to the right of the decimal for factors calculated within the written premium calculations. Rounding Rounding precision. This option applies to each step in the written premium calculation. You can enter a constant value or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference.

Setting Field Data Types Setting Field Data Types Save Page to .PDF Fields View To set the value of the Data Type property: Click the field. On the Value tab, in the Data Type list, click the appropriate data type. Fields and tables can hold or return the following data types. Type Description boolean True or false (1 or 0) value. Any number other than 1 returns a 1, and any string other than True or False yields an error. date Date in a YYYY-MM-DD format. datetime Date and time in a YYYY-MM-DD hh:mm:ss format. float Floating point number. May be as low as 5.0 x 10^-324 and as high as 1.7 x 10^308 and may have 15 to16 significant digits. int Integer. Whole number that may be as low as -2147483648 and as high as 2147483647 and may have 10 significant digits. string Character value. time Time in a hh:mm:ss format.

Setting Field Classes Setting Field Classes Save Page to .PDF Fields View Classes are a method used for sorting tables, fields, and groups. To set the value of the Class property: Click the field or group. On the Value tab, click Class , and then enter a name for the class.

Setting Field Annotations Setting Field Annotations Save Page to .PDF Fields View An annotation may be added to any field, page, or layout. Annotations are generally used to add descriptive information that might be used as help text if the ManuScript is used to support a product user interface. The text in documentation views is supported by the annotations. To set the Annotations property for a field: Click the appropriate field. On the Value tab, click Annotations . The Annotation and Topics dialog box appears. Enter an annotation for the field. Click OK . Annotations Buttons The Annotations toolbar contains the following buttons. Button Name Action Set Context Sets or modify the context on the selected text. Remove Context Removes the context on the selected text. Insert Field Inserts a field value. Insert Formatted Field Inserts a formatted field value. Cut Cuts the selected text to the clipboard. Copy Copies the selected text to the clipboard. Paste Pastes the text from the clipboard. Find Opens the Search dialog box to enter text to find. Find Next Finds the next occurrence of the previously entered text. Annotations Keyboard Shortcuts Use the following keyboard shortcuts to navigate within Annotations. Keyboard Shortcut Action CTRL+I Inserts a field value. CTRL+K Sets or modify the context on the selected text. CTRL+R Removes the context on the selected text. CTRL+W Inserts a formatted field value. Inserting Fields into Annotations and Messages You can add fields to the text of annotations or messages to display the value or caption of the field at execution. If the value or caption of the field changes, the annotation or message changes as well. Inserting a Field Fields View To insert a field in an annotation or message: In the Annotation and Topics dialog box or the Message Editor dialog box, click the Insert Field button . The Reference Select dialog box appears. You can also: Press CTRL+I. Click the field to insert, and then click OK . Inserting a Formatted Field Fields View To insert a formatted field in an annotation or message: In the Annotation and Topics dialog box or the Message Editor dialog box, click the Insert Formatted Field button . The Formatted Field dialog box appears. You can also: Press CTRL+W. Click Current field value or Selected Field . If you click Selected Field , enter the field name. You can enter a field name or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. In Format Specification , click the type of formatting. The options are: No formatting Caption of field Numeric format Date format Custom format If you click Numeric format or Date format , click the format type in the associated list. If you click Custom format , enter a format mask into the text box. Click OK . Formatting Embedded Fields Fields View You can embed fields into message text, which can contain any number of embedded fields. The mark-up EXAMPLE Author ® inserts into the text uses the tilde (~) character to indicate the name of the field to be replaced with its value at runtime. You can also use an optional formatting string to control the presentation of the data within the message. There are three types of format syntax: numeric, date, and character. The first character of the format indicates the type of formatting: Numeric format is indicated by a first character of # or 0. Date formatting is indicated by a first character of d, m or y. Character formatting is indicated by any other first character. Numeric Formatting Options Character formatting begins with the @ character. The edit mask string immediately follows the @ character, and consists of three fields separated by semicolons. The first part of the mask is the mask itself. The second part is the character that determines whether the literal characters of the mask are matched to characters in the Value parameter or are inserted into the Value string. The third part of the mask is the character used to represent missing characters in the mask. The following are the special characters used in the first field of the mask. Character Meaning in mask > If a greater than symbol appears in the mask, all a format characters that follow are in upper case until the end of the mask or until a < character is encountered. < If a less than symbol appears in the mask, all a format characters that follow are in lower case until the end of the mask or until a > character is encountered. <> If the less than and greater than symbols appear together in a mask, no case checking is done. This turns off the > or <, while > or < will turn it back on. (sequentially) a Requires an alphabetical character in this position, and the case is based upon the presence of one of the case indicators (>,<,<>) in the mask previous to the 'a' format character. L Requires an alphabetic character in this position. A Requires an alphanumeric character in this position. C Requires an upper-case alphabetic character in this position. c Requires a lower-case alphabetic character in this position. 0 Requires a numeric character in this position. 9 Requires a numeric character or a positive or negative sign (+ or -) in this position. # Requires a numeric character or a positive or negative sign (+ or -) in this position. - Inserts spaces into the returned string. * Requires a character in this position in a format mask. This can be any character allowed by the interface. ^ The character that follows a caret is a literal character. Use this character before any mask characters used as a literal. For example, in the mask @LL^xAA the third character of the entry must be x . Any character that does not appear in the preceding table can appear in the first part of the mask as a literal character. Literal characters are inserted automatically if the second field of the mask is 0, or matched to characters in the Value parameter if the second field is any other value. The special mask characters can also appear as literal characters if preceded by a back slash character (\). Adding a Context to an Annotation or Message Fields View To add a context to an annotation or message: In the Annotations and Topics dialog box, highlight the text to either hide or show when the ManuScript is viewed under a certain context. Click the Set Context button to open the Context Details dialog box. The text appears in the Text box of the Context Details dialog box. You can also: Press CTRL+K. In the Show Only with this context or Hide when this context lists, click the appropriate contexts. You can click the Select All button to select all the contexts or the Clear All button to clear all the context selections. (Optional) Click the Ellipsis button to the right of Show Only when condition is valid to use the Reference Select dialog box to select an appropriate boolean field. Click OK . The color of the selected text changes. You can set context colors on the Color Configuration tab of the Options dialog box. For more information, see Defining Contexts . Removing a Context from an Annotation or Message Fields View To remove a context from an annotation or message: In the Annotations or Message dialog box, highlight the text from which you wish to remove contexts. Click the Remove Context button . You can also: Press CTRL+R. The color of the selected text changes and the context is removed. You can set context colors in the Color Configuration tab of the Options dialog box.

Setting Field Comments Setting Field Comments Save Page to .PDF Fields View A Comment is information about the field that may be useful for documentation purposes. To set the value of the Comment property: Click the field. On the Value tab, click Comment , and then enter the comment text.

Setting the Always Execute Property Setting the Always Execute Property Save Page to .PDF Fields View The Always Execute property prevents a field value from being cached when it is called from another field during ManuScript execution, forcing it to be evaluated each time it is referenced. To set the value of the Always Execute property: Click the field. On the Value tab, select the Always execute check box. The Always Execute property can not be used with Sum/Accumulator fields.

Setting Field Visibility Setting Field Visibility Save Page to .PDF Fields View The visibility of a field identifies whether the field is viewable by the end user (public) or used only within the ManuScript (private). To set the visibility of a field: Click the field. On the Value tab, click Public or Private . For more information on Public and Private settings, see Visibility .

Complete the Definition pane for the value type.

Complete the Definition pane for the value type. Define the default. If a field has an Option list, you may also set the Default value for the field by clicking the desired option and then clicking Set Default .

Complete the Definition pane for the value type.

In the Maximum list, click the appropriate value type. The Maximum property is most often defined as a Constant/Field value type. For more information, see Defining Property Value Types . Complete the Definition pane for the value type.

Setting Field Miscellaneous Rules Setting Field Miscellaneous Rules Save Page to .PDF Fields View You can add customized rules to a field by adding miscellaneous rules. Each miscellaneous rule consists of a condition that evaluates to True or False , and specific values assigned to each potential value of the condition ( Then Value for True , Else Value for False ).Unique messages can be assigned to each potential value of the condition. In addition, you can define rules that allow a miscellaneous rule to execute only when data values in its condition have changed. When a field contains miscellaneous rules, the value of the field is determined by the conditions on the rules. Rules are resolved in the order they are listed in EXAMPLE Author ® . To set the value of the Miscellaneous Rules property: Click the field. On the Value tab, in the Miscellaneous Rules list, click (defined) . The Miscellaneous Rules Definition pane appears. To the right of the Details , click CB . The Condition Builder dialog box appears with the Reference Select dialog box over it. Build the conditions that define the rule. For more information on specifying a condition, see Conditional . Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Then value text box to select a field reference or value. (Optional) To include a message associated with the Then value, select the Message check box, click the Message button ,and then create the message. For more information on creating a message, see Adding Messages to Field Properties . Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Else value to select a field reference or value. (Optional) To include a message associated with the Else value, select the Message check box, click the Message button ,and then create the message. (Optional) To configure the miscellaneous rule to execute only when data values in its condition have changed: Click the Ellipsis button to the right of Store Reference Values to select a boolean field reference or enter a boolean value. When True , this rule causes the system to store the data values from the condition in the session when the rule is evaluated. These stored values are used as the point of comparison when Ignore Rule If Unchanged is True . Click the Ellipsis button to the right of Ignore Rule If Unchanged to select a boolean field reference or enter a boolean value. When True , this rule causes the system to skip the miscellaneous rule's Then Value and Else Value if the data values produced when the condition is evaluated have not changed from the last time the values were stored (the last time Store Reference Values was True ). If the conditions used for Store Reference Values or Ignore Rule If Unchanged apply to all miscellaneous rules on a field, click Apply to All Rules to apply the conditions to all miscellaneous rules on the field. (Optional) Repeat steps 3 through 9 for each additional rule. Miscellaneous Rules Toolbar Buttons The Miscellaneous Rules Definition toolbar contains the following buttons. Button Name Action Add Rule Create a new row in the list. Delete Rule Remove the selected rule from the list Duplicate Rule Duplicate the selected rule in the first open row. Move Up (CTRL+ALT+U) Move the selected rule up in the list. Move Down (CTRL+ALT+D) Move the selected rule down in the list. Find (CTRL+F) Opens the Search dialog box to enter text to find. Find Next (F3) Finds the next occurrence of the previously entered text.

Setting Field Worksheet Options Setting Field Worksheet Options Save Page to .PDF Fields View The Worksheet value specifies whether to include the field in the rating worksheet. To set the value of the Worksheet property: Click the field. In the Worksheet list, click the appropriate value type. For a field to be included in a rating worksheet, this value must be set to defined. Set any of the following properties for the field's display in the rating worksheet. Option Description Condition Builder Specifies conditions for including the field in the rating worksheet. For more information, see Conditional . Always Include Includes the field value in the worksheet data, even if the value is non-altering. Exclude Children Excludes child fields from the rating worksheet. Caption Specifies the caption to display on the rating worksheet. (Does not affect screen name display.) Field or string value. Format Mask Specifies the format mask to be applied when displaying the field on the rating worksheet. (Does not affect screen name display.) Field or string value. Summarize Controls the output of the rating worksheet. Allows user to see values of the selected factors. If the above properties are not defined, the system will use field display definition.

Enter the message. If you do not create a message, the system automatically generates a message that indicates that the default, maximum, or minimum value was used. For more information, see Inserting Fields into Annotations and Messages .

Setting Field Paths Setting Field Paths Save Page to .PDF Fields View The Data Path property is the element or attribute of the XML data document where the group is mapped or that contains the value of the field. To change or add a path to a field, the field must be public, and a schema must be defined for the ManuScript. To set the Data Path property: Click the field or group. On the Definition tab, click Path . The Path Selector dialog box appears. Click the item from the pre-determined schema to develop a path. Click OK .

Setting the Encrypt Data Property Setting the Encrypt Data Property Save Page to .PDF Fields View The Encrypt data property encrypts the value of public fields in session data. This encryption is useful in securing sensitive user-entered data. For the Encrypt data property to work correctly, a valid Encryption.config file must be specified for the EncryptionConfigPath configuration setting in the Server.config file. To enable encryption on a public field: Click the public field. On the Definition tab, select the Encrypt data check box.

Setting Field Captions Setting Field Captions Save Page to .PDF Fields View The Caption property specifies the name of the field as the user sees it. To set the value of the Caption property: Click the field or group. On the Definition tab, in the Caption list, click the appropriate value type. Captions are typically a Constant/Field value type. For more information, see Defining Property Value Types . Complete the Definition pane for the value type.

Setting Field Maximum Lengths Setting Field Maximum Lengths Save Page to .PDF Fields View The Maximum Length property specifies the maximum number of characters the field can contain. To set the value of the Maximum Length property: Click the field. On the Definition tab, in the Maximum Length list, click the appropriate value type. For more information, see Defining Property Value Types . Complete the Definition pane for the value type.

Setting Field Format Masks Setting Field Format Masks Save Page to .PDF Fields View The Format Mask property is used to provide format information for field display in EXAMPLE Express ® . To set the value of the Format Mask property: Click the field. On the Definition tab, in the Format Mask list, click the appropriate value type. The Format Mask is most often defined as a Constant/Field value type. For more information, see Defining Property Value Types . Complete the Definition pane for the value type. Format Masks Format masks apply to both user input and display output. Labeled Format Masks Labeled format masks must be supported by each installation of EXAMPLE Express ® in order to function properly. The following labeled format masks are supported by EXAMPLE Express ® out of the box. String Format Mask Output email user@domain.tld phone ###-###-#### time HH:MM:SS AM/PM (Note: seconds are optional.) time24 HH:MM:SS (military time) textarea:rows:4:width:100%: textarea:rows:4:width:100: 4 and 100 represent values for the number of rows (height) and the percentage of the window or the width in percentage or pixels. Any non-numeric characters other than the percent sign (%) entered after the width will be removed and replaced with "px" to denote that they are pixels. zipcode ##### or #####-#### ip ??#.??#.??#.??# ssn ###-##-#### hide: Always masks characters (displays an asterisk (*)) on entry and on display. An additional format mask can be added after the colon. (For example, hide:ssn.) This format mask only works with interview pages. hideOnEntry: Masks characters (displays an asterisk (*)) as they are entered. An additional format mask can be added after the colon. (For example, hideOnEntry:ssn.) This format mask only works with interview pages and does not hide characters displayed as read-only. hideOnDisplay: Always masks characters (displays an asterisk (*)) only on display, not on entry. An additional format mask can be added after the colon. (For example, hideOnDisplay:ssn.) This format mask only works with interview pages. Float Format Mask Output C n Field value with culture-appropriate (as determined in ManuScript properties) currency symbol and numeric separators, to n number of decimal places. With the base culture in a ManuScript set to en-US , the following are examples of output for the value 11.87: C0 — $12 C1 — $11.9 C2 — $11.87 The rounding that is applied when using this format mask occurs at the display level and is completely separate from any rounding defined in a ManuScript field. hide Always masks characters (displays an asterisk (*)) on entry and on display. An additional format mask can be added after the colon. (For example, hide:ssn.) This format mask only works with interview pages. hideOnEntry Masks characters (displays an asterisk (*)) as they are entered. An additional format mask can be added after the colon. (For example, hideOnEntry:ssn.) This format mask only works with interview pages and does not hide characters displayed as read-only. hideOnDisplay Always masks characters (displays an asterisk (*)) only on display, not on entry. An additional format mask can be added after the colon. (For example, hideOnDisplay:ssn.) This format mask only works with interview pages. Date Date field display (with no format mask applied) defaults to the short date format appropriate to the culture set in Web browser options. Format masks placed on Date fields will override this default. Input fields of a Date data type will assume dates are entered in the short date order appropriate to the browser settings and will accept hyphens (-), periods (.), or slashes (/) as separators. Applying format masks to date input fields can result in incorrect data collection and other errors. To avoid confusion, Duck Creek Technologies recommends indicating in the user interface which format the end user should use. Format Mask Output mm-dd-yyyy month-day-year format dd-mm-yyyy day-month-year format yyyy-mm-dd year-month-day format MMMM d, yyyy Long date format such as January 1, 2007 . d Short date format (such as 01-01-2007 ) appropriate to the culture as determined by browser settings. D Long date format (such as Monday, January 1, 2007 ) appropriate to the culture as determined by browser settings. This mask should only be applied to read-only fields. Boolean The default display for a Boolean field is a check box. Format Mask Output radio Displays a Boolean field as side-by-side Yes and No option buttons. radio:1 Displays a Boolean field as above/below Yes and No option buttons. Other (with options) The default display for defined options is a combo box. Format Mask Output radio Displays each as an option button in a single row. blankOption:(select) (select) will appear as a default in a dropdown list of options, and may be replaced in the mask with any text. Custom Format Masks There are two types of custom format syntax: numeric and character. The first character of the format indicates the type of formatting: Numeric format is indicated by a first character of # or 0. Character formatting is indicated by the @ character. The edit mask string immediately follows the @ character, and consists of three fields separated by semicolons. The first part of the mask is the mask itself. The second part is the character that determines whether the literal characters of the mask are matched to characters in the Value parameter or are inserted into the Value string. The third part of the mask is the character used to represent missing characters in the mask. The following are the special characters used in the first field of the mask. Character Meaning in mask > If a greater than symbol appears in the mask, all a format characters that follow are in upper case until the end of the mask or until a < character is encountered. < If a less than symbol appears in the mask, all a format characters that follow are in lower case until the end of the mask or until a > character is encountered. <> If the less than and greater than symbols appear together in a mask, no case checking is done. This turns off the > or <, while > or < will turn it back on. (sequentially) a Requires an alphabetical character in this position, and the case is based upon the presence of one of the case indicators (>,<,<>) in the mask previous to the 'a' format character. L Requires an alphabetic character in this position. A Requires an alphanumeric character in this position. C Requires an upper-case alphabetic character in this position. c Requires a lower-case alphabetic character in this position. 0 Allows a numeric character in this position. 9 Requires a numeric character or a positive or negative sign (+ or -) in this position. # Requires a numeric character or a positive or negative sign (+ or -) in this position. - Inserts spaces into the returned string. * Requires a character in this position in a format mask. This can be any character allowed by the interface. ^ The character that follows a caret is a literal character. Use this character before any mask characters used as a literal. For example, in the mask @LL^xAA the third character of the entry must be x . Any character that does not appear in the preceding table can appear in the first part of the mask as a literal character. Literal characters are inserted automatically if the second field of the mask is 0, or matched to characters in the Value parameter if the second field is any other value. The special mask characters can also appear as literal characters if preceded by a back slash character (\).

For more information, see Defining Property Value Types . Complete the definition pane for the value type. The result must be either 1 (for true) if the field is read only, or 0 (for false) if the field is not read only.

Setting Field Options Setting Field Options Save Page to .PDF Fields View With the Options property, you can define the valid choices for a field. To set the Options property: Click the field. On the Definition tab, in the Options list, click (defined) . In the Option Definition pane, click one of the following options in the T ype list: Standard options Key in a table Reference list Data Service Request ODBC data source (Optional) Select the Combine check box if you wish to make the value in the response be a combination of the value and the caption. Complete the Definition pane for the option type. Setting Options: Standard Fields View When you set Standard Options, you specify a list of options to be used for the field. To set standard options: In the Type list of the Option Definition pane, click Standard Options . In Value column, enter the option values to be used by the ManuScript. A Float or Int data type value must use the decimal and thousands separator appropriate to the culture defined in the ManuScript properties. In the Caption column, edit the captions to be seen by the user. (Optional) In the corresponding Valid Indicator column, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field that indicates whether or not this option should be displayed for the user. Setting Options: Key in a Table Fields View To set options based on keys in a table: In the Type list of the Option Definition pane, click Key in a table . In the Table list, click the appropriate table. In the Key list, click the table key to use. The values for the selected key are included in the grid. When setting options based on table keys, captions match the values and cannot be modified. Setting Options: Reference List Fields View To set options based on a field reference: In the Type list of the Option Definition pane, click Reference list . Click one of the following options: The value for each option is the ID of the group The value for each option is the value of field . (Optional) Click Include deleted to include groups marked for deletion. Do one of the following: If the value is the ID of the group, click the group in the list. If value is the value of the field, click the Ellipsis button to select the field from the Reference Select dialog box. (Optional) Select a field reference for The caption for each option is the value of field . (Optional) Select a field reference for Enter a validation reference . This field is used to restrict the option list. (Optional) Select a field reference for Enter a sort reference . This field is used to sort the option list. Setting Options: Data Service Fields View The data key is the primary key that is used to lookup the data set information. The last data key used to look up the Data Services is the final ID of the data key. The data set is the information that is found using a data key. A data set requires a data key. To set options based on data services: In the Type list of the Option Definition pane, click Data Service . In the Data Service list, click one of the following options: dataSet dataKey Set the data set or data key options. Setting Data Key Options To set data key options: (Optional) If the Data Service data key is the last reference, select the ID is value check box. Click View Catalog, and then click the data services from the Data Key Catalog dialog box. You can also: Enter the name of the Catalog in quotation marks in Data Name , or use a field to determine the Catalog by clicking the Ellipsis button to select the field using the Reference Select dialog box. In the Parameter Name list, enter the required parameter name. If you purchased Data Services from Duck Creek Technologies, Inc., the parameters are provided to you. In the corresponding Value list, click the Ellipsis button to select the value to pass into the parameters using the Reference Select dialog box. Setting Data Set Options To set data set options: In the Code list, enter the code for the Data Set . Setting the data set by using the Default allows you to create a list box for the user to override the data set by choosing the data set from the list. You can also: Create a table and a lookup field to determine the codes by setting the lookup as the default for the field to allow the user to override the data set. Setting Options: Request Fields View To set options based on a Request command: In the Type list of the Option Definition pane, click Request . In the Request Command list, click the appropriate command. Click Request Parameters and specify any request parameters in the Name Value Definitions dialog box. Enter the Response path to indicate which elements within the response contain the information to be displayed within the option list. You can also: Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Enter the Value item to specify the attribute within the element indicated in the response path that provides the values to be associated with each item in the option list. You can also: Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Enter the Caption item to specify the attribute or element within the element indicated in the response path that provides the text displayed in the option list during an interview. You can also: Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Setting Options: ODBC Data Source Fields View To set options based on an ODBC data source: In the Type list of the Option Definition pane, click ODBC data source . Enter the Connection . Paste Data (CTRL+V) Pastes the data from the clipboard starting at the selected cell. This menu is also available as a shortcut menu from a table data cell. Modify Data, Insert Record, and Delete Record are not available when a conditional table is selected. Increase Min Width Incrementally increases the minimum width of condition cells. Decrease Min Width Incrementally decreases the minimum width of condition cells. Creating Tables Creating Tables Save Page to .PDF In EXAMPLE Author ® , you can create three types of tables: Regular tables that contain values corresponding to table keys Conditional tables that contain values determined by specified conditions External tables that contain data not included in the ManuScript You can also import external tables or map the XML of a ManuScript to import Excel data. Creating a Regular Table Tables View To create a regular table: Click the New Table button . You can also: • On the Edit menu, click New Table . • Right-click the Tables list, and then click New Table . • Press CTRL+A. Complete the Table Properties dialog box: Enter a Name for the table. The name cannot exist as a field or group name in the current or base ManuScript. Enter the Data Names . Enter one or more items to retrieve from the table. (Optional) Enter the Class for the table. Classes are a way to select certain tables. Click OK . The Key Properties dialog box appears. Complete the Key Properties dialog: Enter a Key Name . The Key Name identifies the data to be passed into the table. In the Span list, click Row , Column , or Page . The Span refers to the way you search for data in the table. You can search down the rows, across the columns, or across pages, which appear as tabs in the display. In the Search Type list, click the appropriate search type. The Search Type defines the way to look up the data. The default method is = . Other options are > , < , >= , <= , Range Check , Interpolated , and Graduated . For more information on search types, see Key Search Types . In the Data Type list, click the appropriate data type. Data types can be Boolean , Currency , Date , Datetime , Float , Int , String , or Time . Enter the Key Values to be used in the table. These are the values that can potentially be passed into the table by the lookup field. Click OK . Enter the table data. You can enter the information directly into the table, or cut and paste it from a table in a different program, such as Microsoft Excel or Microsoft Word. You can manipulate the size of the cells in the table by dragging the cell borders. After you build a table, you must create a lookup field to access the information from the table. The group level for the table is important for conditional tables because the table checks the condition by looking for the value of the field at the level where the table is placed. Complete the Table Properties dialog box: Enter a Name for the table. The name cannot exist as a field or group name in the current ManuScript or a base ManuScript. Enter the Data Names . Enter one or more items to retrieve from the table. (Optional) Enter the Class for the table. Classes are a way to select certain tables. (Optional) Click Always execute . This causes the table to always execute, even if not called from a field. Click OK . The main Table pane displays cells in which you can define conditions. These cells default to 1, which indicates True and is used if no condition must be met to retrieve the corresponding value from the table. Edit the conditions for all condition cells. You can manipulate the size of the cells in the table by dragging the cell borders. After you build a table, you must create a lookup field to access the information from the table.
