Importing Tables from Another ManuScript Tables View To import tables from another ManuScript: On the File menu, click Import . The Import Wizard dialog box appears. Click Catalog . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. Click the ManuScript from which to import tables, and then click OK . Click Import Tables , and then click Next . The tables in the selected ManuScript are displayed in the Select the item(s) to import list. Click the tables to import, and then click Next . The tables you selected are listed. Tables that you selected in the previous display that already exist in the current ManuScript are not selected for importing. You can clear any element you do not want to import by clearing the check box. You can force the importation of an element you already have in the current ManuScript by selecting an unselected check box or by clicking Select All . Click Finish . All selected tables are imported into the ManuScript. Working with Microsoft Excel Files Tables View Excel files can be imported into EXAMPLE Author ® or they may be accessed externally at runtime. Files may be imported into EXAMPLE Author ® by importing specific spreadsheet tables, or by mapping ManuScript XML. Files may be accessed externally at runtime through a user-specified data provider connection. The following table outlines some of the benefits of each method of working with Excel files. Method Benefits XML Mapping One-time import of large amounts of data. Share existing data without creating new tables in EXAMPLE Author ® . Large risk schedules can be imported from external data sources. Importing an External Table Share existing data without creating new tables in EXAMPLE Author ® Quick access to easily specified data within a spreadsheet Data Provider Connection Real-time access to immediately referenceable and updated data. Continued use of the existing file as a data source. Quick access to data following the initial creation of a data source name. Flexibility through programmatic mapping at runtime. If the data being accessed is efficiently managed in an Excel spreadsheet, it is recommended that you create a table in EXAMPLE Author ® to manage the data more reliably. Mapping XML to Import Excel Files To map XML to import Excel files, server requests are coordinated and called from a ManuScript. Tables must be imported into fields or groups, and columns must be collected. To import tables into a field or group: Create a field named TriggerExcelData outside the repeating group to import. Create a public group named Upload as a child of the data group. Create a public group named Mapping as a child of the Upload group. Add a private group and field named UploadInput . Add a private group and field named UploadPrivate . To collect specific columns, create a public group named Mapping as a child of the Upload group. For each column of data you wish to import, create another public group with the word Map prepended to the field name. (Optional) Repeat step 1 for each column of data you wish to import. (Optional) If this group represents data that will also have a value map section to match existing dropdown options, include [@valueMap=&quot;1&quot;] as part of the path attribute. Each of these public groups has one field that describes the mapping of the field information to the column in the Excel spreadsheet using the <ExcelImport.inferValueRq> request. On the Column field, do the following: Set the Default value to be the same as the column name on the spreadsheet. Set the Caption value to be the same as the column name on the spreadsheet. In the Value list, click Request , and then add <ExcelImport.inferValueRq> request to load the column name. The response path of this request should be @column . The first parameter for this request specifies the spreadsheet file name and location. The second parameter specifies the type of data in this column of the spreadsheet. This request identifies which column of the spreadsheet to use for the import. Add an Options setting of <ExcelImport.getColumnNamesRq> request with the following values: A response path columns/column Value item @name Caption item @caption. This loads the data of the column whose name is in the Caption attribute of this field. The Default value and the Caption value of the map field must be identical, and these values should also be identical to the Caption value of the target data field. To specify what data to import from the spreadsheet, do the following: Duplicate the Map group created in step 3 of the import tables into a field or group so that there is one group per column on the spreadsheet to be imported. Make any necessary changes to the group path, Default or Caption values, and the <ExcelImport.inferValueRq> request parameters. Specify which data fields should be populated with the imported values that have been specified. Each of the data item fields must have the identical path value as the column name on the spreadsheet. On the TriggerExcelData field created in step 1, add a pre-effect field for each column field that has been added. On the page where the data is to be loaded, in the Execute field data.TriggerExcelData , set Execute ManuScript to Calculate and Accept to make the execute field available. Insert an Action and the following three requests: <ExcelImport.getcolumnnamesRq> request <ExcelImport.importvaluesRq> request <ExcelImport.importdataRq> request Add a response path identifying where to put the data to the <ExcelImport.importdataRq> request. Add the data fields specified in step f. To view examples of the XML mapped to import Excel files, click here . Importing an External Table from Excel Tables View You may create a new table by importing a table from an external database or from an Excel spreadsheet. Once imported, you can read or write data in the new table. To import an external table: In Excel, specify a Named Range for the appropriate workbook that includes that data that will be the target data for the import, and save the workbook. In EXAMPLE Author ® , on the Edit menu, click Import External Table . You can also: Right-click the Tables list, and then click Import External Table . The Table Import Wizard dialog box appears. In the Data Source Name list, click the appropriate data source name, and then click Next . If the appropriate data source is not listed, click DSN , and then browse to the desired data source. If you have not set up the file source data name, click the ODBC Administration panel link to open the ODBC Data Source Administration dialog box to set up a data source. For more information on setting up a file source data name, see Defining an ODBC File Data Source Name . In the Table View/Name list, click the table name, and then click Next . Define the new table by selecting appropriate columns in the spreadsheet to specify the table rows, columns, and data fields to import. You must select at least one row key and one data name. Click Finish . The new table appears in the Tables list. Using a Data Provider Connection Tables View The rating engine can access external database tables at runtime through ODBC, using the Microsoft ADO components. This facility is designed to allow the rating calculations to access legacy data that is not maintained within a ManuScript. The ManuScript includes a query defined for each data element needed from the external data source and retrieves the values at runtime. For the engine to access the tables through ODBC, you must define a data source on the machine that is performing the rating. The data source can be a: DSN ODBC source Connection with no DSN specifying the database file and driver to be used for the connection Using a DSN data source in the ManuScript is recommended to allow an abstraction layer between a named data source and the actual location and name of the database. To use a DSN data source, use the ODBC Data Source Administrator to setup a system or file-based DSN. The DSN connection is specified in a data source file that is created using the ODBC Data Source Administrator tool (accessible from the Windows Start menu). The default path for this tool is: Settings/Control Panel/Administrative Tools/Data Sources (ODBC). The user must specify a DSN connection, save the XML file, and then point to the saved file location using the specified DSN connection. Because the process for creating a DSN connection varies depending on the version of Windows used, click the Windows Help menu for assistance.

Table Keys Table Keys Save Page to .PDF Keys are the information a ManuScript passes into a table in order to find the appropriate data. Adding a Key Tables View Keys are used to define the structure of a table. To add a key to a table: Right-click a key within the table. Click Add Key . You can also: On the Edit menu, point to Key , and then click Add key . The Key Properties dialog box appears. Complete the Key Properties dialog: Enter a Key Name . The Key Name identifies the data to be passed into the table. In the Span list, click Row , Column , or Page . The Span refers to the way you search for data in the table. You can search down the rows, across the columns, or across pages, which appear as tabs in the display. In the Search Type list, click the appropriate search type. The Search Type defines the way you look up the data. The default method is = . Other options are > , < , >= , <= , Range Check , Interpolated , and Graduated . For more information on search types, see Key Search Types . In the Data Type list, click the appropriate data type. Data types can be Boolean , Currency , Date , Datetime , Float , Int , String , or Time . Enter the Key Values to be used in the table. Click OK . Auto-Filling Key Values with Codes Tables View You can use auto-fill to help complete the key values with the option codes from fields. To auto-fill the key values with codes: In the Key Properties dialog box, click Codes . The Reference Select dialog box appears. Click the field with the code options, and then click OK . The Code Select dialog box appears. Move the codes to use to the right column from the list of available codes on the left. Click codes and use the arrow buttons to move them between boxes. Click OK . In the Key Properties dialog box, click OK . Auto-Filling Key Values with Numbers Tables View You can use auto-fill to help complete the key values with numbers or number ranges. To auto-fill the key values with numbers: In the Key Properties dialog box, click Numbers . The Auto-Fill Numbers dialog box appears. Enter the Start number , End number , and Increment . If you wish the numbers expressed as ranges, select the Make range values (###-###) check box. If this check box is cleared, the numbers appear with only the first number of the range. Click OK . In the Key Properties dialog box, click OK . Inserting Key Values Tables View To insert key values: In the Key Properties dialog box, click the data value below the insertion point. Click Insert Value . Enter the new value. Removing Key Values Tables View To remove key values: In the Key Properties dialog box, click the data value to remove. Click Remove Value . The remaining values move up. Click OK . The Modify Data dialog box appears. Select one of the following options: Deletion Method Description Truncate data The data at the end of the columns/rows will be removed. Delete data at key change The data at the point in the column/row where the key is different will be removed. Delete data past key change The data one entry point past the point in the column/row where the key is different will be removed. Key Search Types Tables View The Search Type of each key controls how the data is returned for each lookup made into the table. When a key is being searched, each key value is compared against the search value to find a match according to the search type being used. Default Key Values Each key in a table is searched from the top down. The search checks or processes each key until a match or a terminating condition is found. If no match is found in the key list, an error occurs. To avoid this error, it is useful to set a default for each key. To mark a key as the default: In the Key Properties dialog box, click the desired key. Click Default Value . The color of the key changes to indicate it is the default. To remove a default from a key: In the Key Properties dialog box, click the desired key. Click Default Value . The color of the key changes to indicate it is no longer the default. Comparative Search Types The simplest search types are the comparative search types, = , < , > , <= and >= . The comparative types search down a column until the comparison between the key value and the search value is true. The data type of the key is important because the comparison treats the values in the key list according to data type, comparing numbers, strings, or dates. When the less-than or greater-than types are used it is important to ensure the key value order make sense as a top-down search, from the most restrictive value to the least restrictive value. Range Check Search Types The Range Check search type searches the key to find if the search value is in any part of the range specified by each key value. The Range Check search type uses the hyphen (-) and the comma (,) to specify range values. A non-range key value can use either the less-than sign (<) or greater-than sign (>) to indicate the search value can be either less than or equal to, or greater than or equal to, the key value. Any combination of comma-delimited values and range values can be entered for each key item. A hyphen indicates inclusive range (low to high) and a comma separates individual values. When both sides of a range phrase are numeric, the range is checked numerically. When non-numeric values are specified as a range, the comparison is done alphabetically. Each comma-delimited item is checked for enumeration using the same numeric or string logic. The following table provides examples of appropriate Range Check syntax. Key Value Valid Results <100 100 or less 12,16,20-30 12, 16, or any value from 20 to 30 inclusive 23-40, 50-61, >99 23 to 40, or 50 to 61, or 99 and greater Each Additional Support Some tables list amounts for the most common values that do not fit into the interpolation process and then state something such as for each additional 1000, add 5 . To support this kind of lookup, table keys need a method to indicate an each additional amount. Before the final key value in a list, place the text [eachadd] to indicate the value for an each additional phrase. An example of such a table appears below. Key Value 1-10000 10 10001-20000 22 20001-30000 35 [eachadd]5000 5 Results of lookups into this table would include: If the search value is 15000, the returned lookup value would be 22. If the search value is 41000, the returned lookup value would be the next-to-last amount (35) plus the appropriate number of each additional amounts, computed by the number of increments between the request key and the highest defined key value. In this case that would be (41000 - 30000) / 5000 which is 3 increments, so the returned value for this example would be 50 (35 + (3 * 5)). Interpolate Search Types The Interpolate search type performs interpolation and extrapolation using key values. There can only be one key in a table that has the search type of Interpolate. There can be other keys, but only one can be an Interpolate key. The key values of an Interpolate search type must be numeric and the numeric order of the keys must be in ascending order. To enable extrapolation, the last key value should be the each additional amount, and must be smaller than the previous key value. The fact that the last key value is smaller than the previous value is the indicator to the system that extrapolation is desired of higher values than the next to the last value. The system automatically adds an asterisk (*) after each additional key values as an indicator. The interpolation/extrapolation lookup works by searching the key values to find the two entries that are closest to the search value. If the two key values are one lower and one higher, then the returned lookup value is the interpolated value of the associated data in the table of these two keys. If the key is set up to extrapolate (has an each additional value) and the search value is greater than the largest key value, the returned value is the extrapolated. The following table is defined with a single key with a search type of Interpolate. Key Value 0 10 20 15 30 20 40 25 50 30 60 35 70 40 80 45 10* 5 Results of lookups into this table would include: If the search value is 34, the low key would be 30 and the high key would be 40. The interpolation between the two associated data values of 20 and 25 would result in the lookup returning 22. If the search value is 71, the low key would be 70 and the high key would be 80. The interpolation between the two associated data values of 40 and 45 would result in the lookup returning 40.5. If the search value is 102, the low key would be 80 and the high key would be 10. This example would use extrapolation to determine the value. The extrapolation would result in the lookup returning 56. Graduated Search Types The Graduated search type is a special search type used for tables whose keys have a cumulative purpose. There can only be one key in a table that has the search type of Graduated. There can be other keys, but only one can be a Graduated key. The data in a Graduated table identifies a base value as the first key value and then each ensuing key value identifies an additional amount as either a factor or additive value to apply to the base. The returned lookup value is the cumulative total of all key values that apply to the search value. The Graduated search type uses a specific syntax for each key item that defines how the key is to be processed. The rules of the key values for the Graduated type are separated into the first key value, the last key value and the interior key values: First key value — Identifies the base amount. The data associated with the first key is used as the starting value. The numeric value of the first key defines the included amount, or the first threshold amount (for instance, the first 200 dollars). The syntax of the first key value is: First IncludedValue Interior key values — Identify the graduated steps of the value. The data associated with these keys are the additive amounts that are applied to the base amount. The numeric value of the interior keys identifies the increment amount (for instance, the next 100 dollars). Key values can also be defined with an optional per amount by including a comma and the per amount. If there is no per amount, the entire data value of the key is added to the base when the search value applies. If there is a per amount specified, the data value of the key is multiplied by the fractional number of per amounts the search value requires, interpolating the per value. All interim calculations are rounded to the dollar. The syntax of interior key values is: Next IncrementAmount , PerAmount Last key value — Identifies the additive amount if the search value is greater than all the graduated steps. Like the interior values, the last key value can have an optional per amount specified, and behaves the same as the interior keys. The syntax for the last key value is: Excess IncrementAmount , PerAmount The following table is defined with a single key with a search type of Graduated. Key Value First 200 80 Next 100,25 12 Next 300,50 4 Excess, 100 5 Results of lookups into this table would include: If the search value is 125, it is less than or equal to the first amount, so the returned lookup value would be 80. If the search value is 350, the returned lookup value would be 132. This value is the base of 80, plus 48 (4 * 12) from the Next 100,25 key, plus 4 for the first 50 from the Next 300,50 key. If the search value is 650, the returned lookup value would be 155. This value is the base of 80, plus 48 (4 * 12) from the Next 100,25 key, plus 24 (6 * 4) from the Next 300,50 key, plus 3 from the fractional part of the first 100 from the Excess, 100 key.

Data Names Data Names Save Page to .PDF Tables View Data Names in the Table Properties dialog box are the column titles for the table. To add or edit Data Names: Click the table name. Click the Table Properties button . You can also: • On the Edit menu, click Properties . • Double-click the table name. • Right-click the table name, and then click Properties . Enter the new data name into the Data Names text box, or edit an old name. You may also click: Add to add additional lines for data names. Delete to delete a data name.

Selecting Tables By Class Selecting Tables By Class Save Page to .PDF Tables View You can sort and select tables by their optional property Class. To select tables by Class: On the Edit menu, click Select by Class . The Select by Class dialog box appears. In List of Tables by Class , click the appropriate table. Click OK.

Editing Tables Editing Tables Save Page to .PDF Tables can be deleted or duplicated. The data in a table can be entered, inserted, or deleted. The method of data entry can be completed first down and then across, or first across and then down. Deleting a Table Tables View To delete a table: In the Tables list, click the appropriate table. Click the Delete Table button . You can also: • Right-click the table name, and then click Delete Table . • On the Edit menu, click Delete Table . In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to confirm the deletion or No to cancel. Duplicating a Table Tables View To duplicate a table: In the Tables list, click the table. On the Edit menu, click Duplicate Table . You can also: Right-click the table name, and then click Duplicate table . The Table Properties dialog box opens. Enter a new Name for the duplicate table, and then click OK . Changing the Data Entry Method Tables View When you enter data into a table cell and press ENTER, the cursor moves to the next cell down or across based on the selected data entry method. You can enter data in tables in two different directions: Down then across Across then down To change the date entry method for a table: In the Tables list, click the table . Click the first cell in the table. Click the Enter Down button or the Enter Across button to define the data entry method. Inserting Data Records Tables View To insert a data record in a table: Click the table in the list. Click a table cell. Click the Insert Record button . The new cell is empty and the current values are moved down in the table. If the table is full, the bottom value is lost. Deleting Data Records Tables View To delete a data record in a table: Click the appropriate table cell. Click the Remove Record button . The current values are moved up in the table and the last value is blank. Modifying Data in Tables Tables View The data in tables can be modified manually through data entry or can be modified by a factor or percentage by using the Modify Data dialog box. To modify data using the Modify Data dialog: Click the name of the table. On the Edit menu, point to Data and then click Modify Data . Click Modify values by factor or Modify values by percent . Specify the factor or percentage. To specify the factor, click Divide by or Multiply by and enter the appropriate number. To specify the percentage, click Increase by or Decrease by and enter the appropriate number. In the Apply Changes to list, click all cells , the active cell , or selected cells . (Optional)To round the results, click the appropriate Round type. For more information, see Calculation Rounding Options . Click OK .

Viewing Table Inheritance Viewing Table Inheritance Save Page to .PDF Tables View You can view the ManuScript inheritance chain for tables in the current ManuScript in the Inheritance dialog box. The Inheritance (Data) dialog box displays the entire ManuScript inheritance chain for the current ManuScript's data (fields and tables). From this dialog box, you can open any ManuScript in the inheritance chain by selecting the desired ManuScript and clicking Go to . You can access the Inheritance (Data) dialog box from Tables view in two ways: On the View menu, point to Inheritance , and then click Data . On the shortcut menu for Tables View, click View Inheritance (Data) .

Detailed View — Displays all fields and results as well as additional calculation information. Output XML — Displays the XML data format that would be used by the downstream system. Testing ManuScripts Testing ManuScripts Save Page to .PDF The following sections provide instructions for working with ManuScripts in Data Tester view. Setting the Field to Access Data Tester View The Primary Value setting in the ManuScript Properties is necessary to specify the execution field for the finished ManuScript. For testing purposes, you may choose to execute an interim field. To do this, you specify the Field to execute. To set the value of the Field to execute: On the Edit menu, click Advanced Options . The Advanced Options dialog box appears. Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Field to access text box. The Reference Select dialog box appears. Click the appropriate field. (Optional) Enter a Data Path to execute a particular iteration of the selected field. Click OK . Entering Data Data Tester View All the public fields in the ManuScript appear in the Data Records list. Enter data into only the input fields that are required for testing the ManuScript. To view only the input fields: Select the Input only fields check box. To enter the data: In the Data Records pane, click the group where the input fields are located. The public fields appear in the bottom left pane. In each input field, enter the appropriate Data . Adding a Data Record Data Tester View Individual data records represent a single iteration of a group within a given data file. To add a data record: Click the Add Data Record button . You can also: • Right-click a data record, and then click Add Record. • On the Edit menu, point to Data , and then click Add Record . If there is only one public group in the current ManuScript, the record is added. If there is more than one public group to choose from, the Add New Record dialog box appears. If necessary, select a group from the dialog box: Click the group for the new data record. Click OK . The number of data records added cannot exceed the number set in the Maximum property in the Group properties in the Fields view. You can enter the value "*" (with the quotes) into the Maximum property to indicate an unlimited number of records. Duplicating a Data Record Data Tester View Individual data records represent a single iteration of a group within a given data file. When data is duplicated, all the items within the records are duplicated. To duplicate a data record: Click the record. Click the Duplicate Data Record button . You can also: • Right-click the data record, and then click Duplicate Record. • On the Edit menu, point to Data , and then click Duplicate Record . Deleting a Data Record - Data Tester View Individual data records represent a single iteration of a group within a given data file. To delete a data record: Click the data record to be deleted. Click the Delete Data Record button . You can also: • Right-click the data record, and then click Delete Record . • On the Edit menu, point to Data , and then click Delete Record . Sorting Data Records Data Tester View You can sort the fields in the Data Records List. To sort the fields alphabetically: Select the Sort by Field Name check box. Executing a ManuScript Data Tester View When you execute a ManuScript, data entered into the data records is used in the calculations. To execute a ManuScript: Click the Execute button . You can also: • Press F9. • On the Edit menu, click Execute . The output data is displayed in different formats on three tabs: Results Detailed View Output XML If an error message appears indicating that there is no field to execute defined, you must designate a field. For more information, see Setting the Field to Access . Viewing Errors and Messages Data Tester View When an error occurs during execution, the Error dialog box displays a detailed message. To see where an error occurred in a calculation: Click the Find Bug button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click Find Bug . The line containing the calculation in error appears in the Detailed View. To reopen the most recent error message: On the Edit menu, click View Error . You can also: Press CTRL+E. The View Messages dialog box displays any messages generated by executing a ManuScript. To view messages: On the Edit menu, click View Messages . You can also: Press CTRL+M. Printing the Calculation Data Tester View To print the results of the execution: Right-click any field in the Results View, and then click Print . The Print dialog box appears. Click Print . Exporting the Calculation Data Tester View You can export the results of a calculation as a .txt file that can then be opened in a text editor for further review. To export the results as a text file: Right-click any field in the Results View, and then click Export . The Export Debug Log dialog box appears. Browse to a location to save the file. Enter a File name , and then click OK .

The Data File Catalog The Data File Catalog Save Page to .PDF Data Tester View The Data File Catalog is an XML file that contains pointers and other attributes that specify the system location of the data files that you created directly, linked, or imported into the Catalog. To open the Data File Catalog: Click the Open Data File button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click Open Data . The Datafile Catalog dialog box appears. From this point, you can perform a variety of tasks including: Linking a Data File into the Catalog Importing a Data File into the Catalog Exporting a Data File Removing a Data File from the Catalog Catalog Buttons and Keyboard Shortcuts Use the following keyboard shortcuts and buttons to control a Manuscript Catalog: Button Name Action Link Links a file to this Catalog item. Import Imports a file into this Catalog. Export Exports a copy of selected item to file. Remove Removes selected item from Catalog. Properties View properties of the selected item. Dependencies View dependencies for selected item. (Available only in the ManuScript Catalog) New Version Makes a new version of the selected item. (Available only in the ManuScript Catalog) Duplicate Saves as (duplicates) the selected item. This item is not linked to the original item. Columns Switches between showing all columns and showing default columns. Catalog Keys The default and required Catalog keys are lob (line of business) and state . For organizational purposes, you can further subdivide ManuScripts or data files by adding additional keys. You do this in the Catalog dialog box. You can also remove keys you have added. Adding Catalog Keys Catalog keys help to organize your files. The default keys are lob (Line of Business) and state . To add a catalog key: In the Catalog dialog box, click Add Key . The New Key Name dialog box appears. Enter the new key name, and then click OK . Deleting Catalog Keys The default keys, lob (line of business) and state cannot be removed, but additional keys you have created can be deleted. To delete a Catalog key: In the Catalog dialog box, click the key to delete. Click Delete Key . The Confirm dialog box appears. Click Yes to delete the key, or No to cancel. Filtering the Catalog Keys are useful for filtering a Catalog to display only applicable items. To filter a Catalog: For each Key Name , click the appropriate Restriction in the drop-down list. The list of items now shows only those files that match the key value you chose. You can filter by any combination of key values. Data Files Data files contain the simulated input used to test ManuScripts in Data Tester. Creating a New Data File Data Tester View Data Tester works by using data stored in data files. To create a new data file: Click the Create New Data File button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click New Data . Opening an Existing Data File Data Tester View To open an existing data file: Click the Open Data File button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click Open Data . The Datafile Catalog dialog box appears. Click the data file to load, and then click OK . Opening a Session File Data Tester View You may wish to open a session file directly from an EXAMPLE Server ® . This loads session properties as well as session data, ensuring a complete and accurate rating. To load a session file: On the Edit menu, click the Load Session File . The Session Select dialog box appears. Click the session file to load, and then click OK . Saving a Data File Data Tester View To save an existing data file: Click the Save Data File button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click Save Data . To save an existing data file with a new file name: On the Edit menu, click Save Data As . The Data File Catalog Detail dialog box appears. Enter the Description . The default and required Catalog keys are lob (Line of Business) and state . You can further subdivide the data files for organizational purposes by adding additional keys. Click the data file to save, and then click OK . To save a new data file: Click the Save Data File button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click Save Data or Save Data As . The Data File Catalog Detail dialog box appears. Enter the Description . The default and required Catalog keys are lob (Line of Business) and state . You can further subdivide the data files for organizational purposes by adding additional keys. Click the data file to save, and then click OK . Linking a Data File Data Tester View A link to a Data File is similar to a shortcut icon on your desktop. Selecting the link opens the Data File from its original location. To store a copy of the Data File reside in the Data File Catalog directory, you must import the item. To link a Data File into the Catalog: Click the Open Data File button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click Open Data . The Data File Catalog dialog box appears. In the Data File Catalog dialog box, click the Link button . The Link Data File dialog box appears. Browse to and then click the Data File. Click Open . Importing a Data File Data Tester View Importing a Data File copies the file into both the Catalog and the appropriate directory, as defined by the selected keys. To import a Data File into the Catalog: Click the Open Data File button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click Open Data . The Data File Catalog Dialog box appears. In the Data File Catalog dialog box, click the Import button . The Import Data File dialog box appears. Browse to and then click the Data File. (Optional) Select the Open as read-only check box to prohibit changes to the Data File. Click Open . Exporting a Data File Data Tester View You can export a Data File to copy the file from the Catalog directory to the directory of your choice. To export a Data File: Click the Open Data File button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click Open Data . The Data File Catalog dialog box appears. In the Data File Catalog dialog box, click the item to export. Click the Export button . The Export Data File dialog box appears. Click the appropriate directory. Enter a name for the file in the File name text box, and then click OK . Removing a Data File Data Tester View You can remove a Data File from the Catalog two different ways: If the file is linked into the Catalog, remove the link to the file. If the file is imported or is one that was created in the Catalog structure, delete the actual file from the Catalog and from the folder on the computer. To determine if the file is linked or imported, check the Source in the Data File Catalog Detail dialog box. If it is stored somewhere besides the Catalog path, it is a linked file. Click the Open Data File button . You can also: On the Edit menu, click Open Data . The Data File Catalog dialog box appears. In the Data File Catalog dialog box, click the file to remove. Click the Delete button . In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to confirm the removal or No to cancel.

Page Designer Page Designer Save Section to .PDF Save Page to .PDF You use Page Designer to define the layout of input screens and documentation. Each page designed in Page Designer can be accessed through calls to the EXAMPLE Server ® to: Use in an interview process Render documentation about the ManuScript Page Designer defines the information necessary to convert ManuScripts into a series of interview Web pages. When creating pages in Page Designer, you can build them as Version 1 pages or as Version 2 pages. The page version you choose depends upon the skin that will render the pages in EXAMPLE Express ® . When rendering pages in skins based on Duck Creek Technologies, Inc. DemoNational, you must use Version 1 pages. When rendering pages in skins based on the Next Generation Web Design, you can use Version 1 or Version 2 pages (Version 2 pages are recommended). You can convert Version 1 pages to Version 2 pages at any time using EXAMPLE Author ® 's Page Converter tool. The conversion process, however, is a one-way process. To open the Page Designer view: Click the Page Designer button . You can also: On the View menu, click Page Designer . When you double-click a field in Page Designer, the Fields view opens with that field selected. Options are: Main Interview PageSet Linked Interview PageSet Printable Pages Documentation Other Stylesheet XSLT file used to transform the page. Show PageSet Condition indicating whether the PageSet is displayed. Currently, EXAMPLE Express ® does not support this feature. Rule Set The Rule Set property specifies a special set of rules (defined in EXAMPLE Express® skins) to apply to all pages in a PageSet. These rules can control the display or behavior of these pages. Out of the box the following rule set is available: topNav — Changes the default left-navigation layout to a top-navigation layout. Setting the PageSet Name Page Designer View The PageSet Name property specifies the name of the PageSet as the user sees it. To set the value of the PageSet Name property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click PageSet Name . Enter the name. The PageSet name changes in the Page Designer list. Setting the PageSet Caption Page Designer View The Caption property specifies the name of the PageSet as the user sees it. To set the value of the Caption property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Caption . Enter the caption text. Setting the PageSet Purpose Page Designer View The Purpose property specifies the purpose of the PageSet. This property is important to EXAMPLE Express ® and to other applications using EXAMPLE Platform ® page requests to render interview pages. It allows you to categorize the PageSet in such a way that the downstream system can reference the correct pages based on the context of the application. For example, when you start a new quote, EXAMPLE Express ® uses the "Main Interview PageSet." To render an on-line product manual, however, EXAMPLE Express ® uses the appropriate "Documentation" PageSet. To set the value of the Purpose property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Purpose . In the Purpose list, click the appropriate purpose. The Purpose property includes the following options. Purpose Description Main Interview PageSet Defaults interview pages when a new quote or policy is started or a stored quote or policy is loaded in EXAMPLE Express ® . Linked Interview PageSet Pages to be referenced as a hyperlink from a Main Interview PageSet. Printable Pages Pages associated with an actualprintable form. When using the DCT forms package, link these pages with the appropriate forms in the Forms Mapper view. Other implementations may link them directly to a printable HTML or XSL file. Documentation Pages associated with an XSLT style sheet that can be used to republish the ManuScript information into a readable or printable documentation set such as an online manual which has linked values to tables, algorithms, annotations, and so on. Other Miscellaneous category for customers who design pages that can be referenced in a fashion different from those already specified. Setting the PageSet Stylesheet Page Designer View The Stylesheet property specifies the XSLT file used to transform the page. To set the value of the Stylesheet property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Stylesheet . Enter the style sheet. Setting the Show PageSet Property Page Designer View This setting is included for use in third-party downstream systems and is not supported by EXAMPLE Express ® . The Show PageSet property specifies a condition indicating whether the PageSet is displayed. To set the value of the Show PageSet property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Show PageSet . To the right of the Show PageSet text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. Setting the Rule Set Property Page Designer View The Rule Set property specifies the name of the rule set used to style pages in the PageSet. These rules can control the display or behavior of these pages. Out of the box the following rule set is available: topNav — Changes the default left-navigation layout to a top-navigation layout. To set the value of the Rule Set property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Rule Set . In the Rule text box, enter the name of the rule set you wish to use.

Adding a Page Page Designer View To add a version 2 page: Click the PageSet under which to add a page. Click the Add Page button . The Add Page dialog box appears. In the Page Version list, click 2.0 . (Optional) If the page is a meta page, select the Meta Page check box. Meta pages are processed after other Page Designer pages. Enter the name of the new page. The <header> option valid for version 1 pages is not valid for version 2 pages. To create a header page in version 2 pages, select the Meta Page check box for the desired page (Step 4, above), and then define the Position property for the page as desired. (Optional) Select the Referenced Page check box. To the right of the ManuScript text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. The ManuScript defaults to the current ManuScript. Select the appropriate PageSet and Page : If the ManuScript is the current ManuScript — Click the PageSet and page to reference. If the ManuScript is specified — Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field containing the name of the PageSet and page to reference. Click OK . The Select Group Association dialog box appears. Click the group to use for the page, and then click OK . The system adds the group and a corresponding section. Defining Page Properties Page Designer View To define a page, specify the following properties. Property Description Basic Page Name Name of the page. Page Version Version of the page. ManuScript Reference (referenced pages only) Specification of the ManuScript containing the referenced page. (If blank, this property defaults to the current ManuScript.) Page Set Reference (referenced pages only) Specification of the PageSet containing the referenced page. Page Reference (referenced pages only) Specification of the referenced page. Caption Name of the page as the user sees it. Position (meta pages only) Specification of where the meta page content should appear. This property appears only for pages designated as meta pages. Show Page Indication of whether the page should be visible. Read-Only Content Indication of whether the page is read-only. This page-level property overrides the field-level Read Only property. Execute ManuScript Indication of whether the ManuScript calculates values before the page is displayed. Options are: No — Do not execute the ManuScript. Calculate — Calculate the field specified as the primary value and display the result, but do not update the session. Calculate and Accept — Calculate the field specified as the primary value and display the result, and update the session. Execute Field Indication of the field to execute. This property appears only when the Execute Manuscript property is set to Calculate or Calculate and Accept . Allow Calculation Boolean value that specifies whether or not to run Calculate or Calculate and Accept for a specified field. This property appears only when Execute Manuscript is set to Calculate or Calculate and Accept . Include Worksheet Data Indication of whether to enable rating worksheet functionality on the page. This property appears only when the Execute ManuScript property is set to Calculate or Calculate and Accept . Include Debug Boolean value that specifies whether the page response should include rating debug information. Advanced Hide Header Indication of whether the meta page should display on this page. Hide Annotations Indication of whether annotation links should display on this page. Show Rating Messages Indication of whether rating messages should display on this page. Display As Popup Indication of whether to display the page as a pop-up, with the appropriate buttons and pop-up window elements. Navigation Class Indication of the navigation class to associate with the page. The referenced field must return one of the following values: Complete — All required fields on the page are complete. Incomplete — Required fields on the page are not complete. Caution — All required fields on the page are complete, but verification is recommended before proceeding. Depending upon skin customizations, other values may be valid for this property. Hover Help Indication to display annotations when the cursor hovers over a field instead of linking the field caption to the annotation. Next Button Caption Caption to replace "Next" on the Next button. Entering "" prevents the button from displaying. Next Button Type Type of custom button to be used for the Next button. If left blank, the green Avant Garde button type will be used. The Next Button Type property must be set for each page whose Next button you wish to customize. Previous Button Caption Caption to replace "Previous" on the Previous button. Entering "" prevents the button from displaying. Previous Button Type Type of custom button to be used for the Previous button. If left blank, the green Avant Garde button type will be used. The Previous Button Type property must be set for each page whose Previous button you wish to customize. Style Page Style CSS styles to apply to the page content. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. You can also add annotations to pages. Converting a Page (from Version 1) Converting a Page (from Version 1) Save Page to .PDF The Page Converter converts Version 1 pages to Version 2 pages. With the Page Converter, you can convert a single page in a ManuScript or all pages local to a ManuScript. The conversion process performed by the Page Converter tool is final. Version 2 pages cannot be converted back to Version 1. Back up ManuScripts before converting pages to Version 2. Only pages local to the current ManuScript are converted. During conversion, the Page Converter performs the following actions: Converts Version 1 page elements (such as layouts) to Version 2 page elements (such as sections). When converting pages, all layouts are converted to sections. Converts any layout with the Field Caption Display property defined as "First Iteration" to a section with a Field Caption Display property defined as "All". Because sections cannot contain iterations, the First Iteration option for the Field Caption Display property is not valid for sections. Applies all custom parameters defined in Version 1 pages to Version 2 pages. Any custom parameter defined in Version 1 pages with an equivalent advanced property in Version 2 pages is converted to that advanced property. Any custom parameter that does not have an equivalent advanced property in Version 2 pages is added to the Version 2 page as a Custom Property . Custom properties appear below all other properties. All custom properties are italicized. For a list of Version 1 custom parameters supported in Version 2, see Conversion Support for Custom Parameters . When a custom parameter from a version 1 page is added as a custom property to a version 2 page during conversion, you should evaluate whether any of the standard properties for version 2 pages already accomplish what is specified in the custom property. If the standard properties do not accomplish what is needed, you must customize your skin to accommodate the custom property. After page conversion, you can convert any desired sections to different elements (tables or grid widgets) and modify any desired properties. You can also remove any nested sections converted from a Version 1 page that are unnecessary in Version 2. To open the Page Converter: On the Tools menu, click Page Converter . Converting a Single Page Converting a single page from Version 1 to Version 2 requires you to: Convert the page using the Page Converter tool. Remove any unnecessary page elements (such as nested sections) carried over during conversion. Modify any desired page elements or properties that were altered upon conversion. When converting pages from Version 1 to Version 2, the system automatically converts all layouts to sections. If you wish some layouts to become tables or grid widgets, you must manually convert these sections to tables or grid widgets after page conversion. Converting the Page To convert the page using the Page Converter tool: On the Tools menu, click Page Converter . The Page Converter dialog box appears. Click the Convert a single page option. Click the PageSet containing the page you wish to convert, and then click the Page you wish to convert. Click Continue . A warning message appears. To continue with the conversion process, select the Yes, I understand check box, and then click Continue . A process dialog box appears, indicating the progress of the conversion. When complete, a success confirmation dialog box appears. In the success confirmation dialog, click OK . The page will appear as a Version 2 page in the Page Designer list. Click Save to save conversion changes. You must save changes before exiting EXAMPLE Author ® to retain conversion changes. Removing Unnecessary Page Elements To remove any unnecessary page elements (such as nested sections) carried over during conversion: In a section containing unnecessary nested sections, move fields and actions to the highest section in the hierarchy of nested sections. Delete all empty child sections. Modifying Page Elements and Properties To modify any desired page elements or properties that were altered during conversion: (Optional) Convert any desired sections to different elements (tables or grid widgets). (Optional) Modify any desired properties. Because sections do not support iterations, all layouts with a Field Caption Display property set to First Iteration are converted to sections with a Field Caption Display property set to All . To change this property, modify the property for the section, or convert the section to a table or grid widget and adjust the Field Caption Display property. Converting All Pages in a ManuScript The Page Converter only converts pages that are local to the current ManuScript. Converting all local pages in a ManuScript from Version 1 to Version 2 requires you to: Convert the pages using the Page Converter tool. Remove any unnecessary page elements (such as nested sections) carried over during conversion. Modify any desired page elements or properties that were altered upon conversion. When converting pages from Version 1 to Version 2, the system automatically converts all layouts to sections. If you wish some layouts to become tables or grid widgets, you must manually convert these sections to tables or grid widgets after page conversion. Converting Pages To convert the pages using the Page Converter tool: On the Tools menu, click Page Converter . The Page Converter dialog box appears. Click the Convert all pages defined in this ManuScript option, and then click Continue . A warning message appears. To continue with the conversion process, select the Yes, I understand check box, and then click Continue . A process dialog box appears, indicating the progress of the conversion. When complete, a success confirmation dialog box appears. In the success confirmation dialog, click OK . The pages will appear as Version 2 pages in the Page Designer list. Click Save to save conversion changes. You must save changes before exiting EXAMPLE Author ® to retain conversion changes. Removing Unnecessary Page Elements To remove any unnecessary page elements (such as nested sections) carried over during conversion: In a section containing unnecessary nested sections, move fields and actions to the highest section in the hierarchy of nested sections. Delete all empty child sections. Modifying Page Elements and Properties To modify any desired page elements or properties that were altered during conversion: (Optional) Convert any desired sections to different elements (tables or grid widgets). (Optional) Modify any desired properties. Because sections do not support iterations, all layouts with a Field Caption Display property set to First Iteration are converted to sections with a Field Caption Display property set to All . To change this property, modify the property for the section, or convert the section to a table or grid widget and adjust the Field Caption Display property. Conversion Support for Custom Parameters During conversion to Version 2 pages, the Page Converter supports all custom parameters defined in Version 1 pages by: Converting any custom parameters supported by Version 2 pages to the equivalent advanced properties Adding any custom parameters not supported by Version 2 pages (that is, any parameters that do not have equivalent advanced properties) as Custom Properties . Custom properties appear below all other properties. When a custom parameter from a version 1 page is added as a custom property to a version 2 page during conversion, you should evaluate whether any of the standard properties for version 2 pages already accomplish what is specified in the custom property. If the standard properties do not accomplish what is needed, you must customize your skin to accommodate the custom property. The following custom parameters in Version 1 pages are supported by the following equivalent advanced properties in Version 2 pages. Custom Parameter (version 1 pages) Advanced Property (version 2 pages) Pages noHeader Hide Header calculate Show Rating Messages nextButton Next Button Caption previousButton Previous Button Caption Fields readOnly Read Only eliminate Hide Field removeZero Clear to Zero Actions popUp PopUp popUpWidth PopUp Width popUpHeight PopUp Height processingMsg Show Processing Message confirm Confirm confirmText Confirm Text preEventField PreEvent Field preEventFieldContext PreEvent Context preEventFieldConfirm PreEvent Confirm preEventFieldConfirmText preEvent Confirm Text preEventFieldAlertOnly PreEvent Field Alert Only ignoreValidation Ignore Validation PDFPage Print Action skinCommand Custom Command printJob Print Job printJobManuScript Print Job ManuScript

Page Groups Page Groups Save Section to .PDF Save Page to .PDF Page groups are groups in a ManuScript referenced by the page. You can add or remove page groups and define page group properties. Adding a Page Group Page Designer View You can add groups to pages, sections, or tables. When you create a page and assign a group to that page, the system automatically places a section under that group. When you add any other groups to a page, the system automatically places a table under the added group. To add a page group: Click the appropriate page, section, or table. Click the Add Group button . The Select Group Association dialog box appears. You can also: Press CTRL+G. Click the appropriate group, and then click OK . The system adds the group and a corresponding table. Define the properties for the group. Deleting a Page Group Page Designer View To delete a page group: Click the group you wish to delete. Click the Delete Item button . Click Yes to confirm the deletion. Defining Page Group Properties Page Designer View To define a page group, specify the following properties. Property Description Basic Group Group selected from the ManuScript. Maximum Iterations Maximum number of iterations that can be displayed at any one time on the page. Link Iterations Number of action links to display the page. Relational Field Specification of a related field (in a related group) to display for each iteration. (The value of the field specified should be the foreign key in the related group.) Include Deleted Indication of whether records marked for deletion should be included in processing. Restrict Where Specification of conditions under which records should not display. Restrict Xpath Specification of xpath restrictions. Iterations with the specified xpaths do not display. To enable searching through large schedules containing significant numbers of items, you can add an XPath function. For information about XPath functions, see XPath Functions . Order By Field by which to sort iterations. Advanced None Style None In addition to defining the above properties, you can also add actions to groups. For information on adding actions, see Actions . Setting the Group Property Page Designer View The Group property specifies the referenced ManuScript group. To set the value of the Group property: Click the group. On the Definition tab, click Group . In the Group list, click the appropriate group. An asterisk indicates an unlimited number of iterations. To the right of the Relational Field text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. To the right of the Restrict Where text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. XPath Functions XPath Functions Save Page to .PDF To allow users to search through large schedules containing significant numbers of items, XPath functions can be added to the Restrict XPath property in Pages View in EXAMPLE Author ® . The following table lists the XPath functions available, the parameters that may be used with each of them, and the result each one produces. Function Parameters Returns dcfn:contains(haystack, needle) haystack (string) – Item to be searched. needle (string) – Item to search for. True if needle is found in haystack; otherwise returns false . Comparison is not case-sensitive. dcfn:starts-with(haystack, needle) haystack (string) – Item to be searched. needle (string) – Item to search for. True if haystack starts with needle; otherwise returns false . Comparison is not case-sensitive. dcfn:string-compare(string1, string2) dcfn:string-compare(string1, string2, culture) dcfn:string-compare(string1, string2, culture, case) string1, string2 (string) – Two strings to compare culture (string) – A culture string (for example, en-US). If this parameter is not specified or is an empty string, the invariant culture is used. case (string) – String used to specify whether a comparison should be case-sensitive or not (i=case-sensitive, u=not case-sensitive). If this parameter is not specified or is an empty string, the comparison will not be case-sensitive. Less than 0 if string1 is less than string2. Zero if string1 is equal to string2 Greater than 0 if string1 is greater than string2. dcfn:xml-decode(target) target (string) – A string that may contain one or more XML escape sequences. Target with XML escape sequences converted to the actual special characters (for example, "dcfn:xml-decode('O&apos;Mally')" returns "O'mally"). dcfn:xml-encode(target) target (string) – A string that may contain one or more special characters. Target with XML special characters converted to the escape sequences (for example, "dfcn:xml-encode('a "better" way')" "returns "a &quot;better&quot; way"). The following special characters are available for the dcfn:xml-decode and dcfn:xml-encode functions. Escape Sequence Special Character &apos; ' &quot; " &lt; < &gt; > All XPath parameter functions must be true strings (for example, dcfn:xml-decode(@name) will not work). Instead, the function must be converted to a string (for example, dcfn:xml-decode(string(@name))).

Sections Sections Save Section to .PDF Save Page to .PDF Sections are parts of a page used as containers for fields, actions, tables, widgets, or other sections. You can add, duplicate, and remove sections, as well as define section properties and convert sections to tables or grids widgets. Sections can be nested within other sections or within tables. Sections should not be used to display iterative data. When displaying iterative data, use tables or grid widgets. Adding a Section Page Designer View To add a section: Click the section or table under which to add another section. On the Edit menu, click Add Section . You can also: Right-click a section or table, and then click Add Section . Duplicating a Section Page Designer View To duplicate a section: Click the section you wish to duplicate. On the Edit menu, click Duplicate . You can also: Right-click the section, and then click Duplicate . The Duplicate Section dialog box appears. Click the type of action to take. Options are: Duplicate section structure only Duplicate section structure and fields Duplicate section structure and replace fields If you choose to duplicate the section structure and replace fields, define Field Replacement Rules . Click a group for Replace fields from the group . Click a group for With similar field in the group . Click OK . Deleting a Section Page Designer View To delete a section: Select the section you wish to delete. Click the Delete button . In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to confirm the deletion, or No to cancel. Converting a Section For instructions on converting a section to a table or grid widget, see Converting Sections . Defining Section Properties Page Designer View To define a section, specify the following properties. Property Description General Section Caption Text to describe the section. Layout Direction Direction the section content is laid out on the page. Field Caption Position Specification of where to display field captions. Options vary depending upon the Layout Direction and Field Caption Display selected. Field Caption Display Specification of when to display field captions. Show Condition Specification of when to display the section. Read-Only Content Specification of whether section content should be read-only. Advanced Collapsible Indication of whether the section is collapsible. When True , additional properties appear: Start Closed — Indicates whether to default the section to closed. (True/False) Toggle Bar Caption — Specifies the caption for the toggle bar. If blank, the system uses the section caption. Expand Event Specification of a field to execute when the section is expanded. This property is only applicable when the section is in an accordion section. Static Content Type Specification of the method to use for embedding external content in EXAMPLE Express ® . Options include: HTML iFrame Sub-parameters: Content URL – Indicates the URL where the external content you wish to embed resides. Content Title – Indicates whether a title bar containing the specified string will appear in the title bar at the top of the panel. Content Border – Indicates whether to display or hide the border. Content Frame – Indicates whether to display or hide the frame. Content Collapsible – Indicates whether the content should be collabsible. Content Scrollable – Indicates whether the content should be scrollable. Content Height – Indicates the desired height of the panel. Content Name – Applies a name to an iFrame, if applicable. Display Section Style CSS styles to apply to the section content. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Section Caption Style CSS styles to apply to the section caption. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Field Caption Style CSS styles to apply to the captions of fields in the section. Styles do not apply to fields in child sections, tables, or grid widgets. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Field Style CSS styles to apply to read-only fields in the section. Styles do not apply to fields in child sections, tables, or grid widgets. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Action Style CSS styles to apply to the actions in the section. Styles do not apply to actions in child sections, tables, or grid widgets. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Custom Container Custom style to apply to a container. Sub-parameters: panelHeader – Custom parameter that creates a title for a custom container. alignHeader – Custom parameter that center or right-aligns a container title. If not specified, the container aligns to the left. Icon Image Allows an icon to be placed in the section heading. Icons only appear if an associated section caption is present. In addition to defining the above properties, you can also define annotations for sections, and you can add actions to sections. For information on adding actions, see Actions . Setting the Section Caption Property Page Designer View The Section Caption property contains text to describe the section. To set the value of the Section Caption property: Click the section. On the Definition tab, click Section Caption . Enter the section caption. The caption is added to the selected section in the Page Designer list. Setting the Layout Direction Property Page Designer View The Layout Direction property specifies the direction the section content is laid out on the page. The direction can be Across or Down. To set the value of the Layout Direction property: Click the section. On the Definition tab, click Layout Direction . In the Layout Direction list, click Down or Across . Converting Sections Converting Sections Save Page to .PDF If desired, you can convert a section to a table or a grid widget. Converting Sections to Tables Page Designer View You can convert a section to a table at any time. When you convert a section to a table, the system applies all applicable properties defined for the section to the new table as they were defined in the section. To convert a section to a table: Click the section you wish to convert. On the Edit menu, click Convert To Table . The selected section appears as a table in the Page Designer list. You can also: Right-click the section, and then click Convert To Table . Converting Sections to Grid Widgets Page Designer View You can convert a section to a grid widget if the section does not contain any child sections, tables, or widgets. When you convert a section to a grid widget, the system forces the following properties to have the following values: Layout Direction — Across Field Caption Position — Above Field Field Caption Display — First Iteration Read-Only Content — True The above properties do not appear in the Properties list for editing. The system applies all other applicable properties defined in the section to the new grid widget as they were defined in the section. To convert a section to a grid widget: Click the section you wish to convert. On the Edit menu, click Convert To Grid Widget . The selected section appears as a grid widget in the Page Designer list. You can also: Right-click the section, and then click Convert To Grid Widget .

Converting Tables Converting Tables Save Page to .PDF If desired, you can convert a table to a section or a grid widget. Converting Tables to Sections Page Designer View You can convert a table to a section at any time. When you convert a table to a section, the system applies all applicable properties defined for the table to the new section as they were defined in the table, with one exception. If the Field Caption Display property for the table is set to First Iteration , the system will reset the value of this property to All upon conversion. (The First Iteration option for the Field Caption Display property is not valid for sections.) To convert a table to a section: Click the table you wish to convert. On the Edit menu, click Convert To Section . The selected table appears as a section in the Page Designer list. You can also: Right-click the table, and then click Convert To Section . Converting Tables to Grid Widgets Page Designer View You can convert a table to a grid widget if the table does not contain any child sections, tables, or widgets. When you convert a table to a grid widget, the system forces the following properties to have the following values: Layout Direction — Across Field Caption Position — Above Field Field Caption Display — First Iteration Read-Only Content — True The above properties do not appear in the Properties list for editing. The system applies all other applicable properties defined in the table to the new grid widget as they were defined in the table. To convert a table to a grid widget: Click the table you wish to convert. On the Edit menu, click Convert To Grid Widget . The selected table appears as a grid widget in the Page Designer list. You can also: Right-click the table, and then click Convert To Grid Widget .

Accordion Widgets Accordion Widgets Save Page to .PDF An accordion widget is a collection of collapsed sections stacked in a vertical unit. While each section may expand and collapse, only one section may be expanded at a time. You can add and remove accordion widgets, as well as define accordion widget properties. Accordion widgets can be nested within sections or tables. Adding an Accordion Widget Page Designer View To add an accordion widget: Click the section or table under which to add an accordion widget. Display Accordion Caption Text to display on the accordion widget title bar. Show Condition Specification of when to display the accordion widget. Height Height of the accordion widget (in pixels), if different from the default height. The value of this property must be an integer. Width Width of the accordion widget (in pixels), if different from the default width. The value of this property must be an integer. Accordion Style CSS styles to apply to the accordion widget. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Setting the Name Property Page Designer View The Name property contains the name of the accordion widget. This name appears on the accordion widget in the Page Designer list. To set the value of the Name property: Click the accordion widget. On the Definition tab, click Name . Enter the accordion widget name. Setting the Accordion Caption Property Page Designer View The Accordion Caption property contains the text to display on the accordion widget title bar. To set the value of the Accordion Caption property: Click the accordion widget. On the Definition tab, click Accordion Caption . Enter the accordion widget caption. To the right of the Show Condition text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. Setting the Height Property Page Designer View The Height property specifies the height of the accordion widget (in pixels), if different from the default height. The value of this property must be an integer. To set the value of the Height property: Click the accordion widget. On the Definition tab, click Height . Enter the desired height, in pixels. Setting the Width Property Page Designer View The Width property specifies the width of the accordion widget (in pixels), if different from the default width. The value of this property must be an integer. To set the value of the Width property: Click the accordion widget. On the Definition tab, click Width . Enter the desired width, in pixels.
