Comparison Widgets Comparison Widgets Save Page to .PDF Comparison widgets provide a way to display option lists so that the impact of each option on the final premium is visible to the EXAMPLE Express ® user. Comparison widgets can be used in many different contexts and can be easily configured to display in different ways. You can add and remove comparison widgets, as well as define comparison widget properties. Comparison widgets can be nested within sections or tables. Adding a Comparison Widget Page Designer View To add a comparison widget: Click the section or table under which to add a comparison widget. On the Edit menu, click Advanced , and then click Add Comparison Widget . You can also: Right-click a section or table, click Advanced , and then click Add Comparison Widget . Deleting a Comparison Widget Page Designer View To delete a comparison widget: Select the comparison widget you wish to delete. Click the Delete button . In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to confirm the deletion, or No to cancel. Defining Comparison Widget Properties Page Designer View To define a comparison widget, specify the following properties. Property Description General Name Name of the comparison widget. Data Option List Field Field containing the options to render in the comparison widget. This field can be any field with a defined option list. If using the Slider Bar display type, specify a default option for the field specified for this property. If no default is specified, the slider bar may initially appear to have an option selected, but no value will be set in the session. Affected Value Field Field affected by the option selected. This field determines the secondary value that appears for each option. If this field has a numeric value, the secondary values that display will be the difference between the value for the currently selected option and the value for each additional option. If it does not have a numeric value, the value for each option displays. Affected Rating Field Field to use to execute rating when the field specified by the Affected Value Field property does not execute rating on its own or when the option selected affects additional coverages. To minimize performance implications, use the smallest amount of rating necessary to produce the appropriate affected values. However, when the option selected affects coverages outside of the coverage displayed as the affected value (for example, if the selected BI limit changes the available UM limit), select a rating field that accounts for changes in those other coverages. Display Display Type Specification of how to display the comparison widget. Options are: Drop Down List Radio Button List Slider Bar This property defaults to Drop Down List . If an index.xml file does not exist in the Global Root Directory \Express\Widgets\comparison directory, or if this file is empty, this property will display No display types available . Restore Session Indication of whether to restore session data after each affected value is calculated. This option allows ManuScript logic to set session values that may need to be reset after each affected value is calculated. Duck Creek Technologies, Inc. recommends setting this property to True . Caption Override Caption to display. Overrides the caption of the option list field. Affected Value Format Mask Format mask to apply to the secondary value that appears for each option. If no format mask is specified, the system uses the format mask specified on the Affected Value Field . This format mask does not apply to the selected or special options. Selected Format Mask Format mask to apply to the secondary value that appears for the option that is selected. Special Option Format Mask Format mask to apply to the secondary value that appears for the specified special option. Special Option Specific option to style in a special way. This feature can be used to promote a particular option. Read Only Specification of whether comparison widget content should be read-only. Show Condition Specification of when to display the comparison widget. Comparison Style CSS styles to apply to the comparison widget content. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Setting the Name Property Page Designer View The Name property contains the name of the comparison widget. This name appears on the comparison widget in the Page Designer list. To set the value of the Name property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Name . Enter the comparison widget name. Setting the Option List Field Property Page Designer View The Option List Field property specifies the field containing the options to render in the comparison widget. This field can be any field with a defined option list. If using the Slider Bar display type, specify a default option for the field specified for this property. If no default is specified, the slider bar may initially appear to have an option selected, but no value will be set in the session. To set the value of the Option List Field property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Option List Field . To the right of the Option List Field text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. Setting the Affected Value Field Property Page Designer View The Affected Value Field property specifies the field affected by the option selected. This field determines the secondary value that appears for each option. If this field has a numeric value, the secondary values that display will be the difference between the value for the currently selected option and the value for each additional option. If it does not have a numeric value, the value for each option displays. To set the value of the Affected Value Field property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Affected Value Field . To the right of the Affected Value Field text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. Setting the Affected Rating Field Property Page Designer View The Affected Rating Field property specifies the field to use to execute rating when the field specified by the Affected Value Field property does not execute rating on its own. To set the value of the Affected Rating Field property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Affected Rating Field . To the right of the Affected Rating Field text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. Setting the Display Type Property Page Designer View The Display Type property specifies how to display the comparison widget. An example of the selected display type appears below the Display Type property. To set the value of the Display Type property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Display Type . In the Display Type list, click the desired option. Options are: Dropdown - Deluxe — Drop-down list with the value of the affected field aligned to the right. Radio Button List — Collection of radio buttons. Slider Bar — Slider control. Setting the Restore Session Property Page Designer View The Restore Session property indicates whether to restore session data after each affected value is calculated. This option allows ManuScript logic to set session values that may need to be reset after each affected value is calculated. Setting this property to True will impact performance. Use this setting with caution. To set the value of the Restore Session property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Restore Session . In the Restore Session list, click the desired option. Options are: True False Setting the Caption Override Property Page Designer View The Caption Override property specifies the caption to display. This caption overrides the caption of the option list field. To set the value of the Caption Override property: Click the field. On the Definition tab, click Caption Override . Specify the desired caption. Setting the Affected Value Format Mask Property Page Designer View The Affected Value Format Mask property specifies the format mask to apply to the secondary value that appears for each option. This format mask does not apply to the selected or recommended options. To set the value of the Affected Value Format Mask property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Affected Value Format Mask . Specify a value, or, to the right of the Affected Value Format Mask text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. Setting the Selected Format Mask Property Page Designer View The Selected Format Mask property specifies the format mask to apply to the secondary value that appears for the selected option. To set the value of the Selected Format Mask property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Selected Format Mask . Specify a value, or, to the right of the Selected Format Mask text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. Setting the Special Option Format Mask Property Page Designer View The Special Option Format Mask property specifies the format mask to apply to the secondary value that appears for the specified special option. If an EXAMPLE Express ® user selects the specified option, the format specified by the Special Option Format Mask property overrides the format mask specified by the Selected Format Mask property. To set the value of the Special Option Format Mask property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Special Option Format Mask . Specify a value, or, to the right of the Special Option Format Mask text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. Setting the Special Option Property Page Designer View The Special Option property specifies an option to style in a special way. This feature can be used to promote a particular option. To set the value of the Special Option property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Special Option . Specify a value, or, to the right of the Special Option text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. Setting the Read Only Property Page Designer View The Read Only property specifies whether the comparison widget should be read-only. Rules specified here apply in addition to read-only rules specified in the field definition. The page-level Read-Only Content property overrides this widget-level property. To set the value of the Read Only property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Read Only . Specify a value, or, to the right of the Read Only text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. Setting the Show Condition Property Page Designer View The Show Condition property specifies when to show the comparison widget. To set the value of the Show Condition property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Show Condition . To the right of the Show Condition text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. Setting the Comparison Style Property Page Designer View The Comparison Style property specifies the CSS styles to apply to the content of the comparison widget. Styles specified in this property will override any global CSS styles assigned. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. To set the value of the Comparison Style property: Click the comparison widget. On the Definition tab, click Comparison Style . To the right of the Comparison Style text box, click the CSS button to use the CSS Selector dialog box to select the desired styles.

Grid Widgets Grid Widgets Save Section to .PDF Save Page to .PDF Grid widgets display iterative fields and actions within a sortable grid control. Like tables, grid widgets can display iterative fields and actions in a table. Unlike tables, however, grid widgets allow users to sort and rearrange table columns. In addition, grid widgets can paginate, displaying on each page the number of iterations specified in the Maximum Iterations property specified for the associated group. Grid widgets always display in the following manner: Fields are laid out across the page in columns; iterations are laid out down the page in rows ( Layout Direction: Across). Field captions display only for the first iteration ( Field Caption Display: First Iteration). Field captions display above field values ( Field Caption Position: Above Field). Grid widget content is read-only ( Read-Only Content: True). You can add, duplicate, and remove grid widgets, as well as define grid widget properties and convert grid widgets to sections or tables. Grid widgets can be nested within sections or tables. Duck Creek Technologies, Inc. does not support: • Editable radio buttons in grids (editable boolean fields with a format mask of radio or radio:1 ) • Multiple grids per page • Sorting of paging grids Adding a Grid Widget Page Designer View You can add grid widgets by: Adding the grid widget under a section or table Converting the section automatically created under the page group Adding a group and converting the corresponding table to a grid widget When adding grid widgets, remember that a grid widget iterates through the closest parent group in the tree (that is, the group immediately above the grid widget in the hierarchy). To add a grid widget under a section or table: Click the section or table under which to add a grid widget. On the Edit menu, click Advanced , and then click Add Grid Widget . You can also: •Press CTRL+ALT+G. •Right-click a section or table, click Advanced , and then click Add Grid Widget . To convert the section automatically created under the page group: Click the section beneath the page group. On the Edit menu, click Convert To Grid Widget . The selected section appears as a grid widget in the Page Designer list. You can also: Right-click the section, and then click Convert To Grid Widget . To add a group and convert the corresponding table: Click the section or table under which to add the group. Click the Add Group button . The Select Group Association dialog box appears. You can also: Press CTRL+G. Click the appropriate group, and then click OK . The system adds the group and a corresponding table. Click the table beneath the group. On the Edit menu, click Convert To Grid Widget . The selected table appears as a grid widget in the Page Designer list. You can also: Right-click the table, and then click Convert To Grid Widget . Duplicating a Grid Widget Page Designer View To duplicate a grid widget: Click the grid widget you wish to duplicate. On the Edit menu, click Duplicate . You can also: Right-click the grid widget, and then click Duplicate . The Duplicate Grid dialog box appears. Click the type of action to take. Options are: Duplicate grid structure only Duplicate grid structure and fields Duplicate grid structure and replace fields If you choose to duplicate the grid widget structure and replace fields, define Field Replacement Rules . Click a group for Replace fields from the group . Click a group for With similar field in the group . Click OK . Deleting a Grid Widget Page Designer View To delete a grid widget: Select the grid widget you wish to delete. Click the Delete button . In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to confirm the deletion, or No to cancel. Converting a Grid Widget For instructions on converting a grid widget to a section or table, see Converting Grid Widgets . Defining Grid Widget Properties Page Designer View To define a grid widget, specify the following properties. Property Description General Name Name of the grid widget. Display Grid Caption Text to describe the grid widget. Header Caption This property is not available for use with grid widgets. Read-Only Content Specification of whether grid widget content should be read-only. Show Condition Specification of when to display the grid widget. Sorting Indication of whether the grid widget is sortable. Duck Creek Technologies, Inc. does not support sorting of paging grids. Grid Caption Style CSS styles to apply to the grid widget caption. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Grid Style CSS styles to apply to the grid widget content. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. In addition to defining the above properties, you can also define annotations for grid widgets, and you can add actions to grid widgets. Grid widget actions appear as links in the grid widget under an unnamed column. For information on adding actions, see Actions . Setting the Name Property Page Designer View The Name property contains the name of the grid widget. This name appears on the grid widget in the Page Designer list. To set the value of the Name property: In Page Designer, click the grid widget. On the Definition tab, click Name . Enter the grid widget name. Setting the Grid Caption Property Page Designer View The Grid Caption property contains the text to display as the title of the grid. To set the value of the Grid Caption property: In Page Designer, click the grid widget. On the Definition tab, click Grid Caption . Enter the grid widget caption. Setting the Header Caption Property Page Designer View This property is not available for use with grid widgets. Setting the Read-Only Content Property Page Designer View The Read-Only Content property specifies whether the grid content is read-only. To set the value of the Read-Only Content property: Click the grid widget. On the Definition tab, click Read-Only Content . In the Read-Only Content list, click False or True . Setting the Show Condition Property Page Designer View The Show Condition property specifies when to show the grid widget. To set the value of the Show Condition property: Click the grid widget. On the Definition tab, click Show Condition . To the right of the Show Condition text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. Setting the Sorting Property Page Designer View The Sorting property specifies whether the grid widget is sortable by column. Duck Creek Technologies, Inc. does not support sorting of paging grids. To set the value of the Sorting property: Click the grid widget. On the Definition tab, click Sorting . In the Sorting list, click True or False . Setting the Grid Caption Style Property Page Designer View The Grid Caption Style property specifies the CSS styles to apply to the grid widget caption. Styles specified in this property will override any global CSS styles assigned. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. To set the value of the Grid Caption Style property: Click the grid widget. On the Definition tab, click Grid Caption Style . To the right of the Grid Caption Style text box, click the CSS button to use the CSS Selector dialog box to select the desired styles. Setting the Grid Style Property Page Designer View The Grid Style property specifies the CSS styles to apply to the grid widget content. Styles specified in this property will override any global CSS styles assigned. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. To set the value of the Grid Style property: Click the grid widget. On the Definition tab, click Grid Style . To the right of the Grid Style text box, click the CSS button to use the CSS Selector dialog box to select the desired styles.

Converting Grid Widgets Converting Grid Widgets Save Page to .PDF If desired, you can convert a grid widget to a section or a table. Converting Grid Widgets to Sections Page Designer View You can convert a grid widget to a section at any time. When you convert a grid widget to a section, the system applies all applicable properties defined for the grid widget to the new section as they were defined in the grid widget, with one exception: The Field Caption Display property for the section will be set to All . (The First Iteration option for the Field Caption Display property is not valid for sections.) To convert a grid widget to a section: Click the grid widget you wish to convert. On the Edit menu, click Convert To Section . The selected grid widget appears as a section in the Page Designer list. You can also: Right-click the grid widget, and then click Convert To Section . Converting Grid Widgets to Tables Page Designer View You can convert a grid widget to a table at any time. When you convert a grid widget to a table, the system applies all applicable properties defined for the grid widget to the new table as they were defined in the grid widget. To convert a grid widget to a table: Click the grid widget you wish to convert. On the Edit menu, click Convert To Table . The selected grid widget appears as a table in the Page Designer list. You can also: Right-click the table, and then click Convert To Table .

Custom Parameters Supported by Grid Widgets Custom Parameters Supported by Grid Widgets Save Page to .PDF You can add custom parameters to fields used on grids in EXAMPLE Express ® . The following table displays the custom parameters currently supported by grid widgets. Parameter Description customWidth Sets the width of a column within a grid in EXAMPLE Express ® . If the sum of the widths of each column is greater then the total width of the grid, individual column width will be determined by the ratio of the current column width to the sum of the column widths. Using this parameter also allows you to use percentages to determine column width. When a custom parameter from a Version 1 page is added as a custom property to a Version 2 page during conversion, you should evaluate whether any of the standard properties for Version 2 pages already accomplish what is specified in the custom property. If the standard properties do not accomplish what is needed, you must customize your skin to accommodate the custom property. For more information about converting custom parameters, see Conversion Support for Custom Parameters .

Progress Bar Widgets Progress Bar Widgets Save Page to .PDF A progress bar widget is a graphical depiction of progress through a set of steps. Progress bar widgets can include both a graphical depiction and text description of progress. This widget's out-of-the-box appearance can be customized by customizing EXAMPLE Express ® skins. You can add and remove progress bar widgets, as well as define progress bar widget properties. Progress bar widgets can be nested within sections or tables. In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to confirm the deletion, or No to cancel. Defining Progress Bar Widget Properties Page Designer View To define a progress bar widget, specify the following properties. Property Description General Name Name of the progress bar widget. Data Message Text to display. If blank, text defaults to [x]% Completed during a process (were [x] equals the percentage completed), and Completed when the process is complete. Value Current percentage completed. This property must contain a decimal value between 0 and 1. If blank, the value defaults to 0. Display Show Condition Specification of when to display the progress bar widget. Width Width of the progress bar widget (in pixels), if different from the default width. The value of this property must be an integer. Progress Bar Style CSS styles to apply to the progress bar widget. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. If blank, the text defaults to [x]% Completed during a process (were [x] equals the percentage completed), and Completed when the process is complete. To set the value of the Message property: Click the progress bar widget. On the Definition tab, click Message . Specify a value, or, to the right of the Message text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. The value of this property must be an integer. To set the value of the Width property: Click the progress bar widget. On the Definition tab, click Width . Enter the desired width, in pixels. Setting the Progress Bar Style Property Page Designer View The Progress Bar Style property specifies the CSS styles to apply to the progress bar widget. Styles specified in this property will override any global CSS styles assigned. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. To set the value of the Progress Bar Style property: Click the progress bar widget. On the Definition tab, click Progress Bar Style . To the right of the Progress Bar Style text box, click the CSS button to use the CSS Selector dialog box to select the desired styles.

XHTML Widgets XHTML Widgets Save Page to .PDF XHTML widgets allow you to define HTML markup to be inserted at any point on a page. XHTML widgets must contain HTML that is both valid HTML and valid XML. XHTML widgets cannot contain sections, tables, grid widgets, fields, or actions. Because XHTML widgets break consistency in page structure and styling, Duck Creek Technologies, Inc. recommends using XHTML widgets only when necessary. You can add or remove XHTML widgets and define properties for them. XHTML widgets can be nested within sections or tables. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Setting the Name Property Page Designer View The Name property contains the name of the XHTML widget. Defining Field Properties Page Designer View To define a field, you specify the following properties. Property Description General Caption Override Caption to override the field caption on the rendered page. Compute Value Indication of whether to compute the field when it is displayed. Refresh Page on Change Indication of whether to refresh the page when the field changes. Advanced Read Only Specification of whether the field should be read-only. Rules specified here apply in addition to read-only rules specified in the field definition. The page-level Read-Only Content property overrides this field-level property. Hide Field Specification of when to hide the field. Hover Help Indication to display annotations when the cursor hovers over the field instead of linking the field caption to the annotation. The Hover Help property defined at the page level overrides the Hover Help property defined at the field level. Clear If Zero Indication of whether to force any int or float fields with a value of 0 to blank. Display Field Caption Style CSS styles to apply to the field caption. Caption Icon CSS styles to apply to the caption icon. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Watermark CSS styles to apply to a watermark. Styles apply according to CSS inheritance rules. Truncate value if too long CSS styles to apply to force a value to be cut off and replaced with ellipses and a tooltip if the field or value text extends outside the width of its parent element. Truncate label if too long CSS styles to apply to force a label to be cut off and replaced with ellipses and a tooltip if the field or label text extends outside the width of its parent element. In addition to defining the above properties, you can also define options for displaying the field in a rating worksheet. Click Worksheet Options to open the Worksheet Options dialog box and define display options. Actions Actions Save Page to .PDF Actions are buttons that can be placed in a group, section, table, or grid widget. You can add or remove actions, or define properties for actions. Adding an Action Page Designer View To add an action: Click the appropriate section, table, or grid widget. On the Edit menu, click Insert action . You can also: Right-click a section, table, or grid widget, and then click Insert action . Define the properties for the action. Deleting an Action Page Designer View To delete an action: Select the action you wish to delete. Click the Delete button . In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to confirm the deletion, or No to cancel. Defining Actions Page Designer View To define an action, you specify the following properties. Properties vary by action. Property Description General Action Type Action to be performed. Options vary depending upon what the action is added to: Delete Record (sections/tables/grid widgets) Duplicate Record (sections/tables/grid widgets) Add Record (groups) Goto Page Calculate (discard results) Calculate (keep results) Detail for this Record (sections/tables/grid widgets) Iteration Links (groups) Custom Action Mark-for-Delete (sections/tables/grid widgets) Caption Button text to describe the action. Show Condition Conditional field that specifies when to show the action. Pre-Action Field to be executed before this action is performed. Post-Action Field to be executed after this action is performed. Use Editing ID Boolean indication of whether or not to use the value of the ManuScriptEditingID field for the ManuScript, PageSet and Page to be loaded. If false or if the ManuScriptEditingID field has no value, the system will use the ManuScript, PageSet, and Page specified by the ManuScript ID , Page Set , and Page properties. Page Page to be loaded when the action is Goto Page. Page Set PageSet that contains the page to be loaded when the action is Goto Page. ManuScript ID ID of ManuScript. If empty, the system uses the current ManuScript. Field (Optional) Field to use as an entry point for the calculation instead of the Primary Value. Only available with the Calculate (discard results) and Calculate (keep results) actions. Express Action Built-in action to be passed to the EXAMPLE Express ® system. Each EXAMPLE Express ® Action is related to a post Action and optional target Page for the action. The post Action defines the actual command to be performed by Express, such as save , submit , or loadHistory . The target Page identifies the page name to which EXAMPLE Express ® should navigate, such as print , open , or new . The format is postAction:targetPage using a colon to separate the post Action and the target Page. Duck Creek Technologies, Inc. does not support the use of a custom request with an Express Action. If you need to run a request before the Express Action is executed, use a PreEvent instead. Advanced PopUp Indication of whether the page opened by the action should appear in a pop-up window, or whether to close an open pop-up window.If this property is defined, the following additional properties appear: PopUp Title — (optional) Title caption for the pop-up window. PopUp Width — Specification of width (in pixels) of the pop-up window. PopUp Height — Specification of height (in pixels) of the pop-up window. Popup Class — Specification of the CSS class to be applied to a popup for enhanced control of individual popup windows. The specified class is applied with the .x-window class. Hide Popup Close Button — Indicates whether to hide a popup's close button and force the user to interact with the popup before continuing. Show Processing Message Specification of a "processing" message to display for actions that take additional time to process. Any action taking longer than four seconds (or a number specified in EXAMPLE Express ® skins) will automatically display a loading message after four seconds. Confirm Indication of whether to require the user to confirm the action before processing the action. If this property is defined, the Confirm Text property also appears. PreEvent Field Specification of validation rules that must be performed before executing the action. When the specified field returns a blank value or a space, the action can be performed. When the specified field returns any other value, the value is displayed to the user and the action is not performed. If this property is defined, the following additional properties appear: PreEvent Context — If the PreEvent Field is part of an iterative group, indicates the context for the iterative group. PreEvent Confirm — Indication of whether to require the user to confirm the action before processing the action. PreEvent Confirm Text — Specification of the text to appear in the confirmation message. PreEvent Field Alert Only — Transforms the confirmation message to an alert message. Use the PreEvent Confirm Text property to specify text for the alert message. Ignore Validation Indication of whether to skip field-level validation before leaving a page. Cancel Changes Indication of whether to remove any changes not stored in the session when the action is selected. Print Action Specification of a print job to print. This property requires the following syntax: printJob:[print job name]|[ManuScript ID]. (ManuScript ID is optional.) In addition, you can add three custom parameters to this property: effectiveDate quoteIDs portfolioIDs Suppress Refresh Indication of whether the page should refresh after the user views a rating worksheet. Options include: True – Prevents the refresh from occurring. False – Allows the refresh to occur. This property is only available when a value has been entered for the Print Action property. Custom Command Specification of a custom command. Currently, the following commands are valid: windowClose — Performs a window.close() JavaScript function. Print Job Identification of the print job to use to build the forms list. This property is valid only when one of the following Express Actions has been defined: initPrintJob initAsyncPrintJob runRulesPrintJob runRulesAsyncPrintJob closePrintJob previewPrintJob previewAsyncPrintJob Print Job ManuScript Identification of the ManuScript containing the print job to use to build the forms list. If blank, this property defaults to the current ManuScript. This property is valid only when one of the following Express Actions has been defined: initPrintJob initAsyncPrintJob runRulesPrintJob runRulesAsyncPrintJob closePrintJob previewPrintJob previewAsyncPrintJob Display Display Type Specification of how to display the action. Options are: Button Hyperlink Action Style CSS styles to apply to the action. Button Type Indication of the type of button to use. Default is the green Avant Garde button. The following table displays the out-of-the-box button types. Button Type Button Button with Icon standard (default) black cancel or gray hyperlink loneIcon (requires icon) search or blue white transparent (with gray background) lightBlue lightGreen orange red Button Icon Image Specification of the icon to be used with a button. Examples, as displayed above, include: accept.png shield.png cross.png disk.png help.png magnifier.png tick.png lock.png chart_bar.png coins.png email.png control_play_backwards.png When a value has been specified for the Button Icon Image property, the following additional property appears: Button Icon Position — Specification of where a button icon should appear in relation to its associated button. Button Alert Message Specification of whether button alert messages should be used. These messages provide quick help text to assist the user in moving through the interview process. Button Quick Tip Indicates whether a tooltip should appear when the user hovers over a specific button. You can also add requests to actions. You define requests in the Request Command and Name Value Definitions dialog box. Setting the Action Type Property Page Designer View You can define the following types of actions. Action Description Delete Record (sections/tables/grid widgets) Deletes an iteration of an object from the session data document. The default behavior returns to the current page after the object is deleted. If you specify a page or PageSet, EXAMPLE Server ® deletes the object and then goes to the new page. Duplicate Record (sections/tables/grid widgets) Duplicates an iteration of an object in the session data document. The default behavior returns to the current page after the object is duplicated. If you specify a page or PageSet, EXAMPLE Server ® duplicates the object and then goes to the new page. Add Record (groups) Adds an iteration of an object to the session data document. The default behavior returns to the current page after a new object is added. If you specify a page or PageSet, EXAMPLE Server ® adds the object and goes to the new page that contains only the iteration of the newly added object. Goto Page Routes the interview to a different destination. Under normal circumstances the Goto Page destination is determined when the page is rendered. However, it is possible to determine the Goto Page destination after the button is pressed. To determine the destination page after the button is clicked: Enter the name of a field containing the name of the destination page, prefixed by a $ (dollar sign). When the <ManuScript.getPageRq> request is processed and the value of the Goto Page command begins with a $ , the specified field is executed to determine the name of the page or PageSet to which to route. Calculate (discard results) Calculates the ManuScript and do not preserve the calculated results in the session data document. Calculate (keep results) Calculates the ManuScript and preserve the calculated results in the session data document. Detail for this Record (sections/tables/grid widgets) Displays fields for a single iteration. If you specify a page or PageSet, the detail displays on the specified page. (The root group for a page displaying a single iteration must be the iterating group.) This action cannot be used to retrieve data stored in named documents. Iteration Links (groups) Returns a set of actions in conjunction with iterating objects. The number of actions depends on the current number of repeating objects in the data document for the context of this group and the values in the following Iterations fields: Maximum — Each action displays only the number of objects entered in the Maximum field for the specified PageSet. Links — The value in the Links fields specifies the maximum number of actions returned on a page. Custom Action Executes a <getPageRq> request that contains an attribute of command = "custom" . This indicates to the application that custom processing must occur. The setting of command="custom" is ignored by EXAMPLE Server ® . Mark-for-Delete (sections/tables/grid widgets) Deletes an iteration of an object in the session data document. To set the value of the Action Type property: Click the action. On the Definition tab, click Action Type . In the Action Type list, click the appropriate option. The action type is added to the selected action in the Page Designer list. Working with CSS Override Styles Working with CSS Override Styles Save Page to .PDF You can assign CSS override styles to individual page elements using the CSS Selector in Page Designer. To open the CSS selector: To the right of the appropriate Style property for the appropriate element, click the CSS button . Applying Override Styles To apply an override style to a page element in Page Designer: To the right of the appropriate Style property for the appropriate element, click the CSS button . The CSS Selector dialog box appears. In the Available Styles list, click a style to apply, and then click Add . (You can multi-select using CTRL+click.) The Available Styles list in the CSS Selector displays only those styles defined with a class-only selector. It will not display styles with selectors containing conditions or other content. If you wish to define styles with more complex selectors, you can do so in the x_interview.css override file in the [GlobalRootPath]\Express\Skins\ [SkinName] \css directory. To cause these styles to appear in the CSS Selector, you must first define the style with a class-only selector. (The style may contain declarations or be empty.) You can then create additional styles with the same class name and the desired selectors. The class-only style will appear in the Available Styles list, and selecting that style will then apply all styles with that class name. However, the Definition box will only display the declarations defined for the class-only style. The selected styles appear in the Styles to Apply list, and the definition for all styles in the list appears in the Definition box. (Optional) To move a style up or down in the hierarchy, click the style, and then click Move Up or Move Down . Click OK . Removing Override Styles To remove an override style from a page element: To the right of the appropriate Style property for the appropriate element, click the CSS button . The CSS Selector dialog box appears. In the Styles to Apply list, click the style, and then click Remove . Click OK . Viewing Override Style Definition You can view the definition for the override styles applied to certain page elements in the CSS Selector dialog box. To view definition for the override styles currently applied: To the right of the appropriate Style property for the appropriate element, click the CSS button . The Definition box beneath the Styles to Apply list contains the total definition for the selected styles. (Optional) Click styles in the Styles to Apply list to isolate the definition for each specific style. Definition for items selected in the Styles to Apply list appears in italics. The Definition text box is read-only. Managing Override Styles You can add, delete, or modify the available override styles using the CSS Manager tool in EXAMPLE Author ® . This tool reads from and writes to the x_interview.css override file in the Global Root Directory \Express\Skins\ CustomSkin \css directory. From the CSS Manager, you can access and edit the x_interview.css override file. The x_interview.css override file is shipped with several out-of-the-box override styles. From the CSS Manager, you can add to, delete, or modify these styles. Style Definition Bold font-weight: bold Italic font-style: italic LargeFont font-size: large MediumFont font-size: medium SmallFont font-size: small TextAlignLeft text-align: left TextAlignRight text-align: right XLargeFont font-size: x-large XSmallFont font-size: x-small XXSmallFont font-size: xx-small To open the CSS Manager: In EXAMPLE Author ® , on the Tools menu, click CSS Manager . Adding a New Style To add a new style in CSS Manager: On the Tools menu, click CSS Manager . The CSS Manager dialog box appears. Click Create New . The Create New dialog box appears. Enter the Class Name for the style, and then click OK . The new style appears in the Style drop-down list. In the Definition pane, define the style by performing one of the following actions: Click a property from the list of properties, and then click Insert . You can also: Double-click the desired attribute. Define the property in the text box. Enter the desired definition in the text box. When finished, click Apply to save the definition and continue working in CSS Manager, or click OK to save the definition and close CSS Manager. Deleting a Style Before deleting a style in CSS Manager, verify the style is not used in any ManuScripts using the x_interview.css file. To delete a style in CSS Manager: On the Tools menu, click CSS Manager . The CSS Manager dialog box appears. In the Style list, click the style you wish to delete. Click Delete . A confirmation dialog box appears. In the confirmation dialog, click Yes . Modifying a Style To modify a style in CSS Manager: On the Tools menu, click CSS Manager . The CSS Manager dialog box appears. In the Style list, click the style you wish to modify. In the Definition pane, modify the style as desired. When finished, click Apply to save the definition and continue working in CSS Manager, or click OK to save the definition and close CSS Manager. Specificity The EXAMPLE Platform ® uses specificity to determine the order in which identical CSS selectors are applied. For example, if .myClass {float:left; } and .myClass {float:right; } CSS selectors existed (in that order), the .myClass elements would float right because the value of the last CSS selector in the list takes precedence when the CSS selector values are the same. CSS elements within a <div> element take precedence because they are considered to be more specific than elements not contained in a <div> element. Calculating Specificity Values To determine the specificity of nested CSS selectors, each selector is assigned a point value. The following table includes a breakdown of the points assigned to each type of selector: CSS Selector Type Value id selectors (for example, #myId) 100 class selectors (for example, .myClass) 10 HTML selectors (for example, div) 1 For each selector in a group, the point values are added together. The result is the specificity value. The following table includes some examples of how specificity values are calculated. Groups of Selectors Specificity Value .acrossFieldGroup>div 11 (10 + 1) .acrossFieldGroup>.acrossBeforeFormLabel 20 (10 + 10) #requiredAsterisk 100 .required #requiredAsterisk 110 (10 + 100) Overriding Specificity Some CSS files (for example, forms.css) use the greater than (>) symbol to combine CSS selectors into groups to reduce the number of inconsistencies possible in dynamic layouts. As a result, some .css file definitions have become more specific than most of the selectors in the x_interview.css file and could cause issues to surface. Determining Whether Specificity has Been Overridden To determine whether specificity has been overridden: Using the developer toolbar in Internet Explorer 8, the Firebug feature in Mozilla Firefox, or the inspector feature in Google Chrome, search for overridden CSS styles in the toolbar. Overridden CSS styles appear with a line through them. Overriding Specificity When experiencing specificity issues, you can often work around them by overriding specificity rules using the "!important" rule. To override specificity rules: Add the !important rule to the end of the CSS selector to which you wish to give precedence. The following is an example of a CSS selector with overridden specificity. .x_FloatRight { float:right !important; }

Best Practices for Building Version 2 Pages Best Practices for Building Version 2 Pages Save Page to .PDF When building version 2 pages, consider the following Best Practices: When working with iterative data, place fields within a table or grid widget. When working with non-iterative data: If field captions and data can be centered, place fields within a section. If field captions and data must be left-aligned, place fields within a table. Field captions and data within a section may be left-aligned by hardcoding a pixel width for the caption of each field to be aligned. Instead of adding extra spacer elements to position page elements on pages, rely on CSS styles. Limiting the number of page elements used to position items reduces the amount of HTML that must be generated and reduces the number of CSS rules that must be applied to render pages appropriately. Use XHTML widgets sparingly. XHTML widgets break consistency with the structure and styles applied to normal interview pages and page elements, making maintenance of interview pages more difficult. The use of multiple grids per page is not supported.

Conversion Support for Custom Parameters Conversion Support for Custom Parameters Save Page to .PDF During conversion to Version 2 pages, the Page Converter supports all custom parameters defined in Version 1 pages by: Converting any custom parameters supported by Version 2 pages to the equivalent advanced properties Adding any custom parameters not supported by Version 2 pages (that is, any parameters that do not have equivalent advanced properties) as Custom Properties . Custom properties appear below all other properties. When a custom parameter from a version 1 page is added as a custom property to a version 2 page during conversion, you should evaluate whether any of the standard properties for version 2 pages already accomplish what is specified in the custom property. If the standard properties do not accomplish what is needed, you must customize your skin to accommodate the custom property. The following custom parameters in Version 1 pages are supported by the following equivalent advanced properties in Version 2 pages. Custom Parameter (version 1 pages) Advanced Property (version 2 pages) Pages noHeader Hide Header calculate Show Rating Messages nextButton Next Button Caption previousButton Previous Button Caption Fields readOnly Read Only eliminate Hide Field removeZero Clear to Zero Actions popUp PopUp popUpWidth PopUp Width popUpHeight PopUp Height processingMsg Show Processing Message confirm Confirm confirmText Confirm Text preEventField PreEvent Field preEventFieldContext PreEvent Context preEventFieldConfirm PreEvent Confirm preEventFieldConfirmText preEvent Confirm Text preEventFieldAlertOnly PreEvent Field Alert Only ignoreValidation Ignore Validation PDFPage Print Action skinCommand Custom Command printJob Print Job printJobManuScript Print Job ManuScript

Designing Version 1 Pages Designing Version 1 Pages Save Section to .PDF Save Page to .PDF When using Page Designer to create version 1 pages, you specify: PageSets Pages Groups Layouts Fields Section Contents PageSets Pages Page Groups Layouts Page Fields

PageSets PageSets Save Page to .PDF You can add or remove PageSets, and define PageSet properties. Adding a PageSet Page Designer View To add a PageSet: Click the Add PageSet button . The Add PageSet dialog box appears. Enter the new PageSet name,and then click OK . Deleting a PageSet Page Designer View To delete a PageSet: Click the PageSet you wish to delete. Click the Delete button . In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to confirm the deletion or No to cancel. Defining PageSet Properties Page Designer View To define a PageSet, specify the following properties: Property Description PageSet Name Name of the PageSet. Caption Name of the PageSet as the user sees it. Purpose Purpose of the PageSet. Options are: Main Interview PageSet Linked Interview PageSet Printable Pages Documentation Other Stylesheet XSLT file used to transform the page. Show PageSet Condition indicating whether the PageSet is displayed. Currently, EXAMPLE Express ® does not support this feature. Rule Set The Rule Set property specifies a special set of rules (defined in EXAMPLE Express® skins) to apply to all pages in a PageSet. These rules can control the display or behavior of these pages. Out of the box the following rule set is available: topNav — Changes the default left-navigation layout to a top-navigation layout. Setting the PageSet Name Page Designer View The PageSet Name property specifies the name of the PageSet as the user sees it. To set the value of the PageSet Name property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click PageSet Name . Enter the name. The PageSet name changes in the Page Designer list. Setting the PageSet Caption Page Designer View The Caption property specifies the name of the PageSet as the user sees it. To set the value of the Caption property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Caption . Enter the caption text. Setting the PageSet Purpose Page Designer View The Purpose property specifies the purpose of the PageSet. This property is important to EXAMPLE Express ® and to other applications using EXAMPLE Platform ® page requests to render interview pages. It allows you to categorize the PageSet in such a way that the downstream system can reference the correct pages based on the context of the application. For example, when you start a new quote, EXAMPLE Express ® uses the "Main Interview PageSet." To render an on-line product manual, however, EXAMPLE Express ® uses the appropriate "Documentation" PageSet. To set the value of the Purpose property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Purpose . In the Purpose list, click the appropriate purpose. The Purpose property includes the following options. Purpose Description Main Interview PageSet Defaults interview pages when a new quote or policy is started or a stored quote or policy is loaded in EXAMPLE Express ® . Linked Interview PageSet Pages to be referenced as a hyperlink from a Main Interview PageSet. Printable Pages Pages associated with an actualprintable form. When using the DCT forms package, link these pages with the appropriate forms in the Forms Mapper view. Other implementations may link them directly to a printable HTML or XSL file. Documentation Pages associated with an XSLT style sheet that can be used to republish the ManuScript information into a readable or printable documentation set such as an online manual which has linked values to tables, algorithms, annotations, and so on. Other Miscellaneous category for customers who design pages that can be referenced in a fashion different from those already specified. Setting the PageSet Stylesheet Page Designer View The Stylesheet property specifies the XSLT file used to transform the page. To set the value of the Stylesheet property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Stylesheet . Enter the style sheet. Setting the Show PageSet Property Page Designer View This setting is included for use in third-party downstream systems and is not supported by EXAMPLE Express ® . The Show PageSet property specifies a condition indicating whether the PageSet is displayed. To set the value of the Show PageSet property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Show PageSet . To the right of the Show PageSet text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. Setting the Rule Set Property Page Designer View The Rule Set property specifies the name of the rule set used to style pages in the PageSet. These rules can control the display or behavior of these pages. Out of the box the following rule set is available: topNav — Changes the default left-navigation layout to a top-navigation layout. To set the value of the Rule Set property: Click the PageSet. On the Definition tab, click Rule Set . In the Rule text box, enter the name of the rule set you wish to use.

Pages Pages Save Page to .PDF You can add or remove pages, convert pages from version 1 to version 2, and define page properties. Adding a Page Page Designer View To add a version 1 page: Click the PageSet under which to add a page. Click the Add Page button . The Add Page dialog box appears. In the Page Version drop-down list, click 1.0 . Enter the name of the new page. If you wish the page to be a header, enter header . (Optional) Select the Referenced Page check box, and then click a PageSet and Page to reference the new page. Click OK . The Select Group Association dialog box appears. Click the group to use for the page, and then click OK . Deleting a Page Page Designer View To delete a page: Click the page you wish to delete. Click the Delete button . In the Confirm dialog box, click Yes to confirm the deletion or No to cancel. Converting a Page For instructions on converting a page from Version 1 to Version 2, see Converting a Page (from Version 1) . Defining Page Properties Page Designer View To define a page, you specify the following properties. Property Description Page Name Name of the page. Page Version Version of the page. Caption Name of the page as the user sees it. Execute ManuScript Indication of whether the ManuScript calculates values before the page is displayed. Options are: No — Do not execute the ManuScript. Calculate — Calculate the field specified as the primary value and display the result, but do not update the session. Calculate and Accept — Calculate the field specified as the primary value and display the result, and update the session. Include Worksheet Data Indication of whether to enable rating worksheet functionality on the page. This property appears only when the Execute ManuScript property is set to Calculate or Calculate and Accept . Include Debug Boolean value that specifies whether the page response should include rating debug information. Read-Only Content Indication of whether the page is read-only. This page-level property overrides the field-level Read Only property. Show Page Indication of whether the page should be visible. Enable Partial Refresh Indication of whether to enable partial refresh of fields, layouts, and buttons (custom actions) on the page. Annotations Annotations associated with the page. You can also add optional parameters to a page. Setting the Page Name Page Designer View The Page Name property specifies the name of the page as the user sees it. The value of this property is set in the Add Page dialog box when you create the page. To change the name of a page: Right-click the page. In the shortcut menu, click Rename . The Rename Item dialog box appears. Enter the new name for the page, and then click OK . The Page name changes in the Page Designer list and in the Page Name property. Setting the Page Version Page Designer View The Page Version property specifies the page version. The value of this property is set in the Add Page dialog box when you create the page. If you have defined the page version as version 1, you can convert the page to version 2. Pages cannot be converted from version 2 to version 1. To convert a page to version 2: On the Tools menu, click Page Converter . The Page Converter dialog box appears. Click Convert a single page , and then specify the PageSet and Page you wish to convert. Click Continue . A warning message appears. Select Yes, I understand , and then click Continue . In the success confirmation dialog, click OK . The Page version changes in the Page Version property. Setting the Page Caption Page Designer View The Caption property specifies the name of the page as the user sees it. To set the value of the Caption property: Click the page. On the Definition tab, click Caption . Specify a value, or, to the right of the Caption text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. Setting the Execute ManuScript Property Page Designer View The Execute ManuScript property specifies whether the ManuScript calculates values before the page is displayed. To set the value of the Execute ManuScript property: Click the page. On the Definition tab, click Execute ManuScript . In the Execute ManuScript list, click the appropriate option: No — Do not execute the ManuScript. Calculate — Calculate the field specified as the primary value and display the result, but do not update the session. Calculate and Accept — Calculate the field specified as the primary value and display the result, and update the session. Executing a ManuScript often slows performance. Only execute when necessary. Setting the Include Worksheet Data Property Page Designer View The Include Worksheet Data property enables rating worksheet functionality on a page. The Include Worksheet Data property appears only when the Execute ManuScript property is set to Calculate or Calculate and Accept . To the right of the Include Worksheet Data text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. To set the value of the Include Debug property: Click the page. On the Definition tab, click Include Debug . In the Include Debug list, click Yes or No . Setting the Read-Only Content Property Page Designer View The Read-Only Content property specifies whether the page is read-only. This property overrides the field-level Read Only property. To set the value of the Read-Only Content property: Click the page. On the Definition tab, click Read-Only Content . To the right of the Read-Only Content text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. Setting the Show Page Property Page Designer View The Show Page property specifies the condition under which the page should be visible. To set the value of the Show Page property: Click the page. On the Definition tab, click Show Page . To the right of the Show Page text box, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field. Setting the Enable Partial Refresh Property Page Designer View The Enable Partial Refresh property specifies whether to enable partial refresh of fields, layouts, and buttons (custom actions) on the page. To set the value of the Enable Partial Refresh property: Click the page. On the Definition tab, click Enable Partial Refresh . In the Enable Partial Refresh list, click True or False . Adding or Removing Optional Parameters Page Designer View To add an optional parameter: On the Definition tab, right-click the Properties area, and then click Add Parameter . The Add Extra Parameter dialog box appears. Enter the name of the parameter, and then click OK . To remove an optional parameter: Right-click the parameter, and then click Remove Parameter . Setting Page Annotations Page Designer View An annotation may be added to any field, page, or layout. Annotations can contain any text, but they are generally used to display descriptive information as help text for a product user interface. Maximum Iterations Maximum number of iterations that can be displayed at any one time on the page. Link Iterations Number of action links to display the page. Relational Field Specification of a related field (in a related group) to display for each iteration. (The value of the field specified should be the foreign key in the related group.) Style Custom code to format the style of pages. Included Deleted Indication of whether records marked for deletion should be included in processing. Restrict Where Specification of conditions under which records should not display. Restrict Xpath Specification of xpath restrictions. Iterations with the specified xpaths do not display. Order By Field by which to sort iterations. Custom Actions Custom actions that have been added to the page group. Layout Affect ThinBorder Creates a thin border around the fields within the layout. align:center: Centers the fields within the layout. align:left: Left-justifies the fields within the layout. align:right: Right-justifies the fields within the layout. alignC:center: Centers the field captions within the layout. alignC:left: Left-justifies the field captions within the layout. alignC:right: Right-justifies the field captions within the layout. file Creates a Browse button to the right of the input field that can be used to populate the field with a file name. On the Definition tab, in the Custom Actions area, click Insert Action . Define the action. Defining Custom Actions In the Custom Action definition pane, you specify the following properties for custom actions. The properties vary by action. Property Description Caption Button text to describe the custom action. Show Condition Conditional field that determines if this command is returned by the EXAMPLE Server ® . Pre-Action Field to be executed before this action is performed. Post-Action Field to be executed after this action is performed. Action Action to be performed. You can select: Add Record Goto Page Calculate (discard results) Calculate (keep results) Iteration Links Custom Action Mark-for-Delete Use Editing ID Boolean indication of whether or not to use the value of the ManuScriptEditingID field for the ManuScript, PageSet and Page to be loaded. If false or if the ManuScriptEditingID field has no value, the system will use the ManuScript, PageSet, and Page specified by the ManuScript ID , Page Set , and Page properties. Page Page to be loaded when the action is Goto Page. PageSet PageSet that contains the page to be loaded when the action is Goto Page. ManuScript ID ID of ManuScript. If empty, the current ManuScript. Express Action Built-in action to be passed to the EXAMPLE Express ® system. Each EXAMPLE Express ® Action is related to a post Action and optional target Page for the action. The post Action defines the actual command to be performed by Express, such as save , submit , or loadHistory . The target Page identifies the page name to which EXAMPLE Express ® should navigate, such as print , open , or new . The format is postAction:targetPage using a colon to separate the post Action and the target Page. Field (Optional) Field to use as an entry point for the calculation instead of the Primary Value. Only available with the Calculate (discard results) and Calculate (keep results) actions. Request Request specifications. You define requests in the Request Command and Name Value Definitions dialog box. Types of Custom Actions You can define the following types of custom actions for groups. Action Description Add Record Adds an iteration of an object to the session data document. The default behavior returns to the current page after a new object is added. If you specify a page or PageSet, EXAMPLE Server ® adds the object and goes to the new page that contains only the iteration of the newly added object. Goto Page Routes the interview to a different destination. Under normal circumstances the Goto Page destination is determined when the page is rendered. However, it is possible to determine the Goto Page destination after the button is pressed. To determine the destination page after the button is clicked: Enter the name of a field containing the name of the destination page, prefixed by a $ (dollar sign). When the <ManuScript.getPageRq> request is processed and the value of the Goto Page command begins with a $ , the specified field is executed to determine the name of the page or PageSet to which to route. Calculate and Discard Calculates the ManuScript and do not preserve the calculated results in the session data document. Calculate and Keep Calculates the ManuScript and preserve the calculated results in the session data document. Iteration Links Returns a set of actions in conjunction with iterating objects. The number of actions depends on the current number of repeating objects in the data document for the context of this group and the values in the following Iterations fields: Maximum — Each action displays only the number of objects entered in the Maximum field for the specified PageSet. Links — The value in the Links fields specifies the maximum number of actions returned on a page. Custom Action Executes a <getPageRq> request that contains an attribute of command = "custom" . This indicates to the application that custom processing must occur. The setting of command="custom" is ignored by EXAMPLE Server ® .

The post Action defines the actual command to be performed by Express, such as save , submit , or loadHistory . The target Page identifies the page name to which EXAMPLE Express ® should navigate, such as print , open , or new . The format is postAction:targetPage using a colon to separate the post Action and the target Page. Field (Optional) Field to use as an entry point for the calculation instead of the Primary Value. Only available with the Calculate (discard results) and Calculate (keep results) actions. Request Request specifications. You define requests in the Request Command and Name Value Definitions dialog box.

Page Fields Page Fields Save Page to .PDF You can add or remove page fields and define page field properties. Adding a New Page Field Page Designer View To add a page field: Click the appropriate layout. Click the Insert Field button . The Reference Select dialog box appears. You can also: Press CTRL+A. Click the field, and then click OK . Define the properties for the field. Deleting a Page Field Page Designer View To delete a page field: Select the field you wish to delete from the page. Click the Delete button . Defining Page Field Properties Page Designer View To define a page field, you specify the following properties. Property Description Caption Override Caption to override the field caption on the rendered page. Compute Value Indication of whether to compute the field when it is displayed. Refresh Page on Change Indication of whether to reload the page when the field changes. When Selected Fields (NO dependencies) or Selected Fields (FULL dependencies) is selected, the Fields Refreshed on Change property also appears. Worksheet Options Options for displaying the field in a rating worksheet. You can also add optional parameters to a page field. Setting the Caption Override Property Page Designer View The Caption Override property specifies a caption to override the caption of the field. To set the value of the Caption Override property: Click the field. On the Definition tab, click Caption Override . Enter the caption. Setting the Compute Value Property Page Designer View The Compute Value property indicates whether to compute the field when it is displayed. To set the value of the Compute Value property: Click the field. On the Definition tab, click Compute Value . In the Compute Value list, click False or True . Setting the Refresh Page on Change Property Page Designer View The Refresh Page on Change property indicates whether to reload the page when the field changes. To set the value of the Refresh Page on Change property: Click the field. On the Definition tab, click Refresh Page on Change . In the Refresh Page on Change list, click False , True , Selected Fields (FULL dependencies) , or Selected Fields (NO dependencies) . If you click Selected Fields (FULL dependencies) , or Selected Fields (NO dependencies) , the Fields Refreshed on Change property appears. Setting the Fields Refreshed on Change Property Page Designer View The Fields Refreshed on Change property indicates which fields to update in the browser when the field changes. This property is only available if the value of the Refresh Page on Change property is Selected Fields (NO dependencies) or Selected Fields (FULL dependencies) . To set the value of the Refresh Page on Change property: Click the field. On the Definition tab, click Fields Refreshed on Change . Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference, or specify a value. Adding or Removing Optional Parameters Page Designer View To add an optional parameter: On the Definition tab, right-click the Properties area, and then click Add Parameter . The Add Extra Parameter dialog box appears. Enter the name of the parameter, and then click OK . On the line of the new parameter, specify a value, or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. To remove an optional parameter: Right-click the parameter, and then click Remove Parameter .

Importing Pages Importing Pages Save Page to .PDF Page Designer View To import pages into a ManuScript: On the File menu, click Import . The Import Wizard dialog box appears. Click Catalog . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. Click the ManuScript from which to import, and then click Load . Click Import Pages , and then click Next . The pages in the selected ManuScript are displayed in the Select the items to import list. Click the pages to import. If you select a page, you automatically select all elements within the page. Click Next . The elements to be imported are listed. Any items selected in the previous display that already exist in the current ManuScript are not selected for importing. The list includes any groups, fields, and tables that the pages reference. You can clear any element you do not want to import by clearing the check box. You can force the importation of an element already in the current ManuScript by selecting the appropriate check box or by clicking the Mark All button . Click Finish . All selected items are imported into the ManuScript.
