Forms Forms Save Page to .PDF To produce a form in Forms Mapper, you must first create a form that specifies the ManuScript group used to populate that form and specifies properties for that form. You then add sub-forms to the main form. Sub-forms are the .doc or .pdf files that display the ManuScript data when the form is printed. Adding a Form Forms Mapper View To add a form: Click the Add Form button . The Add New Form dialog box appears. You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Form , and then click Add Form . • Right-click the Forms list, and then click Add Form . • Press CTRL+A. Enter the form name, and then click OK . The Select Primary Group dialog box appears . Click the public group whose iterations cause the form to be generated, and then click OK . For more information on adding groups, see Adding a Group . Deleting a Form Forms Mapper View To delete a form: Click the form to delete. Click the Delete Form button . The Deletion Confirmation dialog box appears. You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Form , and then click Delete Form . • Right-click the form you wish to delete, and then click Delete Form . • Press CTRL+D. Click Yes to confirm the deletion, or No to cancel. Renaming a Form Forms Mapper View To rename a form: Click the form to rename. On the Edit menu, point to Form , and then click Rename Form . The Rename Selected Form dialog box appears. You can also: Right-click the form, and then click Rename Form . Enter the new name for the form, and then click OK . Duplicating a Form Forms Mapper View When you duplicate a form, you also duplicate all its sub-forms. To duplicate a form: Click the form to duplicate. On the Edit menu, point to Form , and then click Duplicate Form . The Duplicate Form dialog box appears. You can also: Right-click the form, and then click Duplicate Form . Enter the name for the duplicate form. Click OK . Importing a Form Forms Mapper View You can import forms from other ManuScripts. To import a form from another ManuScript: On the File menu, click Import . The Import Wizard dialog box appears. Click Catalog . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. Click the ManuScript from which to import, and then click Load . In the What to import list, click Import Forms , and then click Next . The forms in the selected ManuScript are displayed in the Select the item(s) to import list. Click the forms to import. You can multi-select using CTRL+click or SHIFT+click . Click Next . The elements to be imported are listed. Any items selected in the previous display that already exist in the current ManuScript are not selected for importing. You can clear any element you do not wish to import by clearing the check box. You can force the importation of an element you already have in the current ManuScript by selecting the appropriate check box or by clicking the Mark all items button . Click Finish . All selected items are imported into the ManuScript. Adding Multiple Documents Forms Mapper View In Forms Mapper, you can add multiple documents to the Forms list simultaneously. Each document added becomes its own form. Forms Mapper automatically names each form with the original document's title, removing any spaces in the original name. To add multiple forms: On the Edit menu, point to Form , and then click Add Multiple Documents . The Select Files to Insert dialog box appears. Browse to and then click the documents to add. You can multi-select using CTRL+click or SHIFT+click . Click Open . The Select Primary Group dialog box appears. Click the group to be associated with each of the documents being added. You can select the Apply selection to all check box to use the same group for all selected documents. Click OK . Setting Print Conditions Forms Mapper View You can define a ManuScript field that determines whether a given form or sub-form prints. To define a form or sub-form print condition: Click the form or sub-form. To the right of the Use this form when text box, click the Ellipsis button . The Reference Select dialog box appears. Click an appropriate boolean field, and then click OK . Adding Watermarks and Stamps Forms Mapper View When creating forms in Forms Mapper, you can add watermarks and stamps to form pages. While watermarks appear beneath the printed text on a page to signify the authenticity of a document, stamps appear on top of the printed text to serve as an official stamp of approval, validity, or certification. Watermarks and stamps are only supported in .pdf output files. To add a watermark or stamp: On the Edit menu, point to Form , then click Form Properties . The Form Properties dialog box appears. Click the Watermark/Stamp tab. Click the Add button . In the Item column, click Watermark or Stamp . In the Type column, click Text or Image . In the Position column, click the position of the watermark or stamp. If Custom is selected: a. In the X column, enter the X coordinate for the horizontal placement of the watermark or stamp. The coordinate X = 0 is the upper left hand corner of the page. b. In the Y column, enter the Y coordinate for the vertical placement of the watermark or stamp. The coordinate Y = 0 is the upper left hand corner of the page. In the Content column, click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference, or specify a value. The value must be an absolute path to the Forms Directory. In the Pages column, enter the pages on which to place watermarks or stamps. Enter an asterisk (*) to print on all pages or enter a range of pages (0-5). Page count begins with 0 instead of 1. In the Orientation column, specify the desired orientation. If the Type is Text: In the Font column, click the desired font style. In the Size column, click the desired font size. In the Color column, click the desired font color. Setting Form Properties Forms Mapper View After you add a form in Forms Mapper, you can change the way it looks and when it is generated by modifying the form properties. To open the Form Properties dialog box: Click the appropriate form. On the Edit menu, point to Form , and then click Form Properties . You can also: Right-click the form, and then click Form Properties . The Form Properties dialog box contains two tabs: Standard Properties Watermark/Stamp Standard Properties Tab The Standard Properties tab includes the following settings. Property Description Caption Enter text for the caption of this form. Caption Override Click the Ellipsis button to select a field. The value of this field will override the Caption property at runtime. Category Enter the category for the form. This category can be useful when sorting forms. Print Default Indicates whether the default print status for the form should be mandatory, selected, unselected, or conditional. Selected — Causes the form to default to selected in the Print Job Preview screen. UnSelected — Causes the form to default to unselected in the Print Job Preview screen. Mandatory — Causes the form to be selected and uneditable in the Print Job Preview screen. A user cannot clear the form from printing. Conditional — Determines whether the form is selected, unselected, or mandatory according to a field in the ManuScript defined by the Default Condition . Bin # Specifies the paper bin to which you wish your printer to print the forms. Duplex and non-duplex printers for each bin are specified in EXAMPLE Form Print. Duplex Print Specifies whether to send the form to the printer set up for duplex printing or the printer set up for single-sided printing. Print Once Specifies whether to print the form every time or only once. Default Condition Click the Ellipsis button to select a field that determines the print status of the form. The field must contain a string value of Selected , UnSelected , or Mandatory . This option is available only when the Print Default is set to Conditional. Form List Click the Ellipsis button to select a field that defines an optional second list of forms to generate. Page Set Specifies the PageSet containing the page used to define the view that appears when a user edits a form from the Print Job Preview screen. Click Associate Page View to open the Associate Page View dialog box to select the PageSet and Page. Click Clear Page View to return to the default PageSet. Page Specifies the page used to define the view that appears when a user edits a form from the Print Job Preview screen. Click Associate Page View to open the Associate Page View dialog box to select the PageSet and Page. Click Clear Page View to return to the default PageSet. Watermark/Stamp Tab Watermarks and stamps are only supported in .pdf output files. The Watermark/Stamp tab contains the following properties. Property Description Add Adds a watermark or stamp. Remove Removes a watermark or stamp from the list. Item Specifies the item to add. Options are watermark or stamp. Type Specifies the type of watermark or stamp. Options are Text or Image. Position Specifies the position for the watermark or stamp. X Enters the numerical value for the X coordinate. This option is available only if the position is Custom. The X = 0 coordinate is at the upper left hand corner of the screen. Y Enters the numerical value for the Y coordinate. This option is available only if the position is Custom. The Y = 0 coordinate is at the upper left hand corner of the screen. Content Enters the path to the content of the watermark or stamp. Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference, or specify a value. The path must be an absolute path to the Forms Directory. Pages Specifies the pages on which to place watermarks and stamps. Enter an asterisk (*) to print on all pages, or enter a range of pages (0-5). Page count begins with 0 instead of 1. Orientation Specifies the orientation of the watermark or stamp. This option is available only when the Type is Text. Font Specifies the font. This option is available only if the Type is Text. Size Specifies the font size. This option is available only if the Type is Text. Color Specifies the font color. This option is available only if the Type is Text.

Sub-forms Sub-forms Save Page to .PDF A sub-form is a document or portion of a document that becomes part of a form. Sub-forms are either .doc (usually created in Microsoft Word) or .pdf documents that contain merge fields to which you can map specific ManuScript fields. A form can use one .pdf sub-form or one or more .doc sub-forms in its printing process. You can use sub-forms in Forms Mapper if you have purchased a license for EXAMPLE Forms™. Adding a Sub-form Forms Mapper View To add a sub-form: In the Forms list, click the form you wish to contain the sub-form. Click the Add Sub-Form button . The Select Files to Insert dialog box appears. You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Sub-Form , and then click Add Sub-Form(s) . • Right-click the appropriate form, and then click Add Sub-Form(s) . • Press CTRL+ALT+A. Browse to the appropriate folder, if necessary. Click the appropriate documents, and then click Open . .Doc documents appear in the preview as they were saved. You can edit source documents in Forms Mapper if they are .doc documents, but you cannot edit source documents in Forms Mapper if they are .pdf documents. Any merge fields in the document will be automatically listed in the Merge Fields grid, but only the merge field names will be listed. You must set the other merge field properties. Deleting a Sub-form Forms Mapper View You can delete a sub-form in Forms Mapper. To delete a sub-form: Click the sub-form to delete. Click the Delete Sub-Form button . The Deletion Confirmation dialog box appears. You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Sub-Form , and then click Delete Sub-Form . • Right-click the sub-form, and then click Delete Sub-Form . • Press CTRL+ALT+D. Click Yes to confirm the deletion, or click No to cancel. Resetting Sub-form Paths Forms Mapper View If a sub-form path is blank or points to the wrong location, you can reset the path to that sub-form. To reset a sub-form path: Right-click the sub-form you wish to modify. Click Reset Sub-Form Path . The Reset Sub-Form Path dialog box appears. You can also: On the Edit menu, point to Sub-Form , and then click Reset Sub-Form Path . Enter the relative path to the sub-form, and then click OK . Setting Print Conditions Forms Mapper View You can define a ManuScript field that determines whether a given form or sub-form prints. To define a form or sub-form print condition: Click the form or sub-form. To the right of the Use this form when text box, click the Ellipsis button . The Reference Select dialog box appears. Click an appropriate boolean field, and then click OK . Editing a .Doc Sub-form Forms Mapper View You can open and edit .doc sub-forms from Forms Mapper. To edit a .doc sub-form: Click the sub-form. On the Edit menu, point to Sub-Form and then click Edit Sub-Form . The document opens in Microsoft Word. You can also: Right-click the sub-form, and then click Edit Sub-Form . Edit and then save the document. You cannot edit .pdf sub-forms from Forms Mapper.

Groups Groups Save Page to .PDF You can define the groups through which the program iterates for a form or sub-form. If a form contains more than one sub-form, the groups listed for each-sub-form will control those sub-forms. You can add, delete, or move groups within the list. The properties for groups appear in the Groups grid in the upper-right corner of Forms Mapper. Adding a Group Forms Mapper View When you add a form, you must specify the main group associated with that form. This group is the public group whose iterations cause the form to be generated. In general, when specifying the main group associated with a form, start with the data group. You may also add additional groups to a form or a sub-form in order to get additional data not available in the initial group. Unless the main group executes the fields in the additional groups, the values of those fields will remain static through subsequent iterations. In general, add additional groups at the form level. For forms with multiple iterative sub-forms, however, you may need to add groups at the sub-form level. To ensure forms function properly, add any additional groups in hierarchical order. For .pdf source documents, you should only add groups at the form level. For .doc source documents, you can add groups to the form or sub-form level. In some cases, iterative fields may need to be associated with groups on both the form and sub-form levels. To add additional groups: In the Forms list, click the form or sub-form. Above the Group grid, click the Add Group button . The Select Group dialog box appears. You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Group , and then click Add Group . • Press CTRL+G. Click the appropriate group, and then click OK . In general, when selecting the main group for a form, select the data group. Only public groups appear in the dialog box. Continue adding groups in hierarchal order until you have included the groups containing all the merge fields in the form or sub-form Merge Field grid. Deleting a Group Forms Mapper View You can delete the additional groups associated with a form or sub-form. To delete a group: Above the Group grid, click the group to be removed. In the Group toolbar, click the Delete Group button . You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Groups , and then click Delete Group . • Press CTRL+L. Moving a Group Forms Mapper View You can change the order of groups in the Group grid. To move a group up or down in the Group grid: In the Group grid, click the group to be moved. Click the Move Up button or the Move Down button to move the group in the list. You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Group , and then click Move Group Up or Move Group Down . • Press CTRL+ALT+U or CTRL+ALT+W. Setting Group Properties Forms Mapper View After adding a form and specifying the groups to use on the form, you can define properties for those groups in the Group grid on the right side of the Forms Mapper interface. The Group grid contains the following group properties. Option Description/Action Group Indicates the selected group. Change by clicking the G button to open the Select New Group dialog box. # of Iterations Specifies the number of iterations to print on a page. If there are more iterations than will fit on a page, an additional page prints. Start Iteration Specifies the starting iteration for a form. If there are more iterations than will fit on a page, an additional page prints. You do not need to specify which iteration begins on the additional page. The program does this automatically. End Iteration Specifies the ending iteration for a specific form. If you enter an asterisk, the form includes all iterations. Include Deleted Indicates whether to include records marked for deletion on the form. Order By Indicates the field used to order the iterations on the form. Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field. Direction Indicates the sort order: Ascending or Descending. This option is available only if you select an Order By field. Restriction Specifies a boolean field that defines which iterations the form should include. Click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a boolean field.

Merge Fields Merge Fields Save Page to .PDF Merge fields associate fields in a ManuScript with specific locations in a printable form. When the form is printed, data items stored in the associated fields are placed in the merge fields and printed within the document. Merge fields can contain text or images. If you already have a .doc or .pdf source document created, you define the merge fields in the document using the Merge Field grid. When mapping merge fields to a source document, you define the mapped fields at the form level rather than the sub-form level. When you load a sub-form, any merge fields in the .doc or .pdf document appear in the Merge Field grid. Mapping a Merge Field Forms Mapper View After loading a sub-form or adding a merge field to a sub-form, you must map each field to a corresponding ManuScript field. To map a merge field: Click the form. In the Merge Field grid, click the merge field. Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Mapped Field text box. The Reference Select dialog box appears. Click the appropriate field, and then click OK . At runtime, public fields mapped to a merge field will pull data from the session document while private fields will calculate their value. Merge fields should be private fields. Because public fields are taken from the session data, they do not run the field rules assigned to them when the form is rendered. This helps improve performance, but it relies on information in the session data being accurate and ready to be printed. If desired, you can set public fields to recalculate at runtime. For information on setting this configuration, see the noRecalc parameter on the ManuScript.printFormsRq in the EXAMPLE Server® Object Reference or see Setting the Always Execute Property . Adding a Merge Field Forms Mapper View You can add a new merge field to the list of merge fields in the Merge Field grid. To add a merge field: On the Merge Field grid toolbar, click the Add Merge Field button . The Reference Select dialog box appears. You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Merge Field , and then click Add Merge Field . • Press CTRL+M. Click the appropriate field, and then click OK . The Merge Field grid pre-fills the Merge Name and the Format if it is defined in the selected field. To update the sub-form preview after adding or changing a merge field, click Refresh Merge Fields . Adding a merge field to the Merge Field grid adds the merge field to the ManuScript. It does not add the merge field to the source document for the form. For the merge field to appear on the printed page, you must add the merge field to both the source document and the Merge Field grid. Deleting a Merge Field Forms Mapper View You can delete a merge field from a selected form. This does not remove the merge field from any source documents that use the field. To delete a merge field: Click the appropriate form. In the Merge Field grid, click the merge field. On the Merge Field grid toolbar, click the Remove Merge Field button . The Deletion Confirmation dialog box appears. You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Merge Field , and then click Remove Merge Field . • Press CTRL+R. Click Yes to confirm the deletion, or click No to cancel. Duplicating a Merge Field Forms Mapper View You can duplicate a merge field so that the same data will appear in more than one place in the document. To duplicate a merge field: Click the appropriate form. In the Merge Field grid, click the merge field. On the Merge Field grid toolbar, click the Duplicate Merge Field button . The Duplicate Merge Field dialog box appears. You can also: • On the Edit menu, point to Merge Field , and then click Duplicate Merge Field . • Press CTRL+ALT+C. Enter the new name for the merge field, and then click OK . If you do not change the name of the merge field, nothing happens after you click OK . Forms Mapper will not support two merge fields with the same name. In addition, to make the same merge field print in more than one place on a form, you must duplicate the merge field in both the Merge Field grid and the source document. Moving a Merge Field Forms Mapper View You can move a merge field up or down in the Merge Field grid. This has no effect on the processing of fields and is for display purposes only. To move a merge field: Click the appropriate form. In the Merge Field grid, click the merge field you wish to move. Click the Move Up button or the Move Down button . You can also: • Press CTRL+U or CTRL+W. • On the Edit menu, point to Merge Field , and then click Move Merge Field Up or Move Merge Field Down . Sorting the Merge Grid Forms Mapper View You can sort the merge grid by column heading. This has no effect on the processing of fields and is for display purposes only. To sort the merge grid: Click the appropriate form. In the merge grid, click the Sort button next to the column heading by which you wish to sort. Clicking the Sort button disables the Move Up and Move Down buttons. (Optional) Click the Reset button to return the merge list to its original state. Using Reserved-Word Mapping When mapping fields to a form or sub-form, you can use reserved-word mapping to map information about the current print job to a merge field. The system generates this information at runtime. When using these reserved words, you do not have to enter a Mapped Field in the Merge Field grid. Instead, you simply use the reserved word as the Merge Field name. Setting Merge Field Properties Forms Mapper View After adding a form or sub-form or adding additional merge fields to a form or sub-form in Forms Mapper, you may need to set some or all of the merge field properties. To update the sub-form preview after changing merge field properties, click Refresh Merge Fields . Properties for merge fields include the following. Property Description/Action Merge Field Name of the merge field used in the source form. Should be a private field. Read Only Indicates the field is not editable. If all fields on a form are read-only, the edit option will not appear for the form in EXAMPLE Express ® . Required Indicates the field is required. Ignore In Compare Ignores this field when comparing versions of a form to determine whether it is necessary to reprint. Mapped Field ManuScript field from which the data is taken. If the merge field name is preceded by an img: prefix, this field's value must be a physical path (absolute or relative to the Forms directory) to an image. The size of the printed image will be the physical size of the image specified in the ManuScript field. Index Iteration number of the data for this merge field. Condition Boolean field in the ManuScript that determines when this merge field appears. Format Format in which the data should be returned. The selected format is applied to all instances of the selected field on the form. Value Values specified for a Format Mask, Coded List, or Calculated Mask option. Worksheet Options for displaying the merge field in a rating worksheet. Click to open the Worksheet Options dialog box. Options for the Format property include the following. Property Description None Applies no formatting. Format Mask Apply formatting based on the format mask specified in the Value property. The formatting rules are the same as those for Formatting Embedded Fields . Use this option if the format mask is not set in the ManuScript. Format Mask From Field Applies formatting based on the format mask specified in the field listed in the Value property. Use this option if the format mask is already set for the selected field. Coded List Selects a value from the list provided in the Value property. If no match is found for the value, then return the unformatted value. You may specify your own codes. Coded List From Field Selects a value from the list of options on the field listed in the Value property. If no match is found for the value, then return the unformatted value. Calculated Mask Uses the format mask that is the value of the field listed in the Value property. This format mask depends on a calculation. The field in the Value property must return a format mask. Adding a Coded List To add a Coded List : In the Format column of the Merge Field grid, click Coded List. In the Value column of the Merge Field grid, click the Ellipsis button . The Edit Coded List dialog box appears. Click Add Code . The Add Coded Option dialog box appears. Enter an appropriate value, and then click OK . The Add Coded Field Display dialog box appears. Enter the code name that relates to the value, and then click OK . The new item displays in the code list. Repeat steps 3 - 5 as appropriate. Click OK . The item appears in the Value column of the Merge Field grid.

Print Jobs Print Jobs Save Page to .PDF A print job defines a set of forms and their print order. You give each print job a name, and then you identify the print job by that name in any print output request. Any number of print jobs can exist in a ManuScript, and a print job can reference any form in a ManuScript's form list. It can also specify the order in which the forms should be printed within the print job. In addition to a list of forms, a print job also generates a control file. The control file provides a list of the forms in a print job in their correct print order. This file allows outside printing services to print the forms correctly. When configuring print jobs, you can control various print job features: Document Type — Specifies the output type of the print job. Condition — Specifies the conditions that must be met for this print job to print. Forms — Configures which forms compose a print job. You create and define print jobs in the Manage Print Jobs dialog box. To open the Manage Print Jobs dialog: On the Edit menu, click Manage Print Jobs . Print Job Toolbar Buttons The Print Job toolbar contains the following buttons. Action Description Add Print Job (CTRL+A) Add a print job to the Print Jobs list. Delete Print Job (CTRL+D) Delete a print job from the Print Jobs list. Rename Print Job Open the Rename Print Job dialog box to rename the selected print job. View Print Job XML Open the XML Viewer dialog box to view or edit the print job XML. Adding a Print Job Forms Mapper View To print specific forms, you must set up a print job in the Manage Print Jobs dialog box in Forms Mapper. A print job may consist of one or more forms, and each form may consist of multiple sub-forms. To add a print job: In the Manage Print Jobs dialog box, click the Add Print Job button . The Add Print Job dialog box appears. You can also: Press CTRL+A. Enter the name of the print job, and then click OK . Adding a Print Job from an External ManuScript Forms Mapper View EXAMPLE Forms™ allows you to reference and include print jobs from other ManuScripts within a print job. This feature allows a print job to combine forms in the active ManuScript with a set of forms in another ManuScript. When including a print job in another print job, all forms from the referenced print job are placed into the output list at the precise spot of the reference in the XML. Print jobs can be recursive. This means external print jobs can contain other external print jobs. External print jobs can also be dynamic. You can define rules that allow the Forms Engine to determine the name of the ManuScript and print job to reference at runtime. To add a print job from an external ManuScript: On the appropriate line in the Used Jobs/Forms grid, click the Browse button . The ManuScript Catalog dialog box appears. Click the ManuScript with the print job, and then click Load . The Select Print Job dialog box appears. Click the appropriate print job, and then click OK . (Optional) On the appropriate row, to the right of the Print Job Condition column, click the Ellipsis button to select a boolean field to determine whether the external print job is printed. Deleting a Print Job Forms Mapper View You can delete a print job if you no longer need it. Deleting a print job does not affect the forms contained in that print job. To delete a print job: In the Print Jobs list, click the print job. Click the Delete Print Job button . The Deletion Confirmation dialog box appears. Click Yes to confirm the deletion, or click No to cancel. Adding a Form to a Print Job Forms Mapper View You must specify the forms to be printed in a print job. To add a form to a print job: In the Print Jobs list, click the print job. In the Available Forms list, click one or more forms in the list, and then click the Add button . You can multi-select using CTRL+click or SHIFT+click . (Optional) Adjust the order of the form by clicking or . To add all available forms, click the Add All button . Removing a Form from a Print Job Forms Mapper View If you have a form in a print job that you do not wish to print, you can delete it from the print job. To delete a form from a print job: In the Print Jobs list, click the print job. In the Used Jobs/Forms list, click the form to be removed. Click the Remove button . You can multi-select using CTRL+click or SHIFT+click . To remove all forms, click the Remove All button . Moving a Form in a Print Job Forms Mapper View You can move a form up or down in the list so the forms print in a different sequence. To change the order in which forms are printed in a print job: In the Print Jobs list, click the print job. In the Used Jobs/Forms list, click the form to be moved. Click the Move Up button or the Move Down button . Setting the Document Type Forms Mapper View You can specify the type of output your print job generates. Options are: PDF MSWord RTF HTML MIXED By using the MIXED docType, users can set different output formats for each form in a print job. When this docType is enabled, the forms generator will determine the output format of each form by the output format of the first sub-form. For example, if the first sub-form in a given form is an HTML file, then all sub-forms of that form will be generated as an HTML file. Before enabling the MIXED docType, you should be aware of the following: Each form in a print job must contain either all PDF sub-forms or all non-PDF sub-forms, regardless of the docType of the print job. For example, you can create a form with a word document sub-form, 2 .rtf sub-forms, and 4 HTML sub-forms. However, you can not create a form that contains 2 PDF sub-forms, one MSWord sub-form, and 3 HTML sub-forms. If one subform in a given form is a PDF, all sub-forms in the form must be PDFs. When using the MIXED docType, the value of the onePDF attribute cannot be set to 1 because it is impossible to combine two documents of different types into one. The value of the onePDF attribute must be set to either 0 or 2 . MSWord (.doc) forms generated through the EXAMPLE Forms™ Engine are actually generated using TXTextControlWords.exe rather than Microsoft Word. Because of this distinction, Duck Creek Technologies, Inc. strongly recommends that users preview the format of .doc source documents in TXTextControlWords.exe before generating forms with those source documents to ensure that they generate appropriately. For more information about previewing .doc source documents in TXTextControlWords.exe, see Previewing in TXTextControlWords.exe in the EXAMPLE Forms™ Reference Guide . To set the document type (for all document types except MIXED): In the Print Jobs list, click the print job. In the Document Type list, click the desired document type. The EXAMPLE Forms™ engine can generate any document type from .doc source forms, but it can only generate the .pdf document type from .pdf source forms. Specifying MSWord, RTF, or HTML as the output type for .pdf source documents will not generate MSWord, RTF, or HTML documents. Instead, all .pdf source documents will be generated as .pdf files. To set the document type to MIXED: Open a text editor. Locate the print job in the ManuScript. Change the docType attribute to MIXED. <printJob name="Both" docType="MIXED">
  <printDoc name="Summary" />
  <printDoc name="Detail" />
</printJob> Setting the Print Condition Forms Mapper View You can specify a condition under which a print job executes. To set a print condition: In the Manage Print Jobs dialog box, click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Condition text box. The Reference Select dialog box appears. Click the appropriate boolean field, and then click OK . Editing the Control File Field List You can use a control file to define the order and location of forms that are to be printed. The control file provides information to a print service that aids in printing the forms. You can only edit the control file field list if you have purchased the EXAMPLE Forms™ license. You can modify the control file to identify a block of control information to be written into the control file. For example, you can specify how many copies of a form to print, or to which printer to send them. You can also identify a list of fields to write out. This passes other information to the application that is responsible for processing the forms. If you are attached to a database, the values for username, email, and FullName are added to the control file from the database. To edit the control file field list: On the Edit menu, click Edit Control File Field List . The Control Block dialog box opens. Click Add. The Reference Select dialog box opens. Navigate to the field to include in the control block, and then click OK . Repeat steps 2 and 3 for each field to include. In the Control Block dialog box, click OK . For each field in the list, the value of the field will be added to the control file (order.xml). The following XML will be added to the control file for the print job: <ControlBlock>
  <[fieldname] value=""/>
  <[fieldName] value=""/>
</ControlBlock> So if the fields added to the control block were Policy.PolicyNumber and Client.FullName, the control block could look like this: <ControlBlock>
  <Policy.PolicyNumber value="P123-897"/>
  <Client.FullName value="James Doe"/>
</ControlBlock>

Viewing Forms Inheritance Viewing Forms Inheritance Save Page to .PDF Forms Mapper View You can view the ManuScript inheritance chain for forms in the current ManuScript in the Inheritance dialog box. The Inheritance (Forms) dialog box displays the entire ManuScript inheritance chain for the current ManuScript's forms. From this dialog box, you can open any ManuScript in the inheritance chain by selecting the desired ManuScript and clicking Go to . You can access the Inheritance (Forms) dialog box from Forms Mapper in two ways: On the View menu, point to Inheritance , and then click Forms . On the shortcut menu for Forms Mapper, click View Inheritance (Forms) .

Validator Validator Save Page to .PDF The Validate pane includes the controls for and results of ManuScript validation. To open the Validate pane: Click the Validate button . You can also: On the Tools menu, click Validate . Validations The Validator may return a warning or an error if it encounters any of the following: Definitions A field has an option list and there is a value in the value tab. A value is used twice within an option list for a field. The default value is different than the default value set in Options. A condition has a duplicate value in the pick list. The data type specified is not valid for the option values used. A (defined) option is empty. A notify option is empty. Miscellaneous rules have been defined but are empty. (Defined) rules are empty. Values A primary value is not selected in the properties of a field. A select value type has an invalid value or reference. Invalid operators exist in any string calculation. A public field has a set minimum. Comparisons of is , isnot or contains are followed by a value type other than string. Data Types A date method has invalid arguments. A substring has no value or reference for base string, start position, or length. The data type used for the session is not valid. A single condition rule is not defined as boolean. A conditional statement has mismatched data types. Paths No path is specified for a field. A path is used by multiple fields in the ManuScript. Coded items in a path are invalid. A group path is not valid for the Data Schema. Group A group level is invalid for a table or a table field does not have access to a group. A group is not mapped to a valid object. Pages A field is used more than once in a PageSet. Topics of iteration links have the same name as a PageSet. PageSet names contain spaces. Iterations Iterator reference groups have an invalid reference field (if not a count). An iterator reference field does not exist. The conditions of an iterating field are defined outside of that field's group. Calculation rules exist on an iterating field with the new Subtotal check box selected. An invalid context of reference groups is used to restrict iterations for an iterator field type. Forms A print job name uses invalid characters. A merge field or mapped file is missing. Tables A lookup references an invalid table. A key of a table is unused or has no value or reference specified. A table does not have at least one key, either row or column. Row data exceeds the number of keys defined for a table. Indexes in a conditional table are not sequential. A table has been overridden in an inherited ManuScript, but it has then been reverted to match the base ManuScript. References A message referenced by a field does not exist in the message block. An external name points to a missing ManuScript or field. A field is used more than once in the Database Mapping tool. A database mapping reference does not exist. For Request value types, an incorrect Request Command was entered or a Response Attribute does not exist. Other Fields or tables may be unused in the ManuScript. These are called orphans. Invalid characters exist, such as a group name or ID which begins with a number. A field has been overridden in an inherited ManuScript, but it has then been reverted to match the base ManuScript. Setting Validator Options To set the validation level select or clear the appropriate check boxes. Item Action Validate against ManuScript Schema Warns if the ManuScript is not a valid ManuScript file. A ManuScript must pass this validation for EXAMPLE Server ® to recognize it. Click the Browse button to select an appropriate schema. Validate paths against Data Schema Warns if paths in the current ManuScript fail to match those in the schema. Click the Browse button to select an appropriate schema. Check for errors only Warns if any errors are encountered. Selecting this check box disables all other validator options. Check for orphaned fields/tables/messages Warns if any orphans are detected. Note that these items may be used by external ManuScripts. Check for captions on all public fields Warns if any public field has no Caption. All public fields must have annotations Warns if any public field has no annotation. Check for references to missing fields Checks for references to fields assumed to be local that do not exist in the local ManuScript. Warn if field has no rules or definition Warns if a field has an incomplete definition. Public fields must have maximum length defined Warns if a public field has no Maximum Length defined. Check for minimums on all public fields Warns if a public field has no Minimum defined. Flag overridden fields/tables that are the same as the base Warns if a field has been overridden in an inherited ManuScript but was then changed to match the base ManuScript. If a ManuScript has many field reference problems, selecting Validate against ManuScript Schema may cause the validation process to fail. In a ManuScript with many reference problems, clear the Validate against ManuScript Schema check box before validating. Validating a ManuScript To validate a ManuScript: In the Validate pane, click Validate ManuScript . The ManuScript validates completely and the Validator window displays the results. Review the Status and description of any errors or warnings. Double-click an item in the list to display it in Fields view. (Optional) Select the Ignore check box to skip a given warning during future validations. This check box is only available for warnings. Errors cannot be ignored.

Build Schema and Data File Wizard Build Schema and Data File Wizard Save Page to .PDF Build Schema and Data File builds an XML schema or data file for the current ManuScript. The schema defines the elements that appear within the ManuScript and the attributes that can be associated with elements. It also defines the structure of the ManuScript which includes: Elements that are child elements of others The sequence in which the child elements can appear The number of child elements Whether an element is empty or can include text Default values for attributes The data file is an XML representation of the data stored in the current ManuScript. You can build a schema file, a data file, or both for the current ManuScript. Building a Schema File To build a schema file: On the Tools menu, click Build Schema . The Build Schema and Data File dialog box appears. Select the Build a schema to file listed below check box. (Optional) Select the Append to an existing schema check box. (Optional) Select the Generate XSD Schemas check box. Default is XDR schema. Enter the path and file name in the file name text box, or click the Ellipsis button to browse to an appropriate file. Click Save . Building a Data File To build a data file: On the Tools menu, click Build Schema . The Build Schema and Data File dialog box appears. Select the Build EXAMPLE data file listed below check box. In the Data File text box, enter a path and file name, or click the Ellipses button to browse to an appropriate file. Click the ManuScripts to use to build the schema and then click Next . You can add ManuScripts from the Catalog and can change the order of ManuScripts. The Group Selection page appears. Do one of the following: Select the Publish group according to Manuscript settings check box,and then click Next. Select the Publish only selected groups (override ManuScript settings) check box, and then click Next . In the Data Mart Attributes section, assign the shredding attributes for the Session, Messages, Message and Properties. Click Set/Edit to enter the following attributes: Primary Key Column Related Table Parent Foreign Key Table Name The Private Fields page appears. (Optional) Select the Add private fields as elements in schema check box, and then click Next . Select the Add maximum length s to string nodes check box. After you have named the files and locations for those files, you can change the settings, if desired. Publish groups according to ManuScript settings — Select to use ManuScript settings. If selected, tabs appear for Session, Properties, Messages, and Message settings. Session — Specifies the parameters for shredding the session data to the database. Messages — Specifies the shredding parameters to shred the messages data to the database. Message — Specifies the shredding parameters to shred the message data to the database. Properties — Specifies the shredding parameters to shred the ManuScript properties data to the database. Add Private Fields — Select to include the private fields in the output. The default is to include the private fields. Add Max Lengths — Select to add maximum lengths to the string nodes. Click OK . The files are generated and stored where specified. Configuring Foreign Key Reduction As of EXAMPLE Platform ® 4.2.0, EXAMPLE Server ® allows you to configure the shredding process to use a modified storage mechanism for foreign keys. Prior to EXAMPLE Platform ® 4.2.0, each time a common Xpath expression was added to multiple places in the group hierarchy in a ManuScript, a new foreign key was added to the corresponding table in the shredded database. As of EXAMPLE Platform ® 4.2.0, multiple foreign keys in a single table can be consolidated into two columns: One containing the unique value found in the primary key of the parent table One representing the name of the parent table This action will affect the structure of your operational database. To configure Foreign Key Reduction: On the Tools menu, click Build Schema . The Build Schema and Data File dialog box appears. Select the Build a schema to file listed below check box. Select the Append to an existing schema check box. In the Schema File text box, browse to the location of the appropriate file, and then select it. Click the Manage Foreign Key Reduction button on the right side of the Build Schema and Data File dialog box. The Manage Foreign Key Reduction dialog box appears. Select the check box for the name of each table where foreign key reduction should be used. Click OK to close the Manage Foreign Key Reduction dialog. Click OK to save the updated schema and close the Build Schema and Data File dialog.

Inserting a Field into an Annotation or Message Add fields to the text of annotations or messages to display the value of the field or the caption of the field at execution. To insert a field in an annotation or message: In the Annotation or Message dialog box, click the Insert Field button . The Reference Select dialog box appears. Inserting a Formatted Field into an Annotation or Message To insert a formatted field in an annotation or message: On the Tools menu, click Message Viewer . The Message Viewer dialog box appears. In the Annotation or Message dialog box, click the Insert Formatted Field button . The Insert Format Field dialog box appears. Click Current field value (~$self~) or Selected Field . If you click Selected Field , enter the field name. Enter a field name or click the Ellipsis button to use the Reference Select dialog box to select a field reference. Click the type of formatting in the Format Specification . The options are: No formatting Caption of field Numeric format Date format Custom format If you click Numeric format , Date format , or Custom format , click the format type in the associated list. Click OK . Formatting Embedded Fields You can embed fields into message text. A message can contain any number of embedded fields. The mark-up for the text uses the tilde (~) character to indicate the name of the field to be replaced at runtime with its value. You can also use an optional formatting string to control the presentation of the data within the message. There are three types of format syntax: numeric, date, and character. The first character of the format indicates the type of formatting. Numeric format — is indicated by a first character of # or 0. Date formatting — is indicated by a first character of d, m or y. Character formatting — is indicated by any other first character. Numeric Formatting Options Numeric formatting options for embedded fields include the following. Character Description 0 or # If the value being formatted has a digit in the position where 0 or # appears in the format string, then that digit is copied to the output string. Otherwise, a 0 (zero) is stored in that position in the output string. ? If the value being formatted has a digit in the position where the ? appears in the format string, then that digit is copied to the output string. Otherwise, nothing is stored in that position in the output string. . The first PERIOD character in the format string determines the location of the decimal separator in the formatted value. Any additional PERIOD characters are ignored. , If the format string contains one or more COMMA characters, the output has thousand separators inserted between each group of three digits to the left of the decimal point. The placement and number of COMMA characters in the format string does not affect the output, except to indicate that thousand separators are used. E+ If any of the strings E+, E-, e+, or e- are contained in the format string, the number is formatted using scientific notation. A group of up to four 0 characters can immediately follow the E+, E-, e+, or e- to determine the minimum number of digits in the exponent. The E+ and e+ formats cause a plus sign to be output for positive exponents and a minus sign to be output for negative exponents. The E- and e- formats output a sign character only for negative exponents. xx/"xx" Characters enclosed in single or double quotes are output as-is, and do not affect formatting. ; The semicolon (;) character separates sections for positive, negative, and zero numbers in the format string. Other numeric format guidelines include the following: The locations of the leftmost 0 before the decimal point in the format string and the right-most 0 after the decimal point in the format string determine the required range of digits that are present in the output string. The number being formatted is rounded to as many decimal places as there are digit placeholders (0 or #) to the right of the decimal point. If the format string contains no decimal point, the value being formatted is rounded to the nearest whole number. If the number being formatted has more digits to the left of the decimal separator than there are digit placeholders to the left of the PERIOD character in the format string, the extra digits are output before the first digit placeholder. To allow different formats for positive, negative, and zero values, the format string can contain between one and three sections separated by semicolons. Number of Sections Values Affected One section The format string applies to all values. Two sections The first section applies to positive values and zeros, and the second section applies to negative values. Three sections The first section applies to positive values, the second applies to negative values, and the third applies to zeros. If the section for negative values or the section for zero values is empty, that is if there is nothing between the semicolons that delimit the section, the format for positive values is used instead. If the section for positive values is empty, or if the entire format string is empty, the value is formatted using general floating-point formatting with 15 significant digits. General floating-point formatting is also used if the value has more than 18 digits to the left of the decimal point and the format string does not specify scientific notation. The following table shows some sample formats and the results produced when the formats are applied to different values. Format String Formatted Data 1234 -1234 0.5 0 0 1234 -1234 1 0 #.## or 0.00 1234.00 -1234.00 0.50 0.00 ?.?? 1234 -1234 .5 ?,??0.00 1,234.00 -1,234.00 0.50 0.00 ?,??0.00;(?,??0.00) 1,234.00 (1,234.00) 0.50 0.00 0.000E+00 1.234E+03 -1.234E+03 5.000E-01 0.000E+00 ?.???E-0 1.234E3 -1.234E3 5E-1 0E0 Character Formatting Options Character formatting begins with the at sign (@) character. The edit mask string immediately follows the at sign (@) character, and consists of three fields separated by semicolons: The first part of the mask is the mask itself. The second part is the character that determines whether the literal characters of the mask are matched to characters in the Value parameter or are inserted into the Value string. The third part of the mask is the character used to represent missing characters in the mask. The following table contains the special characters used in the first field of the mask. Character Meaning in mask ! If an exclamation point (!) character appears in the mask, optional characters are represented in the returned string as leading blanks. If an exclamation point (!) character is not present, optional characters are represented in the returned string as trailing blanks. > If a greater than (>) character appears in the mask, all characters that follow are in uppercase until the end of the mask or until a greater than (<) character is encountered. < If a less than (<) character appears in the mask, all characters that follow are in lower-case until the end of the mask or until a less than (>) character is encountered. <> If the less than and greater than (<>) characters appear together in a mask, no case checking is done and the data is formatted with the case present in the Value parameter. \ The character that follows a forward slash (\) character is a literal character. Use this character before any of the special mask characters used as a literal. L The L character requires an alphabetic character in this position. For the US, this is A-Z, a-z. A The A character requires an alphanumeric character in this position. For the US, this is A-Z, a-z, 0-9. a The a character permits an alphanumeric character in this position, but does not require it. C The C character requires an arbitrary character in this position. c The c character permits an arbitrary character in this position, but does not require it. 0 The zero (0) character requires a numeric character in this position. 9 The nine (9) character permits a numeric character in this position, but does not require it. # The pound (#) character permits a numeric character or a plus or minus sign in this position, but does not require it. : The colon (:) character is used to separate hours, minutes, and seconds in times. If the character that separates hours, minutes, and seconds is different in the regional settings of the Control Panel, that character is substituted in the returned string. / The forwardslash (/) character is used to separate months, days, and years in dates. If the character that separates months, days, and years is different in the regional settings of the Control Panel, that character is substituted in the returned string. ; The semicolon (;) character is used to separate the three fields of the mask. _ The underscore (_) character automatically inserts spaces into the returned string. Any character that does not appear in the preceding table can appear in the first part of the mask as a literal character. Literal characters are inserted automatically if the second field of the mask is 0, or matched to characters in the Value parameter if the second field is any other value. The special mask characters can also appear as literal characters if preceded by a backslash character (\). The second field of the mask is a single character that indicates whether literal characters from the mask are included in the Value parameter. For instance, the mask for a telephone number with area code could be:(000)_000-0000;0. The 0 in the second field indicates that the Value parameter should consist of the 10 digits of the phone number, rather than the 14 characters that make up the final formatted string. A 0 in the second field indicates that literals are inserted into the Value string. Any other character indicates that they should be matched to characters in the Value parameter. The character to indicate whether literals should be included can be changed by changing the MaskNoSave typed constant that is declared in the Mask unit. The third field of the mask is the character that appears in the returned string for blanks (characters that do not appear in Value). By default, this is the same as the character that stands for literal spaces. The two characters appear the same in the returned string. Adding a Context to an Annotation or Message To add a context to an annotation or message: In the Annotations or Message dialog box, highlight the text to either hide or show when the ManuScript is viewed under a certain context. Click the Set Context button to open the Context Details dialog box. The text appears in the Text box of the Context Details dialog. Click the appropriate contexts in the Show Only with this context or Hide when this context lists. You can click the Select All button to select all the contexts or the Clear All button to clear all the context selections. (Optional) Click the Ellipsis button to the right of Show Only when condition is valid , and then click the appropriate boolean field. Click OK . The color of the selected text changes. You can set context colors in the Preferences tab of the Options dialog box. Removing a Context from an Annotation or Message To remove a context from an annotation or message: In the Annotations or Message dialog box, highlight the text from which you wish to remove contexts. Click the Remove Context button . The color of the selected text changes and the context is removed. You can set context colors in the Preferences tab of the Options dialog box. Editing Messages You can use the Message Viewer to enter new messages. You can also edit existing messages in the Message Editor. To edit a message: Open the Message Viewer. Click the message in the list. The Field Message text box displays the message text. Make any changes to the message. You can change the text, flag, or category. Click Update or OK . If you click Update , the message changes are saved and the Message Viewer remains open. If you click OK , the message changes are saved and the Message Viewer closes. Adding an Existing Message to a Field To add an existing message to a field property: Click the field. Expand the appropriate property: Default , Maximum , or Minimum . Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Message property to open the Message Editor dialog box. Click Use Existing Message . The Message Editor expands to list the existing messages. Click the message to use, or enter a new message. (Optional) Edit the selected message. If you edit an existing message, the message text changes for all fields linked to that message. Click OK . Removing a Field from a Message To remove a message from a linked field: On the Tools menu, click Message Viewer . The Message Viewer dialog box appears. In the list of messages used in the ManuScript, click the message. Click Remove Field . The Select Field to Remove dialog box appears. Click the field to remove from the message. Click OK . The selected field is no longer linked to the message. Removing a Link to a Message for a Field To remove a link to a message for a field: Click the field. Expand the appropriate property. Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Message property to open the Message Editor dialog box. Click Unlink . The selected field is no longer linked to the message. Deleting a Message for All Linked Fields To delete a message for all linked fields: On the Tools menu, click Message Viewer . Click the message from the list of messages already used in the ManuScript. Click Delete Message . This action deletes the message for all fields previously linked to it. These fields no longer contain a message. Click OK .

Database Mapping Database Mapping Save Page to .PDF You use the Database Mapping tool to specify which fields in the ManuScript to store as fields in the database. The entire ManuScript is stored in the database, but the data is stored as one large group, which makes it difficult to access specific data items. By storing the data as fields, you may access the data with any standard database access tool. The fields you store may be required or optional fields in the ManuScript. Public fields in the Database Mapping are not executed at save time in EXAMPLE Express ® . Private fields in the Database Mapping must be executed at save time because the value of a private field must be executed to be determined. Because the Database Mapping contains private and public fields, the ManuScript can determine if rules are performed only at save time or if they are always run by the system. If the rules are to be run at save time, use a private field that references the fields or rules to be executed. If the XML data is to be extracted as is, use a public field in the Database Mapping. To open the Database Mapping tool: On the Tools menu, click Database Mapping . The Database Mapping dialog box appears. Mapping Required Fields There are 18 required fields already defined in the database. You must map a ManuScript field to each of these fields. You can also define and map to optional fields if you wish to store additional data. To map the required fields: In the Database Mapping dialog box, click the line in the Data Name list. Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Field text box to use the Reference Select dialog box to select the appropriate field. Repeat steps two and three for all of the required fields. Mapping Optional Fields In addition to the 18 required fields that you must map in your ManuScript, you can also map optional fields. To map optional fields: In the Database Mapping tool, click the first empty row in the Optional Fields grid. Enter an appropriate Data Name . Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Field text box to use the Reference Select dialog box to select the appropriate field. Repeat steps one through three to add each optional field. Inserting an Optional Field To insert an Optional Field: In the Database Mapping tool, click the row in the Optional Fields grid above which to insert the Field. Click Insert Item . The line you selected is moved down, creating room for another entry. Enter an appropriate Data Name . Click the Ellipsis button to the right of the Field text box and then use the Reference Select dialog box to select the appropriate field. Repeat steps one through four for each field to insert. Deleting an Optional Field To delete an Optional Field: In the Database Mapping tool, click the row in the Optional Fields grid to delete. Click Delete Item . Removing All Mapped Fields You can remove all the mapped fields with the Database Mapping tool. This removes the mapping for both the required and the optional Fields. To remove all mapped fields: In the Database Mapping tool, click Remove All . The Confirm dialog box appears. Click Yes to remove all the mappings or No to cancel the process.

Portfolio Mapping Wizard Portfolio Mapping Wizard Save Page to .PDF The Portfolio Mapping tool is an EXAMPLE Author ® wizard that allows users to create, modify, or delete mappings that link Portfolio ManuScripts to shared data fields within associated product ManuScripts. Using the Portfolio Mapping Wizard, Portfolio ManuScript authors can: Select the product ManuScripts that will be associated with the portfolio Link the Portfolio ManuScript "shared data" fields to selected fields within the individual product ManuScripts. To access the Portfolio Mapping Wizard: From EXAMPLE Author ® , in the Fields View, on the Tools menu, click Portfolio Mapping . The Portfolio Mapping Wizard opens. From this screen, users can create a new portfolio mapping or modify an existing one. Creating a New Portfolio Mapping The Portfolio Mapping tool enables users to create and define new portfolio mappings through a three-step process: Create a new mapping. Select the product ManuScripts that will be included within the portfolio. Link the Portfolio ManuScript shared-data fields with corresponding fields in the product ManuScripts. Step One: Create a Portfolio Mapping The first screen of the Portfolio Mapping Wizard allows users to create and name a new mapping, or to modify or delete an existing mapping. To create a new Portfolio ManuScript mapping: From EXAMPLE Author ® , with the Portfolio ManuScript open, in the Fields View, on the Tools menu, click Portfolio Mapping . The Portfolio Mapping Wizard opens. Select Create new mapping . Enter a name or description of the mapping in the Description box, and then click Next to move to the Select ManuScripts page and begin selecting the portfolio products. Step Two: Select ManuScripts The second screen of the Portfolio Mapping Wizard allows users to select the product ManuScripts to be included within the Portfolio. To select portfolio products: On the Select ManuScripts screen click the Add button . The Available Products selection screen appears. If desired, use the Products Filter to narrow the options in the Available Products list. In the Available Products list, click the appropriate product ManuScripts, and then click OK . The selected product ManuScripts are displayed. To add additional ManuScripts, click the Add button . To delete a ManuScript from the list, highlight the product ManuScript name, and then click the Delete button . When satisfied with the ManuScript selection, click Next to move to the Map Shared Data Fields page and begin mapping shared data fields. Step Three: Map Shared Data Fields The third screen of the Portfolio ManuScript allows users to define shared data for the portfolio. Each field from the sharedData group in the Portfolio ManuScript must be mapped to an appropriate field in each product ManuScript. In addition, each shared data field may be set to be read-only on policy and quote interview screens. To Map Shared Data fields: In the first Shared Data field row, click the check box in the Protected column to set the field as read-only on quote and policy interview screens. In the first product ManuScript column, click the Ellipsis button . The Field Selection dialog box appears. The Field Selection dialog box displays groups and fields for the associated product ManuScript. Select the appropriate ManuScript field or group, and then click OK . Repeat for each Shared Data field. Map the remaining Shared Data fields to the first product ManuScript, and then repeat steps 1- 4 for the remaining ManuScripts. Map all product ManuScripts to the Shared Data fields, and then click Finish . Modifying a Portfolio Mapping Portfolio mappings are modified from within the Portfolio Mapping Wizard. From there, users can add or delete associated product ManuScripts and change shared data field associations. Step One: Modify a Portfolio Mapping The first screen of the Portfolio Mapping Wizard allows users to create and name a new mapping, or to modify or delete an existing mapping. To modify a portfolio mapping: From EXAMPLE Author ® , in the Fields View, on the Tools menu, click Portfolio Mapping . The Portfolio Mapping Wizard opens. Click Modify existing mapping , and then select the name of the portfolio mapping to be modified. Click Next to move to the Select ManuScripts page and modify the list of selected portfolio products. Step Two: Select ManuScripts This page displays the currently selected products for the portfolio and allows products to be added or deleted. To add products to the selected ManuScripts list: On the Select ManuScripts screen, click the Add button . The Available Products selection screen appears. If desired, use the Products Filter to narrow the options in the Available Products list. In the Available Products list, click the appropriate product ManuScripts, and then click OK . The revised Selected ManuScripts list is displayed. To delete products from the Selected ManuScripts list: From the Selected ManuScripts list, select a product ManuScript to delete, and then click the Delete button . Repeat until all appropriate ManuScripts have been deleted. Click Next . The Map Shared Data Fields screen appears. Follow the steps below to modify shared data mapping or to exit the wizard without changing the shared data field mapping, and then click Finish . Step Three: Map Shared Data Fields This page displays the current shared data mappings for the portfolio. To change read-only status of shared data: To the right of the shared data field name, click the check box in the Protected columnto set the field as read-only on quote and policy interview screens, or clear the check box to remove the read-only status. Repeat for all fields to be changed. Follow the steps below to edit shared data field mappings, or click Finish to exit the wizard. To modify the shared data mapping: For the desired product ManuScript, in the Shared Data field row, click the empty text box in the Ellipsis column . The Field Selection dialog box appears. Select the appropriate ManuScript field or group, and then click OK . Repeat for all desired fields in the ManuScript, and then repeat steps 1-3 for any other ManuScripts to be updated. When all changes have been made, click Finish . Deleting a Portfolio Mapping The first screen of the Portfolio Mapping Wizard allows users to delete an existing mapping. To delete a portfolio mapping: From EXAMPLE Author ® , in the Fields View , on the Tools menu, click Portfolio Mapping . The Portfolio Mapping Wizard opens. Click Delete existing mapping , and then select the name of the portfolio mapping to be modified. Click Delete . In the Deletion Confirmation box, click Yes to delete the mapping or No to cancel the deletion.

Page Converter Page Converter Save Page to .PDF The Page Converter converts Version 1 pages to Version 2 pages. With the Page Converter, you can convert a single page in a ManuScript or all pages local to a ManuScript. The conversion process performed by the Page Converter tool is final. Version 2 pages cannot be converted back to Version 1. Back up ManuScripts before converting pages to Version 2. Only pages local to the current ManuScript are converted. During conversion, the Page Converter performs the following actions: Converts Version 1 page elements (such as layouts) to Version 2 page elements (such as sections). When converting pages, all layouts are converted to sections. Converts any layout with the Field Caption Display property defined as "First Iteration" to a section with a Field Caption Display property defined as "All". Because sections cannot contain iterations, the First Iteration option for the Field Caption Display property is not valid for sections. Applies all custom parameters defined in Version 1 pages to Version 2 pages. Any custom parameter defined in Version 1 pages with an equivalent advanced property in Version 2 pages is converted to that advanced property. Any custom parameter that does not have an equivalent advanced property in Version 2 pages is added to the Version 2 page as a Custom Property . Custom properties appear below all other properties. All custom properties are italicized. For a list of Version 1 custom parameters supported in Version 2, see Conversion Support for Custom Parameters . When a custom parameter from a version 1 page is added as a custom property to a version 2 page during conversion, you should evaluate whether any of the standard properties for version 2 pages already accomplish what is specified in the custom property. If the standard properties do not accomplish what is needed, you must customize your skin to accommodate the custom property. After page conversion, you can convert any desired sections to different elements (tables or grid widgets) and modify any desired properties. You can also remove any nested sections converted from a Version 1 page that are unnecessary in Version 2. To open the Page Converter: On the Tools menu, click Page Converter . Converting a Single Page Converting a single page from Version 1 to Version 2 requires you to: Convert the page using the Page Converter tool. Remove any unnecessary page elements (such as nested sections) carried over during conversion. Modify any desired page elements or properties that were altered upon conversion. When converting pages from Version 1 to Version 2, the system automatically converts all layouts to sections. If you wish some layouts to become tables or grid widgets, you must manually convert these sections to tables or grid widgets after page conversion. Converting the Page To convert the page using the Page Converter tool: On the Tools menu, click Page Converter . The Page Converter dialog box appears. Click the Convert a single page option. Click the PageSet containing the page you wish to convert, and then click the Page you wish to convert. Click Continue . A warning message appears. To continue with the conversion process, select the Yes, I understand check box, and then click Continue . A process dialog box appears, indicating the progress of the conversion. When complete, a success confirmation dialog box appears. In the success confirmation dialog, click OK . The page will appear as a Version 2 page in the Page Designer list. Click Save to save conversion changes. You must save changes before exiting EXAMPLE Author ® to retain conversion changes. Removing Unnecessary Page Elements To remove any unnecessary page elements (such as nested sections) carried over during conversion: In a section containing unnecessary nested sections, move fields and actions to the highest section in the hierarchy of nested sections. Delete all empty child sections. Modifying Page Elements and Properties To modify any desired page elements or properties that were altered during conversion: (Optional) Convert any desired sections to different elements (tables or grid widgets). (Optional) Modify any desired properties. Because sections do not support iterations, all layouts with a Field Caption Display property set to First Iteration are converted to sections with a Field Caption Display property set to All . To change this property, modify the property for the section, or convert the section to a table or grid widget and adjust the Field Caption Display property. Converting All Pages in a ManuScript The Page Converter only converts pages that are local to the current ManuScript. Converting all local pages in a ManuScript from Version 1 to Version 2 requires you to: Convert the pages using the Page Converter tool. Remove any unnecessary page elements (such as nested sections) carried over during conversion. Modify any desired page elements or properties that were altered upon conversion. When converting pages from Version 1 to Version 2, the system automatically converts all layouts to sections. If you wish some layouts to become tables or grid widgets, you must manually convert these sections to tables or grid widgets after page conversion. Converting Pages To convert the pages using the Page Converter tool: On the Tools menu, click Page Converter . The Page Converter dialog box appears. Click the Convert all pages defined in this ManuScript option, and then click Continue . A warning message appears. To continue with the conversion process, select the Yes, I understand check box, and then click Continue . A process dialog box appears, indicating the progress of the conversion. When complete, a success confirmation dialog box appears. In the success confirmation dialog, click OK . The pages will appear as Version 2 pages in the Page Designer list. Click Save to save conversion changes. You must save changes before exiting EXAMPLE Author ® to retain conversion changes. Removing Unnecessary Page Elements To remove any unnecessary page elements (such as nested sections) carried over during conversion: In a section containing unnecessary nested sections, move fields and actions to the highest section in the hierarchy of nested sections. Delete all empty child sections. Modifying Page Elements and Properties To modify any desired page elements or properties that were altered during conversion: (Optional) Convert any desired sections to different elements (tables or grid widgets). (Optional) Modify any desired properties. Because sections do not support iterations, all layouts with a Field Caption Display property set to First Iteration are converted to sections with a Field Caption Display property set to All . To change this property, modify the property for the section, or convert the section to a table or grid widget and adjust the Field Caption Display property. Conversion Support for Custom Parameters During conversion to Version 2 pages, the Page Converter supports all custom parameters defined in Version 1 pages by: Converting any custom parameters supported by Version 2 pages to the equivalent advanced properties Adding any custom parameters not supported by Version 2 pages (that is, any parameters that do not have equivalent advanced properties) as Custom Properties . Custom properties appear below all other properties. When a custom parameter from a version 1 page is added as a custom property to a version 2 page during conversion, you should evaluate whether any of the standard properties for version 2 pages already accomplish what is specified in the custom property. If the standard properties do not accomplish what is needed, you must customize your skin to accommodate the custom property. The following custom parameters in Version 1 pages are supported by the following equivalent advanced properties in Version 2 pages.
